# Sample-server code in the X module (`crm.so`)

`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/crm.so` carries the machine-independent and memory-drawing layers of the public X Window System sample server next to its CRIME code. This chapter gives each such routine of the 6.5.30 module its own entry: what it does, the public source file that holds the same routine, and whether it reaches CRIME hardware. None of the routines below touches the CRIME register windows: they draw into memory, and the CRIME routines of [xmodule.md](xmodule.md) call them as the generic path.

Public source: the X.Org server tree at tag `XORG-6_8_2` (`https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/xorg/xserver`, directories `mi`, `cfb` and `mfb`), which descends from the X11R6.6 sample server the IRIX server was built from. Paths in the entries are relative to that tree. The 16-bit and 32-bit `cfb` routines are the `cfb` source compiled for those pixel sizes, and the names with a `..` suffix are file-local routines whose suffix the module's symbol table adds.

Labels follow [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md): an entry is **Established** only where the whole routine was read against the public body; the rest are **Inferred**, matched to the public routine by its calls and shape rather than read end to end. Where an entry says it differs from the public body, the difference was read in the disassembly. Routes tried for the SGI-only code (the symbol-database reader, the line-table helpers and the `nfb` layer, which has no public counterpart): the public X.Org tree holds none either.

The 165 `nfb` routines have their own chapter, [nfb-layer.md](nfb-layer.md).

## Machine-independent layer (mi)

- `miFillArcSliceSetup` in `crm.so`: prepares the edge description a pie or chord slice needs before rows are filled. It normalises the two angles of the arc to the range 0 to 23040 (64ths of a degree), swapping them for a negative span, and starts the top and bottom row limits from half the arc height. In pie mode it classifies each edge as above or below the centre line, handles the axis-aligned angles as special cases that clamp the row ranges, sets flip flags when the slice wraps through the horizontal axis, and gets each edge's slope and starting x through the ellipse slope converter. In chord mode it computes the two end points in floating point (sine and cosine, exact for the quarter turns), derives one common edge from their difference with a ceiling on the offset, and fills the same record. 4436 bytes at offset 0x42f790. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/mifillarc.c`, where the disassembly shows two calls of the combined sine-cosine helper, two ceil calls, and the quarter-turn constant 5760 plus the half-turn constant for the quadrant tests, and two direct calls of the slope converter. Its calls: `miEllipseAngleToSlope`. It stores to the slice record only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miEllipseAngleToSlope` in `crm.so`: converts an arc angle into the slope of the tangent used to fill a pie slice. The four axis angles return exact unit directions (and the matching floating-point offsets when asked); any other angle takes the sine and cosine of the angle scaled by the width and height, makes both absolute, divides by the larger so the larger component is 32768, rounds with floor and restores the signs. 420 bytes at offset 0x4308f0. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/mifillarc.c`, where the disassembly has one call of the combined sine-cosine helper, two floor calls, and the quarter-turn constant 5760 plus the half-turn and three-quarter-turn constants for the axis angles. It writes the output arguments. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miZeroArcSetup` in `crm.so`: prepares the integer thin-arc stepper. From an arc's box and its two angles in 64ths of a degree it computes the first and last pixel of the arc, the decision variables and per-axis increments, and flags for a full ellipse or a 360-degree arc, using sine four times and cosine twice for the end slopes. It returns false for arcs the exact stepper cannot handle, so the caller falls back to the general routine. All results go to a caller-supplied info record, written with 130 word stores. 4968 bytes at offset 0x430aa0. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/mizerarc.c`, where the disassembly matches its quadrant tests with the turn constants for a quarter, a half, three quarters and a full circle, and an eighth-turn constant of 2880. It stores to the info record only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miImageText8` in `crm.so`: looks up the glyph records of a one-byte string in the context's font and, when any exist, calls the context's image-glyph method with them, 140 bytes at offset 0x437530; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/mipolytext.c`, the same steps as the body, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miSendExposures` in `crm.so`: builds and delivers the expose events for a region of a window. It allocates one event record per rectangle, fills each with the event type, the window id, the box position minus the supplied offset, the box size and a descending count so the last event carries zero, and delivers them to the window's owner. This build carries the multi-screen layout hook: for the root window it uses that screen's offset and substitutes the first window, and for a window on another screen it looks the matching window up by screen number through the screen-number lookup, shifting each event's position and window id before delivery. 1720 bytes at offset 0x438a10. Derived from the same-named function in `mi/miexpose.c` with the disassembly in view, which shows one resource lookup by id, one by screen number, three event-delivery calls, the event constants 12 and 0xc000, and a call of `XineramaCollectExpose` that the 6.8.2 text does not make here. Its calls: `DeliverEvents`, `LookupIDByType`, `PanoramiXFindIDByScrnum`. It writes event buffers. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miWindowExposures` in `crm.so`: generates and sends the exposure events when part of a window becomes visible. If the window has backing store it first asks the screen to restore the areas from it, which may return a different region than the one requested. It then fills in the window background over the exposed region through screen methods when the clients' event mask includes exposure, and sends the events by calling `miSendExposures` for the region and for any other exposed region passed in. 880 bytes at offset 0x4390d0. Checked against the same-named function in `mi/miexpose.c`, where the disassembly shows one direct call of the exposure sender, fifteen register-indirect calls through screen offsets such as 348 and 360. Its calls: `miSendExposures`. It writes region records. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miRegionInit` in `crm.so`: initialises a caller-supplied region record as empty or as one box, 268 bytes at offset 0x439440; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miregion.c`, the same steps as the body: with a box it copies it into the extents and clears the data pointer; otherwise it copies the shared empty box and, for a size above one, allocates a header and box block with `Xalloc` (size and a zero count) or else points at the shared empty block, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miRegionExtents` in `crm.so`: returns the address of the region's bounding box, 8 bytes at offset 0x439550; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miregion.c`: the body moves the argument into the return register, because the extents are the first member of the record. Established.
- `miRegionEmpty` in `crm.so`: frees the region's box block when one is allocated, makes the extents zero-sized from their top-left corner and points the data at the shared empty block, 100 bytes at offset 0x439560; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miregion.c`: the body frees the block only when the pointer at +0x8 and the block's first word are both set, stores the shared empty block's address at +0x8 and copies the halfwords at the start of the record over those four bytes further on. Established.
- `miRectIn` in `crm.so`: classifies how a rectangle lies against a region: entirely outside, entirely inside, or partly in. It first rejects an empty region or a rectangle that misses the region's extents, then answers inside or part for a one-box region by whether the box contains the rectangle, and otherwise walks the boxes in y then x order, tracking the next uncovered y and x of the rectangle and stopping once both an in-part and an out-part have been seen or the rectangle's bottom is reached. It returns 0 for out, 1 for in and 2 for part. 580 bytes at offset 0x4395d0. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/miregion.c`, where the disassembly has no calls, 27 `slt` compares, and a stack frame of 48 bytes holding the loop's spilled operands. It writes nothing. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miRegionCopy` in `crm.so`: copies a region's boxes and extents into another region, reallocating its storage as needed, 380 bytes at offset 0x439820; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miregion.c`: the body returns 1 at once when both arguments are the same record, copies the four extent halfwords, and, when the source block holds boxes, keeps the destination block if its capacity word at +0x0 is at least the source's count at +0x4 and otherwise frees it and allocates eight bytes plus eight for each box with the must-have-memory flag set around the call, then copies the boxes with the memory-copy routine and sets the count; with no boxes in the source it frees the destination's block and shares the source's data pointer. Established.
- `miRegionUninit` in `crm.so`: frees the region's box block when one is allocated and leaves the record itself, 72 bytes at offset 0x4399a0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miregion.c`: the body tests the pointer at +0x8 and the first word of its block, frees the block when both are set, and leaves the pointer unchanged. Established.
- `miSendGraphicsExpose` in `crm.so`: tells a client which parts of a copy could not be filled in, 352 bytes at offset 0x446ec0; the public sample-server source `mi/miexpose.c` holds the same routine, and the body read in full matches it. For a region with boxes it builds an array of 32-byte events on the stack, one per box, each of type 13 with the drawable, the box's x and y, its width and height, the number of events still to come, and the minor and major opcodes it was given, and sends the array in one call. For no region or an empty one it sends a single event of type 14 (no exposure) with the drawable and the two opcodes. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miComputeCompositeClip` in `crm.so`: recomputes a context's composite clip from the drawable's clip and the client clip. For a window it takes either the clip list or, when the context includes inferiors, the region not covered by children; when the context has no client clip it keeps that region itself, remembering whether it owns it; otherwise it intersects the window region with the client clip, translated by the clip origin, reusing one of the two regions when it is a private copy and creating a new one when neither is. For a pixmap it makes a box of the pixmap size and intersects it the same way. It then clears the changed-clip flag in the context's state word (mask -65). 716 bytes at offset 0x447020. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/migc.c`, with the disassembly showing one call to the covered-by-children routine and 13 register-indirect calls through screen offsets such as 376 and 356. Its calls: `NotClippedByChildren`. It writes the context's composite-clip fields. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miFillPolygon` in `crm.so`: is the entry point of the polygon fill method. It returns at once for no points, converts the point list to absolute coordinates in place (adding the drawable origin when the context translates, or accumulating deltas when the mode is previous-point), and then picks the convex scan converter when the shape argument says convex, and the general active-edge filler otherwise. 1988 bytes at offset 0x4472f0. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/mipoly.c`, and the disassembly shows exactly one call to each of the two fillers, loads and stores of 16-bit coordinates (86 halfword loads, 72 halfword stores) and prefetches (9 of them) in the conversion loops. Its calls: `miFillConvexPoly`, `miFillGeneralPoly`. It writes the caller's point list. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miFillGeneralPoly` in `crm.so`: fills an arbitrary polygon by the active-edge method. It allocates one edge entry per vertex, builds the edge table and the active-edge list with `miCreateETandAET`, then walks the rows from the top. For the even-odd rule it pairs the active edges in sorted order and fills between each pair; for winding-number rule it keeps a second winding list and fills while the winding count is non-zero. Spans accumulate in a buffer of 200 entries and are flushed through the context's span method at offset 0 of its operation table, three flush sites. It returns success at once for fewer than three points. 2504 bytes at offset 0x447ac0. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/mipolygen.c`, where the disassembly shows `miCreateETandAET` once, `Xfree` once, the constant 200 for the buffer and three indirect flush calls. It writes only the edge records and span buffer. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miPolyRectangle` in `crm.so`: draws the outlines of rectangles. For solid lines with miter joins and nonzero width it builds each outline as at most four filled rectangles (top, bottom and two sides, merged when the width swallows the interior) with their edges clamped to the signed 16-bit and unsigned 16-bit limits, and sends them to the context's fill-rectangle method at offset 44 of its operation table; a rectangle with zero width and height becomes a two-point line through the polyline method at offset 24. Otherwise it outlines each rectangle as a closed five-point polyline through offset 24. 1684 bytes at offset 0x448490. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/mipolyrect.c`, where the disassembly has five indirect calls through those two offsets, no direct calls, and the clamp constants 32767, -32768 and 0xffff. It writes a temporary rectangle array and stack points. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miPolyText8` in `crm.so`: draws a string of 8-bit characters, a sibling of the 16-bit routine that loads single bytes. It adds the drawable origin when the context translates, calls `GetGlyphs` with the byte-per-character flag, and draws the found glyphs through the polygon-glyph method at offset 72 of the context's operation table, returning the new pen position. 500 bytes at offset 0x448b30. Checked against the same-named function in `mi/mipolytext.c`, where the disassembly (125 instructions against 133) loads bytes with a mask where the 16-bit routine loads halfwords, and again has one glyph lookup and one indirect call. Its calls: `GetGlyphs`. It writes nothing besides the return value. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miCreateETandAET` in `crm.so`: builds the edge table and the empty active edge list for the general polygon filler. For every polygon edge it works out which end is the top, drops horizontal edges, fills one edge entry (top and bottom y, the slope parameters of the Bresenham stepper, and a winding direction), and files it in the scan-line list of its top row, allocating more list blocks as needed. The extremes of y go into the table header. 1008 bytes at offset 0x448d30. Checked against the same-named function in `mi/mipolyutil.c`, whose disassembly shows one allocation and one free for the list blocks and the constants 304 (the block size) and 0x7fff and 0x8000 for the y limits. It writes the edge entries it was given. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miClipSpans` in `crm.so`: clips a list of spans against a region and writes the surviving pieces to an output list. For a region without a data block it clips each span to the extents box with a simple compare of the y range and of the left and right ends; for a banded region it walks the bands (sorting the input first with the span sorter if it is not already ordered) and emits the intersection of each span with each rectangle that overlaps its row. It returns the number of output spans. 1016 bytes at offset 0x449120. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/miregion.c` where the disassembly has one call, to the span sorter. Its calls: `QuickSortSpans..BN`. It writes the output arrays. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miTranslateRegion` in `crm.so`: shifts a region by a delta in x and y. It adds the offset to the extents and, if they still fit the signed 16-bit range, to every box of the data block. If the whole extent has moved off one side of that range it empties the region and frees its data. Otherwise it clamps the extents to the range, then walks the boxes, clamps each and drops the ones that collapse to nothing. A region with no data block just has its single extents box moved. 2156 bytes at offset 0x449520. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/miregion.c`, where 16-bit loads and stores of box fields (82 halfword loads, 90 halfword stores) and the two free calls dominate the disassembly. Its calls: `Xfree`. It writes region memory only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miRectsToRegion` in `crm.so`: builds a banded region from an array of rectangles. It creates an empty region and returns it when there are no rectangles. A single rectangle just sets the extents, with the far corner clamped to 32767 and nothing set when it has no area. For more it allocates a data block, converts each position-and-size rectangle to a box with the same clamp (dropping the ones of no area), then, unless the caller said the array is already sorted in y then x, calls the validate routine to sort and merge the boxes. If the allocation fails it returns the broken region. 704 bytes at offset 0x449d90. Derived from the same-named function in `mi/miregion.c` with the disassembly beside it: one allocation, one free, one validate call and one direct call of the region creator, and the clamp constant 32767. Its calls: `miRegionValidate`, `miRegionCreate`. It writes the new region. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miRegionReset` in `crm.so`: replaces the extents with the given box and frees the box block so the region has one box, 116 bytes at offset 0x44a050; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miregion.c`: the body copies the four halfwords of the given box into the extents, frees the block when its first word is non-zero and clears the data pointer at +0x8. Established.
- `miRegionCreate` in `crm.so`: allocates a region and initialises it with one box or as empty with room for a hint of boxes, 340 bytes at offset 0x44a0d0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miregion.c`, the same steps as the body (a box copy with a null data pointer, or the shared empty box with an optional block of the given size), except that the 12-byte record comes from the server allocator under the must-have-memory flag, so the broken-region result of the public body cannot occur, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miIntersect` in `crm.so`: intersects two regions into a third. It resets the result to empty when either input is empty, the extents do not overlap or either is broken (marking a broken input as such); when neither has a data block it just intersects the extents; when one is a single box that contains the other it copies the other; and otherwise it calls the region engine with the overlap function for intersection and then trims the extents. 804 bytes at offset 0x44a230. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/miregion.c`, where the disassembly shows one engine call, three region-copy calls and two frees. Its calls: `miRegionOp..MM`, `miRegionCopy`. It writes the result region. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miClearToBackground` in `crm.so`: clears a rectangle of a window to its background and optionally generates exposures for it. It turns the position and size into a box (a zero width or height meaning to the window's edge), clips it to the window's clip list extents, and if it is not empty, intersects it with the clip list, fills it with the background through the screen's paint-window method, and, when exposures were requested, sends them for the cleared area; for a window with backing store it also handles the saved region. 492 bytes at offset 0x44a560. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/miwindow.c`, where the disassembly has no direct calls and seven register-indirect calls through offsets such as 340 and 356. It writes window pixels only through those methods. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miZeroLine` in `crm.so`: draws one-pixel lines with the Bresenham stepper. It takes the drawable box clamped to 32767 on each axis and allocates a point array and a width array on the stack, each as long as the larger side. For each segment it computes the outcodes of both ends, the deltas, the octant and the X-major or Y-major error terms; a segment with an end outside the box is clipped (the clip routine is inlined) and the error term is recomputed for a moved start. The inner loop appends one point per step to the span list, which goes out through the context's span method at offset 0 of its operation table at each segment boundary and at the end. The last point is added only when the cap style is not not-last and the end was unclipped. 5408 bytes at offset 0x4501a0. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/mizerline.c`, where the disassembly has two indirect calls (the span flushes), no direct calls, and many conditional moves (86 `movz`, 64 `movn`) in the octant adjustment. It writes memory only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miHandleExposures` in `crm.so`: works out the part of a copy's destination that could not be filled from the source and, when the context asks for graphics exposures, sends exposure events for it and returns the region. It subtracts the visible source clip from the unclipped source box and translates the remainder to destination coordinates, intersects it with the destination clip, and then either paints the background of exposed window areas through the screen's paint-window method or sends the events through the exposure sender. It calls `NotClippedByChildren` for the source and destination regions when the context includes inferiors, and it frees the temporary regions through the screen's method vector. 1724 bytes at offset 0x456240. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/miexpose.c`; the disassembly has two calls to the covered-by-children routine and a heavy use of the screen method table (32 register-indirect calls, offsets such as 340 and 344). It writes region records. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miChangeGC` in `crm.so`: does nothing, an empty return, 8 bytes at offset 0x456900; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/migc.c`: the body is the same bare return, ignoring its arguments as the copy routine does. Established.
- `miPolySegment` in `crm.so`: draws each segment by calling the context's polyline method with two points in origin mode, 152 bytes at offset 0x456910; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/mipolyseg.c`, the same loop as the body, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miModifyPixmapHeader` in `crm.so`: rewrites a pixmap header's depth, bits per pixel, size, row pitch and data pointer, clears its position fields and gives it a new serial number, returning false for a null pixmap, 104 bytes at offset 0x4569b0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/mipixmap.c`: the body returns 0 for a null pixmap, stores the depth and bits per pixel as bytes at +0x2 and +0x3, zeroes the word at +0x4 and the halfwords at +0x8 and +0xa, stores the width and height at +0xc and +0xe, takes a new serial number from the global counter for +0x14, sets the reference count at +0x18 to 1 and stores the pitch and data pointer at +0x1c and +0x20. Established.
- `miWideLine` in `crm.so`: draws a connected line wider than one pixel. It sets up the span record (skipped for simple raster ops) and tests whether the first and last points coincide. For each pair of distinct points it builds the rotated rectangle of the segment, then either starts the line (a round cap through the arc helper, or one dot through the one-point helper when the width is 1 and there is no span record) or joins the segment to the previous one. The last point gets its round cap or the join back to the first face, and an all-coincident list draws a zero-length segment with two round caps. A span record is flushed through the cleanup routine. 6636 bytes at offset 0x456a20. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/miwideline.c`, where the disassembly shows the segment routine at two sites, the one-point helper at two, the arc helper at five, one join, one cleanup and one polygon-helper call. Its calls: `miWideSegment..FO`, `miLineJoin..BO`, `miLineArc..EO`. It reaches no register window. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miRegionNotEmpty` in `crm.so`: returns 0 when the region has a data block that holds no boxes and 1 otherwise, 40 bytes at offset 0x45b5a0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miregion.c`: the body loads the data pointer at +0x8, answers 1 when it is null, then loads the box count at +0x4 of the block and answers 0 when that is zero. Established.
- `miRegionAppend` in `crm.so`: appends the rectangles of one region to another without merging, ready for a later validate step, used when a region is built from many pieces. It returns the broken-region result if the source is broken, takes the source's extents directly when the destination is empty and the source has no data block, and otherwise grows the destination (with a minimum of 200 entries when it was empty), copies the source rectangles after the destination's, with a move when the source must go in front of the existing ones, and widens the destination extents to cover both. 1224 bytes at offset 0x45b5d0. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/miregion.c`; the disassembly shows one room check, two calls of `memmove` and the constants 200 and 8 (the box size). Its calls: `miRectAlloc`. It writes region memory only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miRegionValidate` in `crm.so`: turns a region whose rectangle list was appended unsorted (and possibly overlapping) into a proper banded region. It sorts the rectangles by band with the rectangle sort helper, then merges them into a set of region records, one per run of equal-top rectangles, combining them pairwise with the shared band engine until a single region is left, and reports through its second argument whether any input rectangles overlapped. It grows the working array through the realloc wrapper at four sites and allocates the temporary region records with the allocator. A region that already has no data block returns at once. 2884 bytes at offset 0x45baa0. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/miregion.c`, where the disassembly shows the sort call once, the region-combine routine once, and the constants 250 and 500 among its sizes. Its calls: `miRegionOp..MM`, `QuickSortRects..PM`. It writes region memory only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miUnion` in `crm.so`: forms the union of two regions into a third. It handles the trivial cases first: two identical regions or a second region that is empty copy the first, an empty first region copies the second, a broken input breaks the result, and a single box that covers the other region's extents yields that box. Everything else goes through the region engine with the union overlap and non-overlap functions, and the result extents are the bounding box of both. 712 bytes at offset 0x45c5f0. Checked against the same-named function in `mi/miregion.c`, where the disassembly has one engine call and five region-copy calls for the trivial cases. Its calls: `miRegionOp..MM`, `miRegionCopy`. It writes the result region. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miSubtract` in `crm.so`: subtracts one region from another into a result. It copies the minuend when the subtrahend is empty or does not overlap its extents, empties the result when the minuend is empty or the subtrahend covers it, marks a broken input as broken, and otherwise passes both to the region engine with the subtract overlap function and first-region-only non-overlap, then trims the extents. 520 bytes at offset 0x45c8c0. Checked against the same-named function in `mi/miregion.c`, where the disassembly shows one engine call, two region-copy calls and one free. Its calls: `miRegionOp..MM`, `miRegionCopy`. It writes the result region. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miHandleValidateExposures` in `crm.so`: delivers the exposures and border repaints that a clip validation left on a window and its children. It walks the window tree, and for each window holding validation data it repaints the exposed border through the screen's paint-border method, sends the exposed area through the screen's window-exposures method, frees the saved regions and the data record, and descends into children. 360 bytes at offset 0x45cad0. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/miwindow.c`, where the disassembly shows one free and five register-indirect screen calls (offset 352 twice among them). It writes window records. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miPolyGlyphBlt` in `crm.so`: draws text with a transparent background one glyph at a time. After adding the drawable origin when the context translates, it makes a scratch context (`GetScratchGC`), sets it to a stipple fill with the foreground, and for each glyph builds a one-bit bitmap from the glyph's bits, puts it into a scratch pixmap with the scratch context's put-image method (offset 8 of the operation table), and paints it into the drawable at the glyph's position through the context's push-pixels method (offset 76). The scratch context and the temporary pixmap are freed afterwards. 1168 bytes at offset 0x464430. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/miglblt.c`, with the disassembly showing one get and two frees of scratch contexts, one change and one validation, and seven register-indirect calls (pixmap create and destroy, put-image, push-pixels). Its calls: `GetScratchGC`, `FreeScratchGC`, `DoChangeGC`. It writes a temporary pixmap. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miPushPixels` in `crm.so`: paints through a bitmap mask: where the one-bit pixmap has a one, the context's current fill is drawn into the drawable. It reads each pixmap row through the screen's get-spans method, scans the row word by word and bit by bit, collects the runs of set bits as spans in a small buffer, and flushes them through the context's span method when the buffer fills and at the end, adding the destination origin to every span. A row buffer of the pixmap width, padded to words, comes from the allocator and goes back through the free routine. 1544 bytes at offset 0x4648c0. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/mipushpxl.c`, with one allocation and one free in the disassembly, eight register-indirect call instructions (seven span flushes at offset 0 and one get-spans at 168), and the shift constants 0x1f and 32 for the word scan. It writes row and span buffers. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miFillConvexPoly` in `crm.so`: fills a convex polygon with the classic edge-stepping scan converter. It scans the vertex list once for the top and bottom rows and the index of the top vertex, allocates a point and a width array of one entry per row on the stack, and walks the left edge forwards and the right edge backwards from the top vertex. At each vertex it sets up a Bresenham-style integer slope (the quotient, the quotient plus one and the two error increments), then emits one span per row between the two edges until a vertex is reached, and flushes the arrays in a single call of the context's span method at offset 0 of its operation table. It returns early with success for fewer than three points and gives up when the edges cross, which means the polygon was not convex. 3196 bytes at offset 0x46e3d0. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/mipolycon.c`, where the disassembly has exactly one indirect call, that span flush, no direct calls (the bounds scan and the slope initialiser macro are inlined), and prefetch instructions (10 `pref`) in the row loop. It writes only stack arrays. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miDestroyClip` in `crm.so`: releases the client clip by its type, a pixmap through the screen's pixmap destroy or a region through the region destroy, then clears the clip and its type, 152 bytes at offset 0x471a90; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/migc.c`, the same steps as the body, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miDestroyGC` in `crm.so`: releases a graphics context's owned objects: the rotated pixmap through the pixmap-destroy method at +0xf0, the composite clip through the region-destroy method at +0x15c when its flag is set, and a private operations table with `Xfree`, 176 bytes at offset 0x471b30; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/migc.c`, the same three releases as the body, but the rotated pixmap, composite clip and its free flag are read from the context's private record at +4, +8 and bit 6 of its first word rather than from the context itself; the operations table is freed when its marker word at +0x50 is set, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miCopyPlane` in `crm.so`: copies one bit plane of a source drawable into a destination as foreground and background pixels. It takes a plane mask, extracts the plane through the helper that builds a one-bit pixmap of it, and paints the bitmap into the destination through the opaque-stipple routine, with the usual source and destination clipping and the exposures built by `miHandleExposures` when the context asks. Derived from the same-named function in `mi/mibitblt.c` with the disassembly in view: `ffs` once, the plane extraction once, one direct call of the opaque-stipple routine, one call of the exposure routine and eight register-indirect calls into the screen tables. Its calls: `miHandleExposures`, `miGetPlane..MN`. It writes destination memory through the stipple routine. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miOpqStipDrawable` in `crm.so`: draws an opaque stipple (a bitmap painted in foreground where set and background where clear) in two passes. It builds a one-bit scratch pixmap as wide as the source offset plus the width, clears it through a scratch context, copies the source clip translated by the offset into that context, and stores the bitmap rows into the pixmap with the context's set-spans method (offset 4 of its operation table). It then changes the caller's context to stippled fill with the scratch pixmap as the stipple at the destination origin minus the source offset, fills the destination rectangle (drawing the foreground where bits are set), inverts the scratch pixmap with a copy-area under the invert raster operation, swaps the foreground and background pixels, and fills the rectangle again so the clear bits take the background. The caller's fill style, stipple and origin are restored at the end. 1488 bytes at offset 0x480730. Derived from the same-named function in `mi/mibitblt.c` with the disassembly beside it, showing one scratch-context get and two frees, five changes of context values, and the mask constants 14592 and 14604 among the immediates. Its calls: `GetScratchGC`, `miClearDrawable`, `dixChangeGC`. It writes pixmap memory only through those methods. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miPolyFillRect` in `crm.so`: fills a list of rectangles by turning each into one span per row. With the context's translate flag set it first adds the drawable origin to every rectangle while it finds the tallest one, then allocates a point array and a width array of that height on the stack. For each rectangle it writes one point and one width per row, with the same width and consecutive rows, and calls the context's span method at offset 0 of its operation table once per rectangle with the unsorted flag set to one. If either allocation fails it returns without drawing. 2364 bytes at offset 0x480d00. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/mifillrct.c`, and the disassembly has exactly one indirect call (the span fill), no direct calls and conditional moves (28 move-if-zero, 27 move-if-nonzero) in the maximum-height scan. It writes stack arrays and the caller's rectangle list. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miChangeClip` in `crm.so`: stores a new client clip in a graphics context, as a rectangle list or a copy of a bitmap-derived region, 296 bytes at offset 0x481640; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/migc.c`, the same steps as the body: it calls the clip-destroy slot at +0x14, turns a pixmap clip into a region with the screen's bitmap-to-region method at +0x198 and destroys the pixmap, stores a region as it is, builds a region from rectangles with the method at +0x19c and frees the list, then sets the type bits and the clip-mask change bit 0x80000 at +0x3c, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miPolyText16` in `crm.so`: draws a string of 16-bit characters. It adds the drawable origin when the context translates, asks `GetGlyphs` to turn the character pairs into glyph descriptions (the call is made with the two-byte-per-character flag), and, if any glyphs were found, draws them through the context's polygon-glyph method at offset 72 of its operation table, returning the pen x position advanced by the glyph widths. 532 bytes at offset 0x481770. Checked against the same-named function in `mi/mipolytext.c`, where the disassembly shows one call of the glyph lookup and one indirect call, with halfword character loads (`lhu`). Its calls: `GetGlyphs`. It writes nothing besides the return value. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miSetShape` in `crm.so`: reacts to a change of a window's shape (the clip shape used by the shape extension). If the window is viewable it saves the old clip, marks the windows the new shape could uncover, recomputes the size and border size through the screen's methods, validates the tree for a shape change, and tells the DDX of the restructure and confines the cursor again. 812 bytes at offset 0x489640. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/miwindow.c`, whose disassembly shows one call each of the size setter, the border setter, the child-size routine, the cursor-confinement routine and the restructure notice, and sixteen register-indirect calls. Its calls: `SetWinSize`, `SetBorderSize`, `WindowsRestructured`. It writes window records. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miInitializeBackingStore` in `crm.so`: switches a screen to backing store, 328 bytes at offset 0x4900c0; the public sample-server source `mi/mibstore.c` holds the same routine, and the body read in full matches it. On the first call of a server generation it takes a screen private index and a context private index. It then reserves a context private of size zero, allocates a 28-byte record and saves in it the screen's close, get-image, get-spans, window-attribute change, context-create and window-destroy methods together with a pointer to a module table. It installs the backing-store versions of those methods in the screen record, sets the six backing-store methods of the screen (save doomed areas, restore areas, expose copy, translate, clear and draw guarantee, at +0xf4 to +0x108) and stores the record in the screen's private array. A failed allocation returns without changing the screen. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miPolyArc` in `crm.so`: draws a list of arcs with the context's line width. A zero-width solid line goes arc by arc through the thin-arc code and then flushes the collected spans. For wider solid lines it first fills any complete ellipse through the cached wide-ellipse routine, then draws the remaining arcs, each followed by the caps and joins it is due, once per phase (background first for double-dash, switching the pixel with `dixChangeGC`). When the raster operation is not clear, copy, copy-inverted or set it draws into a one-bit scratch pixmap sized to the arcs' box widened by half the line width, using the screen's create-pixmap method at offset 236 and destroy method at offset 240, then pushes the pixels with the context's push-pixels method at offset 76 of its operation table and clears the scratch. 7924 bytes at offset 0x541d40. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/miarc.c`, where the thin-arc segment routine, the wide-ellipse fill and the cap and join drawers are folded in, so the body calls the zero-arc drawer twice, the quadrant arc drawer twice and the arctangent helper five times. Its calls: `miComputeArcs..IDB`, `miFreeArcs..GDB`, `miGetArcPts..CDB`. It stores to the scratch pixmap, arrays and context only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miFreeArcCache` in `crm.so`: frees the span data of every cached arc entry, clears each entry's age and width and resets the cache's age counter, clearing the cache type when the id is non-zero, 136 bytes at offset 0x543c40; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miarc.c`, the same loop as the body, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miCopyArea` in `crm.so`: copies a rectangle between drawables the portable way, one row at a time. It returns nothing when the destination window is not realized. Otherwise it takes the source clip (a box for a pixmap, or the region of the window not covered by children when the context includes inferiors), allocates a point array, a width array and a box-order array, and orders the clip boxes so overlapping copies inside one drawable are safe (reversing bands for rightward or downward moves). - `miGetImage` in `crm.so`: reads a rectangle of pixels from a drawable into a client buffer, in the format the request asked for. For a ZPixmap request whose plane mask does not cover every bit of the depth it takes a scratch context and a one-row pixmap, clears the row, sets the plane mask and then reads each source row through the screen's get-spans method (offset 168 of the screen record) and masks it; for an unmasked request it reads rows directly into the buffer. Derived from the same-named function in `mi/mibitblt.c` and checked against the disassembly, which shows one scratch-context get, two frees, two validates, one change of plane mask, and six register-indirect calls (two get-spans, one set-spans, one pixmap create, one destroy and one fill-spans). Its calls: `GetScratchGC`, `miGetPlane..MN`. It writes the client buffer and a scratch pixmap. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miPolyFillArc` in `crm.so`: fills a list of arcs or pie slices. For each arc it picks the whole-ellipse path, which steps the two edges down from the top row, or the slice path, which calls the slice setup routine to get the slice edges and fills each row between them; the drawable origin is added when the context translates. Rows leave through the context's span method at offset 0 of its operation table (four indirect calls). The integer and floating-point row loops of the public file are folded in. 4536 bytes at offset 0x545100. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/mifillarc.c` by call structure. Its calls: `miFillArcSliceSetup`. It writes point and width arrays only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miFillSppPoly` in `crm.so`: fills a convex polygon whose vertices are floating-point, which the round caps of wide arcs need. After translating by the integer and fractional offsets it finds the lowest vertex and the row range, allocates the point array, the width array and a mark array of one entry per vertex, then walks the left edge forward and the right edge backward from the lowest vertex. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/mifpolycon.c`, whose y-bounds scan is inlined, the disassembly shows one indirect call (that flush), no direct calls, 39 double compares and 16 conditional double moves, with the epsilon selected through `movt.d`. It writes only stack arrays. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miCopyGC` in `crm.so`: does nothing, an empty return, 8 bytes at offset 0x546c00; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/migc.c`: the body is a bare return with a no-op in its delay slot, so all three arguments are ignored. Established.
- `miCopyClip` in `crm.so`: copies the client clip from one graphics context to another, duplicating the region it holds, 244 bytes at offset 0x546c10; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/migc.c`, the same steps as the body: the type field decides between raising a pixmap's reference count at +0x18, copying a region through the region-create and region-copy methods at +0x150 and +0x158, and passing nothing, and each case ends in the destination's clip-change slot at +0x10, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miImageGlyphBlt` in `crm.so`: draws text with an opaque background: it fills the background rectangle of the string and then draws the glyphs on it. It asks `QueryGlyphExtents` for the string's overall extents, sets the context to a solid fill with the background colour, fills the extents rectangle through the fill-rectangle method (offset 44), restores the foreground, and then draws the glyphs through the polygon-glyph method (offset 72). 456 bytes at offset 0x546d10. Checked against the same-named function in `mi/miglblt.c`, where the disassembly shows the extents query once, three context changes, two validates and two indirect calls, with the constant 261 in the change mask. Its calls: `QueryGlyphExtents`, `DoChangeGC`. It writes the drawable only through those methods. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miImageText16` in `crm.so`: draws two-byte-per-character opaque text by looking up the glyphs and calling the glyph blit, 168 bytes at offset 0x546ee0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/mipolytext.c`, the same steps as the body: it fetches the glyph records with `GetGlyphs`, choosing the linear or two-dimensional two-byte layout by the font's last-row field, and, when any exist, calls the opaque glyph-blit slot at +0x44 with a zero glyph-base argument, which is also what the public body's glyph-base macro expands to, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miInverse` in `crm.so`: computes the complement of a region inside a bounding box, for example the part of a window not covered by a region. If the region is empty or does not meet the box it returns the box; otherwise it builds the result by subtracting the region from the box through the region engine, and trims the extents. 504 bytes at offset 0x546f90. Checked against the same-named function in `mi/miregion.c`, where the disassembly shows one engine call and one free. Its calls: `miRegionOp..MM`, `Xfree`. It writes the result region. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miPointInRegion` in `crm.so`: tests whether a point lies in a region and returns the box that contains it, 356 bytes at offset 0x547190; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miregion.c`, the same tests as the body: a quick extents test, a single-box shortcut that returns the extents, then a band-ordered scan that skips boxes above or left of the point and copies the matching box into the caller's result box, returning 1 on a hit and 0 otherwise, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miCreateScreenResources` in `crm.so`: creates the root pixmap and screen privates once the screen's frame buffer is known, 280 bytes at offset 0x547300; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miscrinit.c`, the same steps as the body: when the initialisation record at +0x74 gives a width it creates an empty pixmap with the create-pixmap method at +0xec, points its header at the screen's memory with the header-modify method at +0x1bc, then frees the record with `Xfree` and stores the pixmap, or the plain memory pointer, in its place, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miCloseScreen` in `crm.so`: destroys the screen's root pixmap through the screen's pixmap-destroy method and returns that result, 52 bytes at offset 0x547420; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miscrinit.c`: the body calls the method at +0xf0 of the screen record with the word at +0x74 of it as its only argument and returns what that call returns. Established.
- `miSubtractSpans` in `crm.so`: removes a set of spans from an existing group, splitting them where they overlap, 1100 bytes at offset 0x547460; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miAppendSpans` in `crm.so`: merges two sorted span groups into one list, interleaving by row so later painting stays top to bottom, 336 bytes at offset 0x5478b0; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miFillUniqueSpanGroup` in `crm.so`: turns an accumulated group of spans into a list with no row painted twice before filling, 2540 bytes at offset 0x547a00; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miValidateTree` in `crm.so`: recalculates the clip lists of a parent window's children after a configuration change such as a map, unmap, restack or resize. It sets up the total clip from the parent's client clip, walks the children marked as affected in stacking order (forward or backward by kind), for each computes its border clip from what remains, calls the clip computation for the child and its inferiors, accumulates the union of the marked children, and finally handles the parent's own exposure through the screen's method vector. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/mivaltree.c`; the disassembly shows one call of the clip pass, inside the child loop, and 29 register-indirect calls through screen method offsets such as 340 and 352. Its calls: `miComputeClips..EN`. It writes window and region records. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miShapedWindowIn` in `crm.so`: tests how a rectangle lies in the bounding shape of a shaped window. For each box of the shape, moved by the window origin, it clips the box to the rectangle and asks the screen's rectangle-in method (offset 380 of the screen record) how that clipped box lies in the visible universe, accumulating whether any part is in and whether any part is out, and stops early when both are known. Derived from the same-named function in `mi/mivaltree.c` with the disassembly beside it: one register-indirect call and four pairs of double-word shifts that sign-extend packed arguments. It writes nothing. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miWideDash` in `crm.so`: draws a connected sequence of dashed lines wider than one pixel. It hands two cases off first: a line width of zero goes to the zero-width dash routine, and double-dash with a tiled or opaque-stippled fill goes to the solid wide-line routine so that both phases take the fill. Otherwise it sums the deltas for the self-join test, steps the dash list by the dash offset, and cuts each segment at dash boundaries; every on-dash becomes a rotated rectangle of the line width, filled through the polygon helper or, for axis-aligned pieces, the rectangle helper, with round or projecting caps and joins to the neighbouring dash drawn by the arc and join helpers. It writes only its stack frame of 2560 bytes, allocated span buffers and the context. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miCleanupSpanData` in `crm.so`: finishes a wide line that was drawn into span groups. For double-dash it changes the context's foreground to the background pixel if different, fills the background group's unique spans and frees it, then restores the foreground; in every case it fills the foreground group's unique spans and frees that group. 280 bytes at offset 0x54d170. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/miwideline.c`, where the disassembly shows two direct calls of the group-fill routine, two frees, and two change-and-validate pairs. Its calls: `DoChangeGC`, `miFillUniqueSpanGroup`. It writes the span groups. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miGetLayerWindow` in `crm.so`: returns the window's first child, 8 bytes at offset 0x54d290; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miwindow.c`: the body loads the word at +0x24 of the window record into the return register. Established.
- `miSlideAndSizeWindow` in `crm.so`: moves and resizes a window while keeping the right pixels on screen. It records the old position and clip, works out the new position and size, and when the window is viewable and not just resized in place it saves the old clip list and the visible border, marks the windows the change uncovers, builds the region of each child that gravity would keep, moves each child by its gravity (many call sites), re-validates the clip lists, copies the surviving pixels through the screen's copy-window method, and generates exposures for the newly visible area. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/miwindow.c`, where the disassembly shows direct calls to the gravity translation (three sites), the tree traversal, the restructure notice, the stack move and the size and border setters, and 52 register-indirect calls through screen method offsets such as 336 and 344. - `miMarkOverlappedWindows` in `crm.so`: marks the windows whose clip lists change when a window is restacked, mapped or resized. When the window is the first changed one it marks it and every viewable inferior (rebuilding a broken size or border region first), else it computes the box that the move covers from the old and new border sizes and marks every sibling below that touches the box, and every viewable inferior of each. Marking is done through the screen's mark-window method, and the routine returns whether anything was marked. 552 bytes at offset 0x54de80. Derived from the same-named function in `mi/miwindow.c` with the disassembly in view: no direct calls, five register-indirect calls and the constant 0x6. It writes window flags. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miMarkWindow` in `crm.so`: gives a window that has no validation record a new 24-byte one holding its present corner, no border region and the not-resized flag, 108 bytes at offset 0x54e0b0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miwindow.c`: the body tests the word at +0x44 of the window, allocates 24 bytes through the allocator when it is zero, clears the words at +0x4 and +0x8 of the new record and copies the window's corner halfwords at +0x8 and +0xa into the first two halfwords of it. Established.
- `miPostChangeSaveUnder` in `crm.so`: after a save-under change, switches backing store back on for windows that no longer rely on the server's own save-under. It starts at the parent of the window and then at each window from the first changed sibling down through all inferiors; any window with no save-under, a backing-store hint of not useful and an existing backing store gets its backing-store attribute reapplied through the screen's change-attributes method. 292 bytes at offset 0x54e120. It writes window attributes only through that method. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miMoveWindow` in `crm.so`: moves a window to a new position without resizing it and keeps the pixels that stay visible. It records the old position and the old clip, sets the new origin, marks the windows the move uncovers, rebuilds the clip lists through the screen's validate method, copies the retained pixels through the screen's copy-window method and sends the exposures for the rest. It restacks the window when a next sibling is named, and it recomputes the child sizes and notifies the DDX of the restructure. 696 bytes at offset 0x54e250. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/miwindow.c`, where the disassembly shows one call each of the size setter, the border setter, the stack move, the child-size routine and the restructure notice, and 13 register-indirect screen calls such as offset 468. Its calls: `SetWinSize`, `MoveWindowInStack`, `WindowsRestructured`. It writes window records and regions. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miChangeSaveUnder` in `crm.so`: decides which windows must save the pixels under them when a window with save-under is mapped, walking the sibling list, 204 bytes at offset 0x54e510; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miwindow.c`, the same steps as the body: it returns zero when no save-under windows are or will be viewable, adds the pending delta to the count, builds a null region with the method at +0x154, calls the checker with the window's parent and the first or next sibling chosen by bit 25 of the flag word at +0x7c, releases the region with the method at +0x160 and returns the checker's result, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miMarkUnrealizedWindow` in `crm.so`: empties a window's clip list and border clip, calling the screen's clip-notify hook between them, unless it is the changed window itself and not from a configure, 124 bytes at offset 0x54e5e0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miwindow.c`, the same steps as the body, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miChangeBorderWidth` in `crm.so`: changes a window's border width and redraws what it exposes. It returns at once when the width is unchanged. When the window is viewable and the border shrinks it first marks the overlapped windows, then stores the new width and recomputes the border size, and for a growing border marks the overlapped windows afterwards; it then validates the clip lists and either redraws the border or generates exposures, tells the DDX of the restructure and, for a border appearing or disappearing, refreshes the window's cursor confinement. 504 bytes at offset 0x54e660. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/miwindow.c`, where the disassembly shows the border setter once and ten register-indirect screen calls, such as offset 468 three times. Its calls: `SetBorderSize`. It writes window records. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miZeroPolyArc` in `crm.so`: draws zero-width arcs one pixel at a time. For each arc it checks whether the exact integer stepper can handle it (the check is inlined) and sends the others one by one to the general arc routine; the point buffer is sized from the largest arc as the longer side plus half the shorter. The points of each arc are generated with the dash walk when the line style is not solid, then drawn through the context's point method at offset 20 of its operation table when the fill is solid, or through the span method at offset 0 with a run of width-one entries otherwise, adding the drawable origin first when the context translates.
