Compared against the same-named function in `mi/mizerarc.c`, whose point generators for solid and dashed arcs are inlined here; the disassembly shows one call to the setup routine, one to the general arc routine and two each to the context-change and validate routines. Its calls: `miZeroArcSetup`, `miPolyArc`. It stores to heap buffers and the context only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miFreeArcs..GDB` in `crm.so`: frees the arc work list built for a wide-arc rendering, 188 bytes at offset 0x5605b0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miarc.c`, the same steps as the body: for the double-dash line style it visits two 24-byte records and otherwise one, freeing each record's arc, join and cap lists when their counts at +0, +0x10 and +8 are above zero, then frees the array itself, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miUnionO..OM` in `crm.so`: merges the rectangles of one band from each of two regions into the result band of a union, the overlap callback given to the region engine. It walks both band lists in x order, always taking the one with the smaller left edge, and either appends it as a new rectangle or extends the previous rectangle to the right when the two touch or overlap. Output room is checked before every append and grown by the inlined rectangle allocator, whose sizing constants 250 and 500 appear. 1472 bytes at offset 0x561310. Checked against the static overlap function for union in `mi/miregion.c`, where the disassembly shows allocation, reallocation and rectangle-grow code at three sites each. It writes region memory only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miDcos..OCB` in `crm.so`: returns the cosine of an angle given in degrees, exact at multiples of 90. It tests whether the angle divided by 90 is whole, then takes the index modulo 4 and returns the exact value from a four-entry case table, and otherwise converts to radians and calls the library cosine. 280 bytes at offset 0x562ed0. Compared with the cosine helper in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly shows one floor call, one cosine call and a four-way jump, and is 70 instructions long against the sine routine's 71. It writes nothing. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miClearDrawable` in `crm.so`: paints the whole drawable with the context's background pixel by temporarily making it the foreground and filling one rectangle, 192 bytes at offset 0x562ff0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miexpose.c`, the same steps as the body: it saves the foreground and background pixels at +0x18 and +0x1c, sets the foreground to the background through `DoChangeGC`, validates, fills a rectangle of the drawable's width and height at origin zero with the fill-rectangle slot at +0x2c and restores the foreground, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miFillPolyHelper` in `crm.so`: fills a polygon described by up to two left and two right edge records, the final step of every wide-line and join. It takes a start row, an overall height, edge records with their remaining counts, and an optional span record. With no span record it allocates a point array and a width array, and if the line colour differs from the context's foreground it switches the foreground with a change-context call, validates, runs the edges row by row (each edge steps x by its quotient plus a Bresenham remainder, reloading from the next edge record when the current one is used up), writes one span for every row where right is not left of the left edge, and fills them through the context's span method at offset 0 with the sorted flag set before restoring the colour. With a span record it calls `Xalloc` and `miAppendSpans` to buffer the spans for later de-duplication. The drawable origin is added when the context translates. 1400 bytes at offset 0x5647f0. Read against the polygon filler in `mi/miwideline.c`, where the binary shows the allocation pair, two change-and-validate calls, one append call and the edge-reload macros inlined. Its calls: `DoChangeGC`. It writes span buffers. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miCreateBSPixmap..ACB` in `crm.so`: creates the pixmap that backs a window's backing store and prepares it. Unless allocation already failed, it takes the extents of the saved region, creates a pixmap of that size and the window's depth through the screen's create-pixmap method (offset 236 of the screen record), records the pixmap origin, and when the window's contents were being tracked only virtually it fills the new pixmap with the window background through the backing-store clear routine and sends any exposures for the part that had to be regenerated. A failure marks the store as bad. 612 bytes at offset 0x564ee0. Derived from the same-named function in `mi/mibstore.c` with the disassembly beside it: one call of the clear routine, one of the exposure sender, and five register-indirect screen calls including create (236) and destroy (240). Its calls: `miBSClearBackingStore..NAB`, `miSendExposures`. It writes backing-store records. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miDasin..ADB` in `crm.so`: returns the arc sine in degrees, exact at the ends of its range and the library arc sine scaled to degrees elsewhere, 120 bytes at offset 0x5662c0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miarc.c`, the same cases as the body, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miComputeEllipseSpans..GCB` in `crm.so`: fills the cached table of row spans for one wide ellipse, which is later reused for every arc of that size. It works quadrant-wise: for each row it finds the outer ellipse x, the inner ellipse x for the width-reduced ellipse, and the points where the inner ellipse stops being reachable, with a closed form using `sqrt`, a cube root and an arccosine when the inner ellipse is thin. The results go into the span-data records, one inner and one outer extent per row. 2732 bytes at offset 0x568e70. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly shows five direct `sqrt` calls, two `cbrt`, one `acos` and one `cos`, seven calls through a pointer slot at offset 264 whose target was not read, and many double compares (34 `c.lt.d`). It writes the span table only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miComputeArcs..IDB` in `crm.so`: builds the description of a list of arcs that the wide-arc drawer works from. A first pass computes both end points of every arc from the sine and cosine of its angles in 64ths of a degree, clamps the span to one full turn of 23040 units and flags an arc that closes on itself. A second pass walks the arcs from a start index found by looking back for the first arc whose end does not meet the next arc's start, adding a cap or a join wherever end points coincide within a small tolerance. For dashed lines it calls `computeDashMap..HDB` to get the length-to-angle table and cuts each arc into dash pieces filed under the foreground or, for double-dash, the background phase. The arc, cap and join arrays grow through the realloc wrapper at nine sites, and the routine returns the record, or nothing after an allocation failure. 10244 bytes at offset 0x56bd20. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/miarc.c`, whose cap, join, arc-adding and angle-to-length helpers are folded into this body; the disassembly makes one allocation, two sine and two cosine calls, four floor calls and one call to the arc-list release routine, and uses the horizontal and vertical zero-area constants 0x2d00 and 0x1680. It builds heap records only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miLineProjectingCap` in `crm.so`: draws the square end that projects half a line width past a segment end, used when a path starts or ends where the cap style is projecting. Given a face record (end position, direction and offset terms), it takes the cap origin from the face when the origin is a whole number, or from the caller's floating-point point when not, and then splits on the face direction: a horizontal face fills a strip lw rows tall from two vertical edges, a vertical face builds its two edge records from the half width and the face length, and a slanted face builds the full set of four edges through the edge builder (inlined). Each of the three cases ends in its own call of the polygon helper. 2296 bytes at offset 0x5707a0. Derived from the same-named function in `mi/miwideline.c` with the disassembly in view: three calls to the polygon helper, no other direct calls, and 16 double-precision divides in the edge set-up. Its calls: `miFillPolyHelper`. It writes edge records on its stack only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miGetPlane..MN` in `crm.so`: extracts one bit plane of a drawable into a packed one-bit-per-pixel buffer for the XY image formats and plane copies. It allocates a buffer of whole words, zeroes it, then reads each row of the source through the screen's get-spans method (offset 168 of the screen record, two sites), shifts the selected plane bit of each pixel into the packed row, and returns the buffer. The word size of 32 and the shift limit of 31 appear in the packing loop. 700 bytes at offset 0x5719a0. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/mibitblt.c`, with the disassembly showing one allocation, one `bzero` and two indirect get-spans calls, and two double-word shifts around the 32-bit packing. Its calls: `Xalloc`, `bzero`. It writes the buffer. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miLineJoin..BO` in `crm.so`: draws the join between two adjacent wide segments, given the face record of the earlier segment's end and the later one's start. A one-pixel-wide line with no span record is special: it returns when either segment already draws the joining pixel or when the segments are collinear, and otherwise plots a single dot (bevel and round) or falls through to the miter. A round join goes to the arc helper. Bevel and miter first test the cross product of the two directions, return for collinear segments, mirror the faces so the turn is always the same way, and then build a triangle (bevel) or a quadrilateral (miter) from the four face points, checking the miter limit of the angle before it chooses the bevel. The polygon is filled through the polygon helper. 4136 bytes at offset 0x572150. Compared against the same-named static function in `mi/miwideline.c`, where the disassembly has one polygon-helper call, one arc-helper call and one one-point call, and heavy double-precision work (42 `mov.d`, 24 `mul.d`). Its calls: `miFillPolyHelper`, `miLineArc..EO`, `miLineOnePoint..AO`. It writes locals and span buffers only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miRoundCap..NCB` in `crm.so`: draws the round end of a wide arc as a filled half-circle polygon. It finds the angle of the cap face with the arctangent helper (two calls), generates the points of a half-circle through the arc-point routine, appends the face's end points and fills the polygon with the floating-point polygon filler, then frees the point array. 420 bytes at offset 0x574370. Compared with `miRoundCap` in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly shows one direct call of the arc-point routine, one polygon fill, one free and 15 double loads. Its calls: `miDatan2..BDB`, `miGetArcPts..CDB`, `miFillSppPoly`. It writes a temporary point array. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miGetArcPts..CDB` in `crm.so`: turns an arc into a polyline of points on the ellipse for the round cap and thin-arc code. It negates the angles for the flipped y axis, picks a step angle from the arc's larger half-dimension (the arcsine of one over that radius, so a step moves at most about a pixel), rounds the point count to cover the whole sweep, generates points with the cosine recurrence from two starting points, and grows the destination array through `Xrealloc` (one call). It returns the number of points, adding a centre point for a pie slice. 1020 bytes at offset 0x5776e0. Checked against the same-named function in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly shows fused multiply-subtract instructions for the recurrence (8 of `msub.d`), one call of the arcsine helper, four of the cosine helper and three of the sine helper. Its calls: `miDasin..ADB`, `miDcos..OCB`, `miDsin..PCB`. It writes the points array. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miRegionDestroy` in `crm.so`: frees the box block when allocated and then the region record unconditionally, 88 bytes at offset 0x577d30; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miregion.c`, identical except that it lacks the test for the shared broken region that the 6.8.2 body has, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miDsin..PCB` in `crm.so`: returns the sine of an angle given in degrees, exact at multiples of 90. It tests whether the angle divided by 90 is a whole number, in which case it takes the index modulo 4 and returns 0, 1, 0 or -1 from a small jump table, and otherwise converts to radians and calls the library sine. 284 bytes at offset 0x577f80. Compared with the sine helper in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly shows one floor call, one sine call, a four-way jump through jr instructions and a divide by 90. It writes nothing. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miLineArc..EO` in `crm.so`: draws the round part of a wide line, a full circle or a half circle bounded by one or two faces. When the centre is a whole number it takes the position from the face record, builds the bounding edge records from the face slopes for the left or right face (the edge record gets its start row, a left flag and a direction), allocates point and width arrays sized to the line width (two `Xalloc` calls), fills them either by integer stepping or by calling the floating-point row routine, and then sends them out. With no span record it switches the foreground to the pixel, validates, fills spans through the context's method at offset 0 and restores the pixel; with one it appends the spans to the span group instead. 3920 bytes at offset 0x57d820. Compared against the same-named static function in `mi/miwideline.c`, whose integer row filler is inlined here; the disassembly shows `Xalloc` twice, the span-group append once, `Xfree` once and two change-and-validate pairs. Its calls: `miLineArcD..DO`, `miAppendSpans`. It stores to the arrays it allocates. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miRegionOp..MM` in `crm.so`: is the shared engine of union, intersection, subtract and inverse on two regions. It sweeps both rectangle lists one horizontal band at a time and, per band, calls the overlap function it was given when both regions have rectangles there, or the matching non-overlap function when only one does, merging a band into the previous one when the rectangles repeat. The result array grows through the rectangle allocator and `Xrealloc`, runs are moved with `memmove` twice, and the old data block is released. The overlap and non-overlap callbacks it receives are called indirectly. 5372 bytes at offset 0x57fee0. Compared against the static region-operation function in `mi/miregion.c`, where the region layout is the 6.8.2 one, 16-bit box fields with a block holding size and count before the boxes. Its calls: `miRectAlloc`, `Xfree`. It writes region memory only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miRectAlloc` in `crm.so`: grows a region's box array by doubling or from the single-box inline form, 296 bytes at offset 0x581830; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miregion.c`, the same three growth cases as the body (no data yet, an empty shared block, a full block grown by the old count or 250 when it exceeds 500) with the server allocator and reallocator, but it raises the must-have-memory flag around the allocation, so a failure is fatal where the public body returns the broken region, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miZeroDashLine` in `crm.so`: sets the context's line width to one, calls the wide-dash routine and restores width zero, 72 bytes at offset 0x581a10; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/mizerline.c`: the body stores 1 to the halfword at +0x6 of its second argument, calls the wide-dash routine through the global table and stores 0 to the same halfword. Established.
- `miLineArcD..DO` in `crm.so`: fills the pixel rows of a round join or cap in floating point, for the case where the arc centre is not on an integer position. For each row it computes the left and right ends of the circle from the radius and the vertical offset, uses `floor` and a ceiling of the edge positions, and trims the row by up to two slanted edges (the cap or join faces), each described by its start row, direction flag and edge record, writing point and width entries. 3936 bytes at offset 0x582f50. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/miwideline.c`, where the disassembly shows three `floor` calls and 64 double adds, 39 subtractions and 32 `c.lt.d` compares in the row loop. It stores into the caller's point and width arrays. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miComputeWideEllipse..ICB` in `crm.so`: looks up or builds the table of row spans for an ellipse of a given size and line width. A width of zero becomes one; for ellipses up to 1500 rows tall it first checks the most recently used cache entry and then the whole cache for the same width, height and line width, bumping a use stamp on a hit; on a miss it evicts the least recently used entry, registers a resource type with `CreateNewResourceType` (once) so the cache is freed at server reset, allocates the span-data block and fills it through the ellipse-span routine. Taller ellipses are built into a private block that the caller must free, which it signals through the second argument. 1504 bytes at offset 0x583fa0. Compared against `miComputeWideEllipse` in `mi/miarc.c`; the disassembly shows the resource-type, fake-client-id and add-resource calls once each, and the allocation and free pair. Its calls: `miComputeEllipseSpans..GCB`, `AddResource`. It writes cache records. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miCheckSubSaveUnder..CN` in `crm.so`: walks the children of a window to see whether a save-under window obscures any of the windows below it, and so whether its saved area must be preserved. It first adds the borders of the viewable save-under siblings above the first changed window to the region, then for each viewable window at or below it intersects that window's border with the region, recursing into children with a separate sub-region so nephews are not saved under, and returns true when something is obscured. 696 bytes at offset 0x585890. Derived from the same-named function in `mi/miwindow.c` with the disassembly in view, showing two recursive self-calls, nine register-indirect region calls and the window flag constants 0x8000 and 0x100. It writes region records. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miFillRectPolyHelper..PN` in `crm.so`: fills an axis-aligned rectangle for a wide line's horizontal or vertical segment. With no span record it makes one rectangle, switches the foreground to the line colour with `DoChangeGC` and validates when the colour differs, draws it through the context's fill-rectangle method at offset 44 of its operation table, and puts the colour back. With a span record it fills a point and width array with one span per row (adding the drawable origin when the context translates) and appends it to the span group. 740 bytes at offset 0x5890d0. Checked against the static rectangle helper in `mi/miwideline.c`; the disassembly shows two allocations, a free, one append call, one change-and-validate pair, three register-indirect calls and prefetches (4 of them). Its calls: `miAppendSpans`. It writes span arrays. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miIntersectO..NM` in `crm.so`: computes the overlap of one band of each of two regions for an intersection, the overlap callback given to the region engine. It walks both rectangle lists in x order and appends, for each pair that overlaps, a rectangle bounded by the larger left edge and the smaller right edge, advancing whichever list ends first; the output room is checked and grown through the inlined rectangle allocator before each append. 556 bytes at offset 0x58a3f0. Compared with the static intersect function in `mi/miregion.c`, where the disassembly has one reallocation and two allocations in the room check and the sizing constants 250 and 500. It writes region memory only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miTileVirtualBS..PBB` in `crm.so`: refreshes the virtual-tile record of a graphics context that draws into backing store, 176 bytes at offset 0x58c1c0; the public sample-server source `mi/mibstore.c` holds the same routine, and the body read in full matches it. It takes the record at +0x74 of the context. When the record's state byte at +0x16 equals three it first calls the release method at +0xf0 of the method table at +0x10 of the context, with the pixmap word at +0x18 of the record. It then stores the top two bits of the context's word at +0x7c (the fill style) in the state byte and the context's tile word at +0x6c in the record, bumps the reference count at +0x18 of that tile when the state is three, and sets the record's byte at +0x15 to two unless it is three. A state of one makes it call `miCreateBSPixmap..ACB` with a zero second argument. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miWideSegment..FO` in `crm.so`: turns one segment of a wide line into filled pixels and records the cap faces of its two ends. It orders the end points top to bottom (swapping the projecting flags and faces), then takes one of three paths: a horizontal segment becomes a rectangle of the line width, with the ends moved out by half a width where a projecting cap applies; a vertical one likewise; and a slanted one gets edge slopes from the length (`hypot`) and half-width, four edge records built by the edge builder (inlined), and a call to the polygon helper. Axis-aligned segments go to the rectangle helper. The faces it writes (position, direction, and the offset terms for the join) are what the join and cap routines use. 4272 bytes at offset 0x58c270. Compared against the same-named static function in `mi/miwideline.c`, where the disassembly shows `hypot` once, the rectangle helper at two sites, the polygon helper once, and 33 double-precision divides in the unrolled edge set-up. Its calls: `miFillRectPolyHelper..PN`, `miFillPolyHelper`. It stores to the two face records and span buffers only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miSubtractO..AN` in `crm.so`: computes the overlap of one band of the minuend with one band of the subtrahend, giving the pieces of the minuend left over, the overlap callback of the subtract operation. It walks both rectangle lists, emitting the parts of each minuend rectangle to the left of the next subtrahend rectangle and keeping the remainder to compare with the following one, and checks memory with the inlined room test before each append. 936 bytes at offset 0x58d4e0. Compared with the static subtract function in `mi/miregion.c`, with the disassembly showing two `Must_have_memory` flag stores, two allocations and one reallocation. It writes region memory only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miResizeBackingStore..CBB` in `crm.so`: adjusts a window's backing-store pixmap when the saved region changes size or the contents move. It reads the saved region's extents, and if the pixmap no longer has the right size it creates a new one (or drops the pixmap when bits need not be saved), copies the still-useful bits across with the scratch context's copy-area method (offset 12 of its operation table) shifted by the move, destroys the old pixmap through the screen's destroy method, and updates the stored origin. 568 bytes at offset 0x5905d0. Checked against the same-named function in `mi/mibstore.c`, where the disassembly has one scratch-context get and free, one validate, and four register-indirect calls (create, destroy, copy and a region method). Its calls: `GetScratchGC`, `FreeScratchGC`. It writes backing-store records. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miComputeClips..EN` in `crm.so`: recomputes the clip lists of one window and, recursively, of its mapped children. From the window's border box it works out the visibility class (fully visible, partly obscured or not visible) from how much of the supplied universe region covers the box, calls `SendVisibilityNotify` to send a visibility-change event when the class changed and the window selected it, and then sets the border clip and the client clip (the border clip minus the border) through the screen's intersect, subtract and copy methods at offsets such as 340 and 364 of the screen record. Each child is handled by a recursive call on the part of the universe it covers, after subtracting the child's border region from what is left for lower siblings; overlapping children take a union step. Shaped windows are tested with a call of `miShapedWindowIn`. 2236 bytes at offset 0x5928c0. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/mivaltree.c`, where the disassembly has one recursive self-call, and 34 register-indirect call instructions through screen method offsets, and uses the constant 32767 for coordinate clamping. It writes window and region records. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miDatan2..BDB` in `crm.so`: returns the arctangent of dy over dx in degrees, exact for the axes and diagonals. A zero dy gives zero or a half turn by the sign of dx, a zero dx gives a quarter turn either way by the sign of dy, equal magnitudes give the diagonal angle of the quadrant, and anything else calls the library arctangent and scales from radians. 268 bytes at offset 0x595630. Compared with the arctangent helper in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly has one library arctangent call, six branch-on-compare instructions and double constants loaded from read-only data. It writes nothing. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `miRecomputeExposures..DN` in `crm.so`: recomputes the exposed region of a window against its parent when a window is resized or shaped, 184 bytes at offset 0x5975a0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miwindow.c`, the same three subtractions as the body, using the subtract method at +0x16c: window exposure is the clip list minus the valid region, border exposure is the border clip minus the window size and then minus the valid region; it returns 1 for a window with a validation record and 3 otherwise, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miLineOnePoint..AO` in `crm.so`: draws a single pixel for a one-pixel-wide line end or join. It switches the context's foreground to the line colour when it differs (a change and validate), then for a solid fill draws one point through the point method at offset 20 of the operation table, or for other fills one span of width one through the span method at offset 0 with the drawable origin added, and puts the colour back. 348 bytes at offset 0x59b500. Compared with `miLineOnePoint` in `mi/miwideline.c`, where the disassembly has two change-and-validate pairs and two indirect calls. Its calls: `DoChangeGC`. It writes nothing but the drawable through those methods. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.

## Backing store (miBS)

- `miBSCheapDestroyClip..FP` in `crm.so`: restores the context's saved function vector, calls the original destroy-clip method and wraps the vector again, 96 bytes at offset 0x5612b0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/mibstore.c`, the same steps as the body: it installs the saved table, calls its clip-destroy slot at +0x14 and reinstalls the wrapper table, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miBSAllocate..ABB` in `crm.so`: sets up backing-store bookkeeping for a window when the screen supports it. Its argument is the window, and it returns at once when the screen's support flag (byte 36 of the screen record) is zero. If the window has no record it allocates 28 bytes, zeroes the pixmap pointer and the x and y shorts, makes the saved region empty through screen slot 340, copies the viewable bit, and sets status 1 and background state 0. When the status is still 1 and the window asks for backing when mapped and is viewable, or asks for it always, it builds the window's box, inverts the clip list through slot 368, translates by the negated origin through slot 376, and intersects with the bounding and clip shapes through slot 356 when those exist; it then marks a viewable window dirty, retiles the virtual background and sends exposures for the whole saved region. A window that is not viewable and had saved something gets its region emptied through slot 392 and its pixmap destroyed through slot 240. 704 bytes at offset 0x561c80. Compared with the corresponding routine of mi/mibstore.c, the disassembly shows the allocator call with the constant 28, calls to the tile and exposure helpers, and the shape tests on the two region fields of the shape block. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miBSChangeWindowAttributes..MO` in `crm.so`: reacts to a window attribute change by allocating or freeing the window's backing store as the attribute asks, 196 bytes at offset 0x561f50; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/mibstore.c`, the same steps as the body: it restores the saved attribute method at +0xc0, calls it and, when it succeeds and the mask has the backing-store bit 0x40, allocates the window's backing store when its backing-store field (bits 26 and 27 of the word at +0x7c) is nonzero or bit 24 is set and frees it otherwise, then reinstalls itself, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miBSPolyPoint..OP` in `crm.so`: is the backing-store wrapper for the point-drawing request: it draws on the window and then replays the same points into the saved pixmap. Its arguments are the window, the graphics context, the coordinate mode, the point count and the points. It swaps the context's operation and function tables for the saved ones, allocates a point-count times four byte scratch array on the stack, and returns early if that allocation is null. For origin-mode points it copies each point with the backing store's origin (shorts in the record) subtracted, four points per iteration with prefetch; for previous-mode it block-copies the array and subtracts the origin from the first point only. It calls operation slot 20 of the window's table and then slot 20 of the backing context's table, and finally saves and reinstates the wrapper tables. 592 bytes at offset 0x562510. Read against the corresponding routine of mi/mibstore.c, the assembly has one block-copy call, two indirect calls (slot 20 of each table) and one unrolled loop of four shorts per pass. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miBSPushPixels..MAB` in `crm.so`: pushes a stencil on the screen and into the backing pixmap, 304 bytes at offset 0x562760; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/mibstore.c`: the body takes the same steps as the other backing-store wrappers and calls slot +0x4c of both ops tables, and when bit 17 of the context's flag word at +0x10 is set it first subtracts the stipple origin halfwords at +0x8 and +0xa of its pixmap argument from the position it passes the second time. Established.

The loop is unrolled by two, with an odd-count remainder, and the two indirect calls go through slot 32.
