Established.
- `miBSPolyText8..GAB` in `crm.so`: draws one-byte transparent text on the screen and into the backing pixmap, 248 bytes at offset 0x597660; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/mibstore.c`: the body takes the same steps as the sixteen-bit version, calling slot +0x34 of both ops tables, and returns the result of the first call. Established.
- `miBSCloseScreen..JO` in `crm.so`: unwraps the screen procedures that backing store replaced and closes the screen, 148 bytes at offset 0x599da0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/mibstore.c`, the same steps as the body: it puts back the five saved screen methods (close at +0x98, image reader at +0xa4, span reader at +0xa8, attribute change at +0xc0, context creation at +0x130), frees the private record with `Xfree` and calls the restored close method with the screen number and screen, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miBSFillSpans..IP` in `crm.so`: wraps the span-fill request so that the same horizontal spans reach both the window and its saved pixmap. The inputs are the drawable, context, span count, start points, widths and a sorted flag. After swapping in the saved operation tables it takes two stack scratch buffers of four bytes per span each (one for the shifted points, one for the widths), subtracts the backing origin from every start point in a four-way unrolled prefetching loop, block-copies the width array, and calls slot 0 of the window's operation table with the originals. When the context's coordinate-translate flag (bit 17 of the word at offset 16) is set it moves the copied points by the difference between the window origin and the backing pixmap origin, and then calls slot 0 of the backing context with the copies. If either scratch allocation fails it skips both draws, and the tables are restored on every path. 2116 bytes at offset 0x59a940. Compared with the corresponding routine of mi/mibstore.c, the disassembly has one block-copy call, two indirect calls and prefetch hints on source and destination. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miBSCopyArea..MP` in `crm.so`: implements the copy-area request for a window with backing store, so the pixels land both on screen and in the saved pixmap. Its inputs are source and destination drawables, the context, the source rectangle and the destination point, and it returns the combined graphics-expose region. After the usual swap of operation tables, a copy from a window to itself first tries the scroll helper; only if that reports the source disjoint from the saved region does the general path run. That path clips the rectangle to the extents of the backing context's client clip (when it is not a pixmap clip), by advancing source and destination together and shrinking the width and height, calls the copy-area slot (12) of the backing context with the destination shifted by the record's origin, and then calls slot 12 of the window's own table; when the context's clip is a pixmap clip it skips the clamp and makes the same two calls with the rectangle unclipped. The two exposure regions are merged through the screen's union slot (360) and the extra one destroyed through slot 348. 872 bytes at offset 0x59b190. Compared with the corresponding routine of mi/mibstore.c, the assembly has one call to the scroll helper, the clip-extents fetch through slot 396, the same four clamp steps and the zero plane argument. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miBSSaveDoomedAreas..DBB` in `crm.so`: captures the bits of a window that are about to be covered, into the window's saved pixmap, and grows the saved region to include them. It takes the window, the region about to be obscured and the distance the window moves. For an unrealized window it copies the viewable bit into the record and, unless backing is requested always, empties the saved region and drops the pixmap; if the status is bad-allocation it resets it to no-pixmap. A virtual status with background state 0 returns at once. A non-empty obscured region is translated to window coordinates, merged into the saved region through the screen's union slot (360) after the old extents are copied to the stack, and, unless the status is virtual, either creates the pixmap or resizes it; the actual copy goes through the backing-store table reached from the screen-private slot at screen offset 436, first entry, with the origin adjusted by the record's x and y. 736 bytes at offset 0x59b660. Compared with the corresponding routine of mi/mibstore.c, unlike the public routine the disassembly calls the save hook through a screen-private table and has no branch for a broken region. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `miBSCheapCopyClip..EP` in `crm.so`: restores the context's saved function vector, calls the original copy-clip method and wraps the vector again, 96 bytes at offset 0x59b940; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/mibstore.c`, the same steps as the body: it installs the saved table from the destination context's private record, calls its clip-copy slot at +0x18 and reinstalls the wrapper table, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.

## Colour frame buffer (cfb, cfb16, cfb32)

- `cfb8LineSS1Rect` in `crm.so`: is the front end for solid one-pixel polylines under a one-rectangle clip on one-byte pixels: it picks a fast drawing routine and a clipped-line routine from the raster operation byte in the context's private record, then alternates between them until the point list is used up. A copy operation takes the copy pair (with a previous-coordinate variant when the caller's mode is relative), xor takes the xor pair, and anything else the general pair. For relative mode it first seeds the running start point from the first list entry; each pass calls the fast routine with the drawable, context, mode, remaining count, list pointers and four result slots on the stack, and when that returns minus one the polyline is finished. Otherwise it calls the clipped-line routine on the segment that stopped it, giving the clip region's extents and a drop-last-pixel flag, then advances the list by the number drawn. The flag is 1 when the first and last points coincide (a closed path), when the run stopped before the final point, or when the cap-style field (bits 28 and 29 of the context word at offset 16) is zero; it is 0 only for an open run that reached the final point with a nonzero cap style. 732 bytes at offset 0x4292a0; read in full against `cfb/cfb8line.c` (the polyline front end), the same loop and flag rule as the body; the fast and clipped routines are called through registers loaded from the address list at entry, which names three of each. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb8LineSS1RectCopy` in `crm.so`: draws a solid one-pixel polyline on one-byte pixels with the copy raster operation while every segment lies inside one clip box. The copy build for byte pixels has the arguments, box, return value and last-pixel rule of the one-byte general routine `cfb8LineSS1RectGeneral`. Its byte pixel step stores the context's xor word as it is and reads neither the old pixel nor the and word. The byte copy routine is 2304 bytes (576 instruction slots) at offset 0x429580 with 32 byte stores, 73 movz, 1 movn, a 272-byte frame, no calls and no floating point. The public one-rectangle polyline routine of cfb/cfb8line.c in its copy build is the counterpart. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: 250 random byte plain copy polylines (points from -10 to 74, drawable origins from -3 to 3, boxes of random size, two cap styles, random tie words and background) matched a separately written reference in return value and pixels, and 40 more with random and and xor words matched the per-pixel rule.
- `cfb32SegmentSS1Rect` in `crm.so`: draws solid one-pixel segments for a clip of one rectangle, skipping the per-box clip loop on four-byte pixels, 332 bytes at offset 0x429e80; read in full against the public sample-server source `cfb/cfb8line.c`: the body takes the same steps as the eight-bit routine with the thirty-two-bit private-index word and slot pairs. Established.
- `cfb32SegmentSS1RectCopy` in `crm.so`: draws solid segments in four-byte pixels by storing the colour word directly, one rectangle of clip; it takes the drawable, the context, a segment count and the segment array, packs the end points into single words, uses the 0x8000 and 0xfffe masks to test the box, and writes pixels with word stores only (66 stores, 71 conditional moves choosing the direction, none of the and or xor words of its siblings), 2560 bytes at offset 0x429fd0; it is the plain-copy case of the single-rectangle segment function in `cfb/cfb8line.c`; the digest shows 640 instructions and one indirect call, the only one of the three segment builds that makes a call; the loops were not read in detail. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfbPolyFillArcSolidCopy` in `crm.so`: fills a list of arcs and ellipses with a solid colour using the copy raster operation, on one-byte pixels, dropping any arc whose width or height is zero. An arc goes to the generic fill routine (invoked with a one-element list) unless both dimensions are at most 800 and its bounding box, widened by one, is wholly inside the clip as reported by the screen's rectangle-in-region method at offset 380 (called once per arc). Wholly clipped-in full-circle arcs (angle span at least 23040, a full turn in sixty-fourths of a degree) are filled with the midpoint ellipse stepper, writing a left span and right span per row pair as masked words. 5688 bytes at offset 0x42a9d0. Compared with `cfb/cfbfillarc.c` (the copy-rop build). Differences seen from the general sibling `cfbPolyFillArcSolidGeneral`: this one has 1422 instructions to the other's 2711, no call to the arc-slice setup helper, and only 88 doubleword stores against 222, because no and/xor mask pair is kept alive. Its two call sites are the generic fill routine at instruction 36 and the region method at 96. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: dispatch tests, the call sites and the full-circle comparison were read; the span loops were not, and how partial arcs are filled here is unknown.
- `cfb32PolyFillRect` in `crm.so`: fills an array of rectangles on four-byte pixels; its stack frame is about 8464 bytes because it keeps a local buffer of clipped boxes, and it reads the fill style from bits 24 and 25 of the context word at offset 16, handing every style that is not plain solid or plain tiled to `miPolyFillRect` (three call sites), while for the two handled styles it clips each rectangle against the composite clip rectangles, using 111 half-word loads and 112 half-word stores to build the box list, and passes the list to the solid or tile box filler through the context's function pointer, 3756 bytes at offset 0x42c010; compared with `cfb/cfbfillrct.c`, the same two-way split and the same local box array are present; the digest shows 939 instructions and the 0x18 shift constant, while the clipping loops were not read in detail. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb32PolyGlyphBlt8` in `crm.so`: draws a string of bitmap glyphs, transparent, with the context's foreground colour on four-byte pixels; it takes the drawable, the context, a start position, a glyph count and an array of character-info pointers, and before painting it adds the drawable origin to the position, reads the font's first character metrics through the context's font pointer at offset 44 and walks the glyph array, falling to `cfbPolyGlyphBlt8Clipped..JL` (two call sites) when a glyph would leave the clip, and otherwise expanding glyph bits into pixel words by table lookup (3 read-only table references), 2376 bytes at offset 0x42cec0; the public counterpart is the glyph blitter in `cfb/cfbglblt8.c`, which has the same clipped fallback; the digest shows 594 instructions with 70 half-word loads and 64 word stores; the glyph expansion was not read through. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb32FillRectSolidCopy` in `crm.so`: fills boxes of a four-byte-pixel drawable by plain stores of one colour word; it takes the drawable, the context, the box count and the box array, reads the pitch (offset 28 shifted right by two) and base (offset 32) of the backing pixmap, takes the colour word from offset 12 of the context's private record, and runs a per-row store loop that appears to store eight words per pass (50 word stores), 764 bytes at offset 0x42d810; the plain-copy case of the solid rectangle fill in `cfb/cfbfillrct.c` has the same structure, and the digest shows 191 instructions and no calls; the unrolled loop was not read word by word. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfbZeroPolyArcSS8Copy` in `crm.so`: draws a list of one-pixel arcs for the copy raster operation on one-byte pixels, taking the drawable, the context, an arc count and an array of 12-byte arc records. Per arc it applies the same selection as the other raster-operation versions: a pair of sizes that differ with either above 800 goes to the generic poly-arc routine; a bounding box widened by one whose far edges stay within 32767 and lies fully inside the clip (the screen rectangle method at offset 380, answer one) goes to the one-byte copy arc routine; anything else goes to the generic zero-width arc routine. 464 bytes at offset 0x42db10; read in full against `cfb/cfbzerarc.c`, the copy build of the poly-arc function. Against the xor version the instruction sequence is identical apart from the address of the per-arc routine it loads (the copy clone with suffix FL), the order of two address loads and the position of one register save in the prologue; the general version has one more instruction, a register move in its generic-fallback branch. It has three call sites to generic routines or the screen method, and the fast arc routine is called indirectly through the saved address. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb32BitBlt` in `crm.so`: is the copy-area core for four-byte-pixel drawables; it takes source and destination drawables, the context, the source rectangle, the destination origin and, beyond the register arguments, a blit function and a plane value, unpacks the 16-bit coordinates from 64-bit argument registers with shift pairs (12 of each kind), skips a screen-level hook (a method at offset 176 of the screen record, not traced) when both drawables are the same or the hook is absent and otherwise calls it, gets the clipped source region through `NotClippedByChildren` (six calls), blits through ten indirect calls and reports uncovered areas with `miHandleExposures`, 2084 bytes at offset 0x431e10; the public `cfb/cfbbitblt.c` copy-area routine has the same flow, and the digest shows 521 instructions with a 288-byte frame; the middle part of the body was not read in full. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb32CopyArea` in `crm.so`: picks the copy, xor or or block transfer for a full plane mask and the general one otherwise, then runs the bit-block copy with that transfer, 156 bytes at offset 0x432640; read in full against the public sample-server source `cfb/cfbbitblt.c`, the same selection as the body, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb32DoBitbltCopy` in `crm.so`: copies rectangles between four-byte-pixel drawables with a plain word store; its arguments are the two drawables, the raster operation, the box region and the source point array, it takes the word pitch of each side from offset 28 of the drawable (shifted right by two) and the pixel base from offset 32, reads each box with half-word loads (30 of them), and chooses the walk direction so that overlapping source and destination are not clobbered, copying row by row with word stores, 2800 bytes at offset 0x4326e0; this is the copy build of the blitter in `cfb/cfbbitblt.c`, compared with the one-bit copy of the same job it has no variable shifts and only 2 indirect calls (against 4), and the digest shows 700 instructions and 80 word stores; the row loops were not read through. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb32ValidateGC` in `crm.so`: revalidates a graphics context for four-byte pixels after its state changed; it takes the context, a change mask and the drawable, recomputes the composite clip through `miComputeCompositeClip` (3 call sites) when the clip, subwindow mode or drawable origin changed, rebuilds the reduced raster-operation words, rotates a tile or stipple pixmap to the drawable's origin with `cfb32CopyRotatePixmap` when one is selected, picks the span, rectangle, line, arc and glyph function table entries by fill style and line style, and reports a fatal error on an impossible fill state, 3904 bytes at offset 0x4331d0; the public `cfb/cfbgc.c` function of the same role has the same decisions, and the digest agrees on the three composite-clip calls, two allocation calls, two block-move calls and the 32767/0x8000 constants; the table-selection branches (70 plain branches, 37 address-of-table loads) were not all traced. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfbCreatePixmap` in `crm.so`: allocates a pixmap of a given size and depth with its header, row pitch and data in one block on one-byte pixels, unprefixed build, 416 bytes at offset 0x437640; read in full against the public sample-server source `cfb/cfbpixmap.c`: the body reads the screen's twelve-byte format entry for the depth, forms the row size from the width and the entry's pad, shift and scale words, asks the allocator for a pixmap block of that size times the height (or takes one from the screen's cache when it is enabled), and fills the header: type byte 1, reference count 1, screen pointer at +0x10, width and height halfwords at +0xc and +0xe, depth at +0x2, bits per pixel at +0x3 from a floating-point division of the entry's words, pitch at +0x1c, a new serial number from the global counter at +0x14 and, for a non-empty pixmap, the data pointer at +0x20 just after the header. Established.
- `cfb8SegmentSS1RectCopy` in `crm.so`: draws solid one-pixel segments under a one-rectangle clip with the copy raster operation on one-byte pixels, returning the index of the first segment that touches the clip edge or minus one when every segment was drawn. The prologue picks up the drawable's pixmap through the window private slot (a screen-private lookup at offset 436, guarded by a negative index) as well as the screen pixmap, reads the foreground word from the context's private record, and walks the eight-byte segment records, testing each endpoint pair against the box and plotting interior segments with byte stores only, 32 of them, with no load of the old pixel; one jump table dispatches the plotting cases. 2532 bytes at offset 0x439a50. Compared with `cfb/cfb8line.c` (the one-rectangle segment routine in its copy build). It is the smallest of the three one-rectangle segment routines, 633 instructions against 762 for xor and 921 for the general build, because it needs no read-modify-write. It makes no calls. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, return convention and instruction counts were read; the plotting arms were not read through.
- `cfb8SegmentSS1Rect` in `crm.so`: draws solid one-pixel segments for a clip of one rectangle, skipping the per-box clip loop on one-byte pixels, explicit eight-bit build, 332 bytes at offset 0x43a440; read in full against the public sample-server source `cfb/cfb8line.c`: the body takes the private record from the context's private array and picks a clipping routine and a drawing routine from the global table by the record's first byte (3 selects one pair, 6 another and any other value a third), then loops: it calls the clipper on the points left, stops when it returns minus one or the count reaches zero, and calls the drawer with the last segment's four coordinate halfwords, the record's word at +0x8 and a flag that is set when bits 29 and 28 of the context's flag word at +0x10 are clear. Established.
- `cfbPolyFillRect` in `crm.so`: fills a list of rectangles, choosing the box-fill routine from the fill style and the raster operation: solid fills choose the copy, xor or general solid fill by the context's reduced operation; tiled fills choose the odd-width tile fill when there is no rotated tile pixmap and otherwise the 32-wide copy or general tile fill (copy only when the alu is copy and the plane mask is full); stippled and opaque-stippled fills choose the unnatural stipple routine when there is no rotated stipple and the 32-wide transparent or opaque routine otherwise. It then translates every rectangle by the drawable origin, clips each against the composite clip into a box list (a single clip box is handled inline; for several boxes the screen method at offset 396 is called once to fetch the clip's overall extents, then every rectangle is intersected with each clip box in an inline loop, in a stack buffer of up to 1024 boxes and a stack-allocated larger one otherwise), and finally calls the chosen routine through its pointer with the box count (the byte span between the list ends shifted right by 3, boxes being eight bytes) and the list. 3780 bytes at offset 0x43a590. Compared with `cfb/cfbfillrct.c` (the polyfillrect routine, built for the eight-bit pixel case that includes the stipple choices). Its nine routine names are address-taken (three solid, three tile, three stipple), the indirect call to the chosen one is at instruction 736, and the 112 halfword stores are the 16-bit box coordinates. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the dispatch list, the two calls and the coordinate stores were read; the clipping loops were not read through.
- `cfbValidateGC` in `crm.so`: revalidates the colour frame-buffer's graphics-context state for one-byte pixels after a client change, taking the context, a change mask and the drawable. It records the window origin in the context when it has moved, recomputes the composite clip through the shared clip routine when the client clip or the drawable's serial number has changed, copies and rotates the tile or stipple into the private rotated pixmap when the tile changes (an 84-byte private record is allocated, with the must-have-memory flag raised around the call, when none exists yet), switching four ways on the fill style and reporting a fatal error for any other value, and then picks the drawing methods in the operations table. The choices installed include the solid-span fillers for copy, xor and general, the tile and stipple span fillers, the rectangle, line, segment and arc routines, the eight-pixel glyph blits, and a push-pixels routine that falls back to the monochrome one; ones the code does not handle come from the shared fallbacks. 4436 bytes at offset 0x43b460. Compared with `cfb/cfbgc.c` (the validation function there). Evidence in the body: ten outgoing calls, the composite-clip routine at instruction 209, the two rotate-pixmap routines at 429 and 516, the allocator at 645 and the release call at 488, the fatal-error call at 347 (the bad-fill-style default), and the screen method at offset 240 used twice to change the drawable's clip; about 40 routine names are address-taken for the operations table. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the prologue, the call sites and the address list were read, not the selection logic between them.
- `cfbFillRectSolidCopy` in `crm.so`: fills a list of boxes with the foreground pixel using the copy raster operation on one-byte pixels, taking the drawable, the context, a box count and the eight-byte box records. The pitch (shifted right by two into words) and base come from the screen pixmap, and the foreground word is taken from offset 12 of the context's private record. For each box it forms the row address from the top edge, treats a one-pixel-wide box as a column of single byte stores, and otherwise forms a start mask and an end mask from tables in the data section indexed by the box's edge phase; the left word is read-modify-written under the start mask, whole words in between are stored with the replicated foreground, unrolled four to a row pass in the first arms, and the right word is merged under the end mask. 1948 bytes at offset 0x43c5c0; read against `cfb/cfbsolid.c` (the solid box routine built for copy), which the body follows. It differs from the xor and general siblings in storing the middle words with no load (109 word stores, 25 byte stores, no xor or and per whole word). It makes no calls. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: setup, mask tables and the first arms of the loops were read; the unrolled variants were compared by pattern only.
- `cfbPolyGlyphBlt8` in `crm.so`: draws transparent text for a one-byte-pixel destination, one eight-pixel-wide glyph at a time using the table-driven eight-pixel expansion. It first asks the screen's region-rectangle method at offset 380 how the string's overall box falls against the clip: fully outside returns, fully inside draws each glyph straight into the frame buffer with byte, halfword and word stores, and partly inside calls the clipped helper for each glyph (the static clone `cfbPolyGlyphBlt8Clipped..OK`, call at instruction 532). The character-info pointers come through a method at offset 4 of the font's glyph array (a call at instruction 519) for each glyph, and the foreground value is taken from the context. 2292 bytes at offset 0x44c260. Compared with `cfb/cfbglblt8.c` (the eight-pixel glyph routine with the table-driven path). It has three calls (the region method, the glyph-fetch method and the clipped helper), 32 byte, 21 halfword and 2 word stores for the glyph pixels, and two jump tables used for the glyph-width cases. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: setup, the three calls and the store mix were read; the unrolled glyph paths were not.
- `cfbFillPoly1RectCopy` in `crm.so`: fills a convex or general polygon, drawn with the copy raster operation, when the composite clip is a single rectangle, writing one-byte pixels. A previous-mode point list is handed on with a tail call to the generic polygon filler (the call loads its target from the global offset table at the end of the body), as is any polygon whose points stray outside the clip rectangle. Otherwise it packs each point's x and y into one word, finds the top vertex and the bottom row while testing every point against the clip bounds by OR-ing the subtractions, then walks two edges downward with integer stepping and error terms, and for every scanline stores the solid colour into a start-masked word, whole words, and an end-masked word. 4172 bytes at offset 0x44cb60. Compared with `cfb/cfbply1rct.c` (the same routine built for the copy raster operation, so no and/xor combination appears: the pixel word is stored as is). It makes no ordinary calls, and the only transfer out is the tail call, with the 32767 initial minimum-row sentinel and the 0x8000 sign fixup of the packed drawable origin both seen in the opening instructions; 44 word stores and no byte stores carry the pixels. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, the clip test, the tail call and the sentinels were read; the edge-walking and span-writing loops were not read to their ends.
- `cfbBitBlt` in `crm.so`: is the copy-area core for one-byte-pixel drawables: it takes the source and destination drawables, the context, the source rectangle, the destination origin, and on the stack the block-transfer routine to use and a bit-plane value, and returns the exposure region or null. It first calls the screen's source-validate hook (offset 176 of the screen record) when the two drawables differ and the hook exists, adds the source drawable origin, and then picks how to clip the source: a pixmap source is clipped by a fast box, and a window source uses the border-minus-children region through the library's not-clipped-by-children call, the composite clip when source and destination are the same, or the window's clip list. It builds the destination box in a stack region with the screen method at offset 340, then makes two calls through the method at offset 356 and one through the one at offset 376 to combine and shift it (the public source does this with region intersection and translation; the slot names were not checked against the screen layout), and calls the block-transfer routine through the pointer it was given, with the context's alu and plane-mask words. It releases the regions through the methods at offsets 352 and 348. When bit 0x80 of the first word of the context's private record is set (the graphics-exposures flag) it calls the exposure routine that sends events for the uncovered area, in a direct call at instruction 518. 2088 bytes at offset 0x4516c0; read against `cfb/cfbbitblt.c` (the copy-area core, whose same-named routine takes the same arguments) and compared with the existing reading of the four-byte version; the entry, the flag test, the clip choice and the region method slots were read, and the point-list build and the fast-clip paths were not read in full. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the middle of the body was not read in full.
- `cfbCopyArea` in `crm.so`: copies a rectangle between drawables, using the block transfer for same-depth copies and exposures for the rest on one-byte pixels, unprefixed build, 164 bytes at offset 0x45e740; read in full against the public sample-server source `cfb/cfbbitblt.c`: the body picks the block-copy routine from the raster op at +0x5 of the context and its plane mask at +0x14 (the plain copy routine for raster op 3, the exclusive-or one for 6, the general one when the mask is not all ones) and calls the common copy driver through the global table with that routine, a zero flag and its own stack argument. Established.
- `cfbDoBitbltCopy` in `crm.so`: copies pixel rectangles between one-byte-per-pixel drawables with the copy raster operation and a full plane mask, taking a destination region and one source point per box. For each box it chooses top-to-bottom or bottom-to-top and left-to-right or right-to-left so overlapping copies read their source first, and moves words with start and end masks and an unrolled middle, reading the pixel words and merging only under the masks at the ends (the word loop stores without combining). 4616 bytes at offset 0x45e7f0. Compared with `cfb/cfbbitblt.c` (the copy case that the public code selects when the raster operation is copy), with the inner copy loop from `cfb/cfbblt.c` inlined, so there are no calls. Its 116 word stores and 42 or instructions, against 68 word stores in the or and xor siblings, mark the plain-store middle; four jump tables dispatch within the body and their selector is unknown. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: structure, instruction mix and the comparison with its siblings were read; the unrolled loops were not.
- `cfbLineSS` in `crm.so`: draws a solid one-pixel polyline on one-byte pixels. Arguments are the drawable, the context, the coordinate mode (zero absolute, otherwise each point relative to the one before), the point count and an array of 16-bit x and y pairs. The pixmap is the screen record's field at offset 116 (pitch in bytes at pixmap offset 28, base at offset 32), or the drawable itself when its type byte is 1. The clip is the composite-clip region at offset 8 of the context's private record: its data pointer is at region offset 8, the box count at data offset 4 and the boxes from data offset 8, and a null data pointer means a single box, the region's own extents. Each segment is the standard thin line clipped to the boxes, and it stops one pixel short of its end so the next segment owns the joint. When the line passes exactly between two pixels, a per-screen word decides: it is the screen private slot numbered by `miZeroLineScreenIndex` (global at gp minus 26136; a negative index means word zero) and the bit tested is the octant number, 1 for y-major, 2 for y decreasing and 4 for x decreasing; a clear bit steps the minor coordinate on and a set bit holds it. The final point is painted unless the cap style (two bits at bit 28 of the context word at offset 16) is zero, and is also skipped when the polyline returns to its first point after more than two points. A pixel is stored as the private word at offset 12 when the raster-op byte at offset 0 is 3 (copy), otherwise as (old and the word at offset 16) xor the word at offset 12; the final point always goes through a word-wide read-modify-write with that pair. A horizontal run is written with whole-word stores (14 for the 48 pixels from x=3 to x=50, no byte stores); a vertical or sloped run is one byte store per pixel. 9276 bytes (2319 instruction slots) at offset 0x4668b0, no calls, 93 byte stores and 77 word stores, no floating point, 608-byte frame. Compared with cfb/cfbline.c and cfb/cfbhrzvert.c. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: the body ran in a MIPS interpreter on a 64 by 64 surface and matched a separately written reference pixel for pixel on 520 random polylines (coordinates from -6 to 70, one or four clip boxes, both coordinate modes, every cap style, random tie words); the store counts are from the listing.
- `cfbSegmentSS` in `crm.so`: draws a list of independent solid one-pixel segments on one-byte pixels. Arguments are the drawable, the context, the segment count and 8-byte records of two 16-bit points. Each segment is drawn from its own first point as the standard thin line clipped to the boxes and keeps its last pixel unless the cap style (two bits at bit 28 of the context word at offset 16) is zero; there is no coordinate mode, no previous point and no closing-point test, and a zero-length segment paints its single pixel unless the cap is zero. The tie word, pixmap, clip and pixel-write rules are those of the polyline routine; a negative zero-line screen index leaves the tie word at zero and does not abort. A horizontal run is 13 whole-word stores for x=3 to x=50, a vertical or sloped run one byte store per pixel. 8800 bytes (2200 slots, against 2319 for the polyline routine) at offset 0x468cf0, no calls, 93 byte stores and 76 word stores, no floating point, 576-byte frame. The segment build of cfb/cfbline.c is the public routine with this contract. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: 150 random segment lists (up to five segments, coordinates from -6 to 70, one or four boxes, two cap styles, random tie words) matched the reference pixel for pixel.
- `cfb8ClippedLineCopy` in `crm.so`: draws one clipped thin line segment inside a single box on one-byte pixels with the copy raster operation. It takes the drawable, the context, two end points (relative to the drawable, added to the 16-bit drawable origin at offsets 8 and 10), a pointer to a box of four 16-bit values (left, top, right, bottom, the last two exclusive) and a flag that leaves out the last pixel when nonzero. The pixels are the standard thin line clipped to the box; an exact tie between two pixels is decided by the per-screen octant word that the polyline drivers use (a negative screen index means word zero), and a pixmap drawable is its own pixmap. Its byte pixel step stores the context's xor word and never reads the old pixel; the and word is ignored. The byte copy routine is 4804 bytes (1201 instruction slots) at offset 0x46af50 with 64 byte stores, 106 movz and 70 movn, a 496-byte frame, four unsigned divides, no calls and no floating point. Compared with the copy build of cfb/cfb8line.c. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: 200 random byte plain copy segments (end points from -10 to 70, drawable origins from -3 to 3, boxes of random size, last-pixel flag set and clear, random tie words and background bytes) matched a separately written reference byte for byte.
- `cfb32SegmentSS` in `crm.so`: draws a list of independent solid one-pixel segments on four-byte pixels. Arguments, per-segment rules (last pixel kept unless the cap style is zero, no coordinate mode) and tie word are those of the one-byte segment routine; the context-private index is the global at gp minus 25920 and the pixmap comes through the screen private slot numbered by the global at gp minus 25916. Every pixel is one word store (48 for a 48-pixel horizontal or vertical run), 165 word stores in the body; raster-op byte 3 stores the xor word and any other value stores (old and the and word) xor the xor word. Clipping uses conditional moves, 96 movz and 102 movn, in place of branches; there are no calls and no floating point, and the frame is 560 bytes. 8624 bytes (2156 slots) at offset 0x46c220. cfb/cfbline.c in its segment build is the matching public routine. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: 150 random segment lists matched the reference pixel for pixel; store and conditional-move counts are from the listing.
- `cfbSolidSpansCopy` in `crm.so`: fills a list of horizontal spans with the foreground pixel using the copy raster operation on one-byte pixels, taking the drawable, the context, a span count, start points, widths and a sorted flag. It reads the foreground word from offset 12 of the context's private record, scans the clip region's boxes for the tallest band (an inlined loop whose result multiplies the span count to size the work arrays), takes the two arrays from the stack, and calls the generic span clipper once (instruction 87) to cut the spans down to the clip. For each clipped span it forms the row address from the pixmap pitch and base, then writes a short span (width at most four) as single bytes, and a longer one as a start-masked word read-modify-written from a table of end masks in the data section, a run of whole words stored with the foreground replicated, unrolled eight to a pass, and an end-masked word. 1124 bytes at offset 0x475090; read against `cfb/cfbsolid.c` (the solid span routine built for copy), which the body follows step for step, with the band scan inlined where the public file calls its max-band helper. The distinguishing counts are 33 byte stores, 27 word stores, one load of the partial word per end and no xor, against the xor sibling's 28 xor instructions. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: setup, the clip call and the first unrolled arms were read; later arms were compared by pattern only.
- `cfb8SetOpaqueStipple` in `crm.so`: loads the module's shared stipple state for an opaque stipple, taking the raster operation, foreground, background and plane mask. It stores the mode value 3 (opaque stippled) into the mode global, the raster operation, and each of foreground, background and plane mask masked to a byte into their own globals, then reduces the raster operation separately for the foreground and the background through two 16-entry jump tables (a raster operation above 15 skips the reduction), each deriving an and value and an xor value from the replicated byte (the byte copied to all four lanes by three shift-and-or steps, folded with the mask). When the two reduced operations agree that operation is stored in the reduced-operation global, and when they differ the value for set is used instead. It then builds the sixteen entries of the and and xor tables, one pair per nibble pattern of the stipple: each and entry is the foreground and value or'd with the complement of the pattern mask, all and'ed with the background and value or'd with the pattern mask, and each xor entry the foreground xor under the mask or'd with the background xor under the complement, unrolled so that each pass writes four word pairs at the table offsets. 1508 bytes at offset 0x476700; read in full against `cfb/cfb8bit.c` (the opaque-stipple setup, which has the same state variables, the two reductions, the equality test and the mask loop), with the loop unrolling and reduction switches inlined here. It makes no calls and touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfbCopyPlane1to8` in `crm.so`: expands a one-bit-deep source bitmap into an eight-bit destination, for each box in the destination region reading the source bits and writing the colour that the active stipple state gives to a set bit and to a clear bit (four words are taken from a table row chosen by the cached stipple raster operation; the caller must have set that state up beforehand). Source and destination bases and word pitches come from the drawable's pixmap, or the screen's pixmap for a window, and the pitch is shifted right by two to count words. Each source row is converted a nibble at a time, four pixels packed into each destination word, with start and end partial words merged under masks. 4620 bytes at offset 0x476cf0. Compared with `cfb/cfbbitblt.c`, where the public routine of this name does the same expansion for the eight-bit build. There are no calls, one jump table whose selector is unknown, 62 word stores for the destination words and 83 doubleword spills. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the setup and table fetch were read; the conversion loops were not.
- `cfbCopyPlane` in `crm.so`: copies a plane of a source drawable into a colour destination, expanding set bits to the foreground colour on one-byte pixels, unprefixed build, 1200 bytes at offset 0x477f00; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfbResolveColor` in `crm.so`: rounds a requested red, green and blue (each an unsigned halfword passed by address) to the nearest value the visual can represent, writing the three results back. It reads the visual's class from the halfword at offset 4 and branches. A true-colour or static-colour class scales each channel through that channel's own mask: the value is multiplied by the channel's top code plus one, shifted right by 16 to give a channel code, widened to 16 bits by multiplying by 65535 and dividing by the channel's top code, then reduced to the visual's bits per colour value and widened again by dividing through that value's limit. A pseudo-colour or direct-colour class does the plain per-channel step, shift right then multiply by 65535 and divide by the limit. The static-grey class forms the weighted grey (a divide by one hundred done as a multiply by the reciprocal, with weights thirty, fifty-nine and eleven), quantises it first to the number of colormap entries and then to the bits-per-value limit, and stores it in all three channels; the grey-scale class forms the same grey and quantises it only by the bits-per-value limit. 740 bytes at offset 0x4783b0; read in full against `mi/micmap.c`, whose single resolver this routine inlines, but the class split differs from the public body: that one treats both grey classes alike with the bit-width limit and has no mask-scaled true-colour branch. The routine makes no calls; the teq instructions are divide-by-zero guards. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfbPutImage` in `crm.so`: writes a client image into the drawable, taking the context, the depth, destination origin, size, left pad, format and image pointer. It returns for an empty rectangle. For the packed and bitmap formats it wraps the image in a scratch pixmap header (the row size and bits per pixel come from the depth-indexed padding table, as a floating divide of the padded bit count by the pixel size), clears the expose flag, which is bit 0x80 of the first word of the context's private record, calls the context's copy-area method (slot at offset 12 of the operations table) for packed pixels or its copy-plane method (offset 16, with plane 1) for a bitmap, restores the flag by setting that bit and releases the header. For the plane-separated format it saves the foreground, background and plane mask, forces foreground to all ones and background to zero by the change-context call, then for each bit from the top of the depth down that is set in the saved plane mask it changes the plane mask, revalidates the context and calls the context's own put-image method (offset 8 of the operations table) as a one-bit bitmap, stepping the image pointer by one plane's size for every bit position, and finally restores the three saved values with the change-context call (no validate follows it). 808 bytes at offset 0x4786a0; read in full against `cfb/cfbimage.c`, the same sequence as the body. Evidence: three change-context calls (masks 12 for the colours, 2 for the plane mask and 14 for all three) and one validate call, inside the per-plane loop. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb32CopyPlane` in `crm.so`: forwards its arguments to the routine held in the global `cfbPuntCopyPlane`, 72 bytes at offset 0x482eb0; read in full, it belongs to the SGI display code and differs from the body, which does the plane copy itself; the forwarding hook is not in the public source, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb32UnnaturalStippleFS` in `crm.so`: fills spans with a stipple whose width is not a word divisor, bit by bit on four-byte pixels, 2484 bytes at offset 0x482f00; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb32PutImage` in `crm.so`: writes client pixels into a four-byte-pixel drawable, taking the drawable, the context, depth, destination, size, left pad, format and data pointer; it returns for a zero width or height, and for the packed and bitmap formats wraps the data in a scratch pixmap header (the row pad from the 12-byte-per-depth padding table, the bits-per-pixel computed in floating point), clears bit 0x80 of the first word of the context's private record around the call, and uses the area-copy method for packed pixels and the copy-plane method with plane 1 for bitmaps; for the plane-by-plane format (1) it saves the context's plane mask, foreground and background, sets foreground to all ones and background to zero through the change-context routine (mask 12), then for each plane bit from the top of the depth down that the saved plane mask contains, sets the plane mask (mask 2), revalidates through the validate routine, calls the drawable's own put-image method with the one-bit bitmap format and advances the data by the plane size, and finally restores all three (mask 14), 808 bytes at offset 0x4838c0; read in full against `cfb/cfbimage.c`, whose two branches it follows, with the call counts of three scratch-header, three change-context and two validate calls matching the digest. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb16CopyPlane` in `crm.so`: forwards its arguments to the routine held in the global `cfbPuntCopyPlane`, 72 bytes at offset 0x487150; read in full, it belongs to the SGI display code and differs from the body, which does the plane copy itself; the forwarding hook is not in the public source, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb16UnnaturalStippleFS` in `crm.so`: fills spans with a stipple whose width is not a word divisor, bit by bit on two-byte pixels, 4548 bytes at offset 0x4871a0; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb16ValidateGC` in `crm.so`: brings a 16-bit-per-pixel context up to date after its state changed, choosing which drawing routines the context will use. Its inputs are the context, the changed-state mask and the drawable. It records the window origin in the context (halfwords in the record) and notes whether it moved, recomputes the composite clip through the machine-independent routine when the mask has any clip origin, clip mask or subwindow-mode bit (0xe8000) or the drawable's serial number differs from the context's low 31 bits, and records whether the clip has a single rectangle. It then walks the changed bits to flag what needs rebuilding: the reduced raster operation with its and and xor words, line, text, span-fill and area-fill choices, and the rotated tile when the tile width is a power of two of at most 32 bits, using the rotate routine, plus an inlined pixmap copy and pad for stipples, with the allocator, free and move routines. 4000 bytes at offset 0x488370. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbgc.c, the disassembly shows calls to the clip routine, the rotate routine, the allocator, its free, the move routine and the fatal-error call, and address loads of the wide-line and image-glyph helpers. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the opening, clip step and tile logic were read, and the selection of each operation table was matched to the source by the callees it names.
- `cfb16PutImage` in `crm.so`: writes client pixel data into a drawable for the 16-bit frame buffer. The inputs are the drawable, context, depth, destination corner, size, left padding, format and the data. It returns at once for zero width or height. For the packed-pixel and bitmap formats it wraps the data in a scratch pixmap header (pitch from the padding table, with a floating-point divide in the width computation), clears the expose bit 0x80 in the context's private word, calls copy-area (slot 12) for packed pixels or copy-plane (slot 16 with plane 1) otherwise, and sets the bit again. For the plane-separated format it saves foreground, background and plane mask, sets foreground to all ones and background to zero, and loops from the top bit of the depth down, changing the plane mask (constant 2), revalidating, and calling slot 8 with a bitmap per plane whose stride is the height times the 32-bit-padded width. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbimage.c, the assembly shows three context-change calls (for the colours, for the plane mask and for all three together), one validate call, the scratch-header acquire and release and the expose-bit handling inside the private area. The plane loop visits only the bits that are set in the saved plane mask, though the data pointer steps one plane for every bit position. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfbAllocatePrivates` in `crm.so`: reserves the colour frame buffer's private slots on a screen, 276 bytes at offset 0x489d10; the public sample-server source `cfb/cfbscrinit.c` holds the same routine, and the body read in full matches its allocation order. When the module's two index words are still unset it first calls `mfbAllocatePrivates` to take both a window and a graphics context private; the results are kept in the module's words and reused on later screens. It then asks for a window private of 16 bytes with `AllocateWindowPrivate` and a context private of 20 bytes with the context-private allocator, returns zero when either fails, stores the address of `miCopyPlane` in a module-level hook word and returns one. - `cfb32AllocatePrivates` in `crm.so`: the 32-bit pixel copy of the colour frame buffer's private allocator, 300 bytes at offset 0x489e30; the public sample-server source `cfb/cfbscrinit.c` holds the routine, compiled here for 32-bit pixels, and the body read in full matches it. Like the plain routine it takes both slots from `mfbAllocatePrivates` only when the module's index words are unset, keeps its own copies of the two indexes in separate module words, and requests a window private of 16 bytes and a context private of 20 bytes, failing with zero when either allocation fails. - `cfb16AllocatePrivates` in `crm.so`: registers the colour-frame-buffer window and graphics-context private areas for one screen. It takes only a screen: the two optional index pointers of the public routine are inlined here as fixed addresses of this module's own index variables, and the argument registers a1 and a2 are overwritten without being read. When the pair is unset (or a pointer is null) it first asks the monochrome allocator for fresh indexes and copies them into those variables, otherwise it adopts the existing pair as the two global indexes. It then reserves a 16-byte per-window area and a 20-byte per-GC area, and finally obtains a screen-private slot and stores it. The routine returns 1 on success and 0 when any allocation fails. - `cfb8LineSS1RectGeneral` in `crm.so`: draws a solid one-pixel polyline on one-byte pixels with the general raster operation while every segment lies inside one clip box. Its arguments are the drawable, the context, the coordinate mode, the point count, the point array, the original array pointer and four result pointers (the last two on the stack); only mode zero, absolute points, is in its contract, and the previous-point variants are separate routines. The box is the clip region's own extents (a multi-box region counts as its bounding box), moved by the 16-bit drawable origin. The routine draws segments while both end points lie inside the box and returns minus one when it reached the end of the list (painting the last point unless the cap style is zero, or the polyline closes on its first point after more than two points). Otherwise it returns the index of the first point that lies outside, having drawn every earlier segment without its last pixel, and returns 1 at once when the first point is outside; it writes nothing through the result pointers in absolute mode, so the clipped segment must be finished by the caller. Its byte pixel step loads the old pixel, ands it with the context's and word, xors the xor word in and stores the result. The byte general routine is 3372 bytes (843 instruction slots) at offset 0x49a460 with 28 byte stores and 29 byte loads, 6 word stores, 41 movz, 31 movn, a 432-byte frame, no calls and no floating point. The public one-rectangle polyline routine of cfb/cfb8line.c in its general build is the counterpart. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution (the earlier claims that it handles relative coordinates and returns the last drawn end point, and the count of 16 word stores, were wrong; the listing has 6): 250 random byte general-operation polylines (points from -10 to 74, drawable origins from -3 to 3, boxes of random size, two cap styles, random tie words and background) matched a separately written reference in return value and pixels, and 40 more with random and and xor words matched the per-pixel rule.

The window origin is added to both endpoints, each endpoint is tested against the box with the packed 0x8000 trick, and the line is walked with Bresenham steps, a half-word store of the xor value per pixel. It has 32 half-word stores in 680 instructions and does not read the destination. 2720 bytes at offset 0x4a63b0. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfb8line.c, the assembly makes no calls, loads one global data word, and has the bounds constant 0x8000. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the structure and counts were read, the octant bodies were not traced.

Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfb8line.c, the assembly has the bounds constant 0x8000, no external calls, and 28 half-word loads matched to 28 stores. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: only the mix and entry code were read.
- `cfb8SegmentSS1RectGeneral` in `crm.so`: draws solid one-pixel segments under a one-rectangle clip with an arbitrary raster operation on one-byte pixels, and returns the number of segments it drew, or minus one when every segment fitted. Compared with `cfb/cfb8line.c` (the one-rectangle segment routine for the general raster operation). It has no calls, 28 byte stores and 28 word stores (the word stores are horizontal runs), and for the copy sibling `cfb8SegmentSS1RectCopy` the body is much smaller (633 instructions against 921) because that one needs no read-modify-write. Unless the two drawables are the same it calls the screen's source-validation slot (offset 176) of the source's screen, then shifts the source rectangle by the source window origin, forms the destination box in destination coordinates, clips it with the composite clip, obtains the not-clipped-by-children region for either drawable in include-inferiors mode, intersects, builds the source corners and calls the callback with the region. If the context asks for exposures it passes the result to the handle-exposures routine and returns what it makes. 2084 bytes at offset 0x4aba20. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbbitblt.c, the disassembly has six references to the clipped-by-children helper, one handle-exposures call, 17 indirect calls and the 0xffff mask for packing coordinates. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the entry sequence and call structure were read, the middle clipping blocks were not.

- `cfb16DoBitbltCopy` in `crm.so`: copies rectangles between two 16-bit drawables in copy mode, walking the destination region box by box. Its inputs are source and destination drawables, the raster operation, the destination region, an array of source corners and the plane mask. It reads each drawable's long-word pitch and base address from the screen-private pixmap record (the pitch and base fields), decides the vertical and horizontal walking direction when the source and destination coincide or are both windows, and reorders the boxes into temporary arrays when needed. Each line is moved with start and end masks and a run of whole words, and an unaligned source is combined from two words with a shift pair (35 variable shifts), so the destination is written with plain word stores without being read in the middle of the line. 4652 bytes at offset 0x4ac3a0. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbblt.c built with the copy rop; the disassembly makes no calls, with 116 word stores and the constant 0x10 for the pixel shift. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the setup, direction choice and the shape of the loops were read, the unrolled word loops were not.

The arguments are as in the single-operation blitters, but the routine receives the and and xor values derived from the raster operation and mask, and every destination word is combined as (destination and a mask) xor value, with 224 and-instructions and 205 xor-instructions in the 1562 instructions. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbblt.c built for the general merge, the assembly has the 111 doubleword spills of its larger register set. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: counts and constants were read, the loops were matched by structure.
- `cfbDoBitbltGeneral` in `crm.so`: copies pixel rectangles between drawables of one-byte pixels under an arbitrary raster operation and plane mask, taking a list of destination boxes and one source point per box. It builds the four merge-rop words from the plane mask (the mask byte replicated into a word) and the raster operation's table row, chooses between a left-to-right and right-to-left direction, and a top-to-bottom or bottom-to-top order when the source and destination overlap, then for each box moves whole words with edge masks, and the body dispatches through three jump tables whose selector is unknown. 6232 bytes at offset 0x4afba0. Compared with `cfb/cfbbitblt.c` and `cfb/cfbblt.c`, where the public code reaches this through its general wrapper and calls the block-copy loop; here that loop is inlined (no callee in the body, 68 word stores and 12 halfword stores for edges). The sibling copy-only, or-only and xor-only routines specialise the per-word step, and this general one uses the merge-rop table lookups in the prologue instead. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: setup, merge words and direction logic were read; the unrolled copy loops were not.

- `cfbDoBitbltOr` in `crm.so`: copies pixel rectangles between one-byte-per-pixel drawables with the or raster operation and a full plane mask, taking a destination region and a source point per box. Compared with `cfb/cfbbitblt.c` and the loop in `cfb/cfbblt.c`, which the public code reaches when the raster operation is or. It has no calls, four jump tables with an unknown selector and a single xor, which is in the edge-mask handling. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: structure and the per-word shape were read, not each unrolled arm.

- `cfbDoBitbltXor` in `crm.so`: copies pixel rectangles between one-byte-per-pixel drawables with the xor raster operation and a full plane mask, driven by a destination region and one source point per box. - `cfb16PolyFillArcSolidCopy` in `crm.so`: fills a list of arcs with a solid colour in copy mode on a 16-bit drawable. It takes the drawable, context, arc count and arcs. Each arc is skipped when empty and otherwise tested for whether its box, limited to 32767, lies wholly inside the composite clip; if so a full ellipse (an angle span of a whole circle) or a slice, set up by the slice-setup routine, is filled inline by stepping the two boundaries in integer arithmetic and writing masked words (234 word stores), and if not, that single arc goes to the generic filler. 5732 bytes at offset 0x4b6ee0. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbfillarc.c, the last two being inlined here; the disassembly has two references to the generic filler, one to the slice setup, three indirect calls in all, and the constant 32767. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: only the call structure and counts were read.
- `cfb32PolyFillArcSolidCopy` in `crm.so`: fills arcs in four-byte pixels by storing the context's colour word straight into the destination spans; it reads the arc array in 12-byte records, computes each ellipse's left and right edge per scan line from the slice setup, and gives an arc to `miPolyFillArc` one at a time when it is not handled by the span code, 4536 bytes at offset 0x4b8550; the structure matches `cfb/cfbfillarc.c` with the copy merge, since the digest shows 218 word stores and no and/xor pairs, in contrast with the general build of the same routine that has 208 pairs, and also two calls to the generic filler, one to `miFillArcSliceSetup`, and 1134 instructions; the span loops were not read word by word. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb32PolyFillArcSolidGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills arcs in four-byte pixels with an arbitrary raster operation, combining the context's stored and and xor words with the destination (208 and and 208 xor instructions, one pair per output word); it reads the private record through the context's private-pointer slot, then walks the arc array in 12-byte records and passes each arc that needs the generic splitter to `miPolyFillArc` with a count of one, 9364 bytes at offset 0x4b9710; compared with `cfb/cfbfillarc.c` the control flow is the same slice-or-full-ellipse split (one call to `miFillArcSliceSetup`, two calls to `miPolyFillArc`), and it differs from the copy build below by using the and/xor merge where the copy build stores the colour word directly; a 1056-byte frame and 2341 instructions; the span loops were not read through. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfbPolyFillArcSolidGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills a list of arcs or ellipses with a solid colour for the general raster-operation case on one-byte pixels. It walks the 12-byte arc records (x, y, width, height, two angles), skips any with a zero width or height, and sends an arc through the generic slow routine, called through a one-arc list, when either dimension is above 800 or the arc's box is not wholly inside the clip. For a clipped-in full ellipse (the angle span tested against 23040, which is 360 degrees in sixty-fourths) it steps the midpoint error terms and fills the left and right spans of each row pair with start and end masks; a partial arc is first reduced by the slice-setup routine and then filled the same way, with the and/xor pair applied under the masks. 10844 bytes at offset 0x4bbbb0. It was read against the public sample-server source `cfb/cfbfillarc.c` (the general raster-operation variant). Evidence in the body is the call to the screen's rectangle-containment method at offset 380 for each arc, two calls to the generic fill routine and one to the slice setup, plus 234 word stores and no byte stores, so pixels go out four at a time. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the arc loop, the size and containment tests and the callees were read, while the span-filling inner loops were not.
- `cfb16PolyFillArcSolidGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills a list of arcs with a solid colour using any raster operation on a 16-bit drawable. The per-arc checks, the single-arc fallback and the slice setup are those of the copy-mode filler, with the same generic-filler and slice-setup references, but every span word is merged as (word and the and value) xor the xor value, with 254 and-instructions, 224 xor-instructions and 234 stores in 2719 instructions, nearly twice the copy form's 1433. 10876 bytes at offset 0x4be610. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbfillarc.c, the assembly has the constant 0xf. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: counts were read, the loops were not.
- `cfbFillRectTileOdd` in `crm.so`: fills boxes with a tile of arbitrary width by choosing the whole-word or the odd-width code path on one-byte pixels, unprefixed build, 208 bytes at offset 0x4c1090; read in full against the public sample-server source `cfb/cfbfillrct.c`: the body tests the low two bits of the tile width halfword at +0xc of the tile record and, when they are clear, takes the word-wide path, otherwise the odd-width one, then chooses between the copy, exclusive-or and general routines from the raster op and a full plane mask before it calls the chosen routine with the box origin moved by the context's halfwords at +0x28 and +0x2a. Established.
- `cfb16PolyFillRect` in `crm.so`: fills a list of rectangles with the context's fill style on a 16-bit drawable. It takes the drawable, context, rectangle count and rectangles. Stippled and opaque-stippled styles (the two highest fill-style codes of the field at bit 24 of the context word) are handed back to the generic machine-independent fill; solid fill picks the copy, xor or general box filler by the raster-op byte of the private record, tiled fill picks the odd-width or the 32-bit filler. It adds the window origin to every rectangle in a four-way unrolled loop, clips each rectangle against either the single clip rectangle or the clip's extents, writing surviving boxes into a stack array of 1024 boxes (the 8464-byte frame) or a heap array, and calls the filler. 3744 bytes at offset 0x4c1160. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbfillrct.c, the disassembly loads the generic filler's address on two paths, and has 112 half-word stores of the clipped coordinates, and the style test on the two high bits at bit 24. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the dispatch, origin add and frame size were read, the clip loops were not.
- `cfb32FillRectTileOdd` in `crm.so`: fills boxes with the tile through the one-word tile box fill, in its copy form for the copy operation with a full plane mask and its general form otherwise, from the drawable and pattern origins, 140 bytes at offset 0x4c2000; read in full against the public sample-server source `cfb/cfbfillrct.c`, the same selection as the body for four-byte pixels, where the odd-width branch cannot occur, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb16FillRectTileOdd` in `crm.so`: fills boxes with a tile of arbitrary width by choosing the whole-word or the odd-width code path on two-byte pixels, 208 bytes at offset 0x4c2090; read in full against the public sample-server source `cfb/cfbfillrct.c`: the body is the sixteen-bit form of the same choice, testing only the lowest bit of the tile width halfword at +0xc and comparing the plane mask with 0xffff, then calling the chosen routine with the origin moved by the halfwords at +0x28 and +0x2a. Established.
- `cfb16UnnaturalTileFS` in `crm.so`: fills spans from a tile whose width is not a word divisor, pixel by pixel on two-byte pixels, 564 bytes at offset 0x4c2160; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb32UnnaturalTileFS` in `crm.so`: fills spans from a tile whose width is not a word divisor, pixel by pixel on four-byte pixels, 496 bytes at offset 0x4c23a0; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb8Stipple32FS` in `crm.so`: fills spans with a 32-pixel-wide stipple as foreground colour only (set bits drawn, clear bits left alone) on one-byte pixels. It first checks a cached stipple mode, and when the cache is not already the transparent kind it stores the new mode, the foreground byte, the plane-mask byte and the raster operation into the module's globals while a 16-way switch builds the matching and/xor pairs. It then lets the generic span clipper trim the spans to the composite clip, and for each span it fetches the stipple word rotated to the span's x and y phase and writes four pixels per stipple nibble as the public routine does; the body also dispatches through four jump tables, whose exact selector is unknown, and its many byte and halfword stores are probably the partial-word edges. 9564 bytes at offset 0x4c2590. Compared with `cfb/cfbfillsp.c` (the thirty-two-bit stipple span filler); the table-driven nibble expansion is inlined here where the public source works from per-nibble mask arrays, and the single callee is the span clipper at instruction 177, while the setup is the public stipple-mode helper expanded inline. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: setup and call sites were read, not the 2391-instruction span loops.
- `cfbUnnaturalTileFS` in `crm.so`: fills spans with a tile whose width is not a divisor of the word, taking the drawable, the context, a span count, start points, widths and a sorted flag, on one-byte pixels. It returns when the plane mask is zero, picks one of the four tile span routines by tile width (the odd-width pair when the low two bits of the tile's width are nonzero, else the whole-word pair) and by whether the alu is copy and the plane mask full, scans the clip region's boxes to find the tallest band (the inlined max-band loop, whose result is multiplied by the span count), allocates the clipped point and width arrays on the stack, clips with the generic span clipper, and calls the chosen routine through its pointer with the clipped spans, the tile, the pattern origin added to the drawable origin, the alu and the plane mask. 560 bytes at offset 0x4c4af0; read in full against `cfb/cfbfillsp.c` (the tile span function of that name), the same steps as the body, except that the public source calls the band-height helper where this one has it inlined. Its only outgoing call is the span clipper at instruction 103, plus the indirect fill call at 120. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb8OpaqueStipple32FS` in `crm.so`: fills spans with a 32-pixel-wide stipple in the opaque style on one-byte pixels, taking the context, a span count, point and width arrays and a sorted flag. It calls the opaque-stipple setup routine (instruction 38) to load the foreground, background, raster operation and plane mask into the module's globals and its and/xor tables, then passes the spans to the generic span clipper (instruction 97) with a stack-allocated work area for the clipped output, and for each surviving span takes the stipple word for the span's row, rotates it to the span's x phase, and writes a left partial word, whole words and a right partial word, each assembled from the per-nibble table words. 3824 bytes at offset 0x4c4d20.
