It differs from the transparent span filler `cfb8Stipple32FS` in having no byte or halfword stores and no jump tables (97 word stores in 956 instructions, against that routine's 2391 instructions with 536 byte stores), because every pixel of a word is written. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the callee sites, the store shape and the comparison were read; the span loop was not.
- `cfbUnnaturalStippleFS` in `crm.so`: fills spans with a stipple whose width does not divide the 32-bit word, for the opaque and the transparent case on one-byte pixels. It tests two bits of a packed context word (the fill style in the public layout), selects the transparent or opaque path from the cached stipple mode, calls the opaque-stipple setup with the raster operation, foreground, background and plane mask when the cache is stale, and then clips the spans with the generic clipper and paints each, walking the stipple row bit by bit across the odd width. One path reports a fatal error when the remaining-bits bookkeeping goes wrong. 6032 bytes at offset 0x4c5c10. Compared with `cfb/cfbfillsp.c` (the unnatural-stipple span function, which is the 8-bit variant). Evidence is three callees at instructions 37 (the setup), 105 (the clipper) and 357 (the fatal-error reporter), one jump table, and 41 word and 69 doubleword stores. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, mode logic and callees were read; the bit-walking inner loops were not.
- `cfbGetSpans` in `crm.so`: copies rows of pixels out of a drawable into a caller's buffer at a list of spans, on one-byte pixels. It switches on the drawable's bits per pixel (the byte at offset 3): a value of one goes to the bitmap routine, eight continues, and anything else reports a fatal error. For each span it reads the start point and width, clamps the width to the drawable, finds the first source word and the bit position of the start, and copies words with funnel shifts so the output rows are word aligned, merging the last partial word, and advancing the destination buffer by the padded row size. 2500 bytes at offset 0x4c73a0. Compared with `cfb/cfbgetsp.c` (the same-named routine). The one call is the fatal-error routine at instruction 24, and the 33 word stores are the output words; the funnel shifts and the clamp are taken from the public source and from the shift and compare instructions seen in the body. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the depth switch and the fatal-error call were read; the copy loop was not read to its end.
- `cfb32GetSpans` in `crm.so`: reads scan-line spans of a drawable into a packed destination for four-byte pixels; it takes the drawable, a maximum width, the span start points, the span widths, a span count and the destination, reads the bits-per-pixel byte (offset 3) and reports a fatal error unless it is 1 or 32, then for each span copies the pixels word by word with an alignment shift (24 and-masks, 14 or-merges) from the row base given by the pixmap pitch, 1676 bytes at offset 0x4c7d70; the public `cfb/cfbgetsp.c` routine also dispatches on pixel width and fails on an unsupported one, as the digest agrees (one fatal-error call, 419 instructions); the copy loops were not read word by word. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb16GetSpans` in `crm.so`: copies rows of 16-bit pixels from a drawable into a packed buffer. The inputs are the drawable, maximum width, start points, widths, the span count and the destination. One-bit drawables go to the monochrome version and any other depth than 16 stops with the server's fatal error call. The long-word pitch and base come from the pitch and base fields of the pixmap record reached through the screen-private index, and a one-span, one-pixel request is answered with a branch on a count of exactly one. For each span the end is clipped to the pitch, the source word and the starting pixel (x bit 0) are found, and the bits are moved with a start mask, a run of whole words and an end mask taken from tables in the read-only data, with separate handling when the span fits in one word. 2516 bytes at offset 0x4c8400. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbgetsp.c, the assembly has the fatal-error call, the one-bit and 16-bit depth tests and mask table loads at the same offsets as the 16-bit mask tables. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the dispatch and head were read, the unrolled word loops were not traced.
- `cfb16PolyGlyphBlt8` in `crm.so`: draws a run of font glyph bitmaps with the foreground colour onto a 16-bit drawable. The arguments are the drawable, context, text position, glyph count, an array of character records and the glyph bit storage. It adds the window origin, builds a covering bounding box from the first glyph's left bearing, the last glyph's right bearing and the character widths, and compares it with the clip: a box inside the clip is drawn directly with half-word pixel stores (41 of them in the body), and any other case is handed to the clipped variant, whose address is loaded five times. 2308 bytes at offset 0x4c8de0. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbglblt8.c, the disassembly shows three indirect calls and 70 half-word loads of the glyph metrics, plus the constant 0xf. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the box computation and call structure were read, the inner glyph loops were not.
- `cfbPolyGlyphRop8` in `crm.so`: draws transparent text with a raster operation other than copy, glyph by glyph on one-byte pixels, unprefixed build, 2836 bytes at offset 0x4c96f0; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb16ImageGlyphBlt8` in `crm.so`: draws opaque text by filling the extents of the glyph run with the background pixel and then drawing the glyphs with the context's glyph method, on two-byte pixels, 356 bytes at offset 0x4ca210; read in full against the public sample-server source `cfb/cfbigblt8.c`: the body takes the same steps as the one-byte routine, but the pixel it stores in the private record's word at +0xc is replicated into both halfwords instead of all four bytes. Established.
- `cfbImageGlyphBlt8` in `crm.so`: draws opaque text by filling the extents of the glyph run with the background pixel and then drawing the glyphs with the context's glyph method, on one-byte pixels, unprefixed build, 388 bytes at offset 0x4ca380; read in full against the public sample-server source `cfb/cfbigblt8.c`: the body asks the glyph-extents routine for the run's extents, builds a one-rectangle fill from the pen position and the font's halfwords at +0x44 and +0x46, swaps the foreground pixel at +0x18 of the context for the background at +0x1c and writes that byte replicated into all four bytes of the word at +0xc of the private record, calls the fill method at slot +0x2c of the context's ops, puts the foreground and its replicated word back and calls the glyph method at slot +0x48 with the original arguments. Established.
- `cfb32ImageGlyphBlt8` in `crm.so`: draws opaque text by filling the extents of the glyph run with the background pixel and then drawing the glyphs with the context's glyph method, on four-byte pixels, 332 bytes at offset 0x4ca510; read in full against the public sample-server source `cfb/cfbigblt8.c`: the body takes the same steps as the one-byte routine, but the pixel word is stored in the private record's word at +0xc as it is, with no replication. Established.
- `cfb32GetImage` in `crm.so`: reads a rectangle of a drawable into a client buffer, taking the drawable, the source origin, the width and height, the format, the plane mask and the destination pointer; it returns at once for a zero width or height, hands drawables whose bits-per-pixel byte (offset 3) is one to `mfbGetImage`, sends every format other than 2 (the packed-pixel format) to the generic image fetcher, and for packed pixels makes a scratch pixmap header over the buffer with the row pad taken from a 12-byte-per-depth padding table, clears the buffer with the block-clear routine unless the plane mask is all ones, builds a one-box region and runs `cfb32DoBitblt` with the copy operation and the plane mask, then releases the region and the header, 412 bytes at offset 0x4ca660; read in full against `cfb/cfbimage.c`, from which it differs in that no drawable-enabled test appears and the non-packed formats go to the generic fetcher, the case taken in the big-endian branch of the public source. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfbGetImage` in `crm.so`: reads a rectangle of pixels out of a drawable into a client buffer, taking the drawable, the source origin, the width and height, the format, the plane mask and the destination pointer. It returns at once for an empty rectangle, hands one-bit drawables to the bitmap routine, and otherwise wraps the client buffer in a scratch pixmap header obtained from the screen. For the packed-pixel format the row pad comes from the depth-indexed padding table, the buffer is cleared first when the low byte of the plane mask is not full, a one-box region for the width and height is built with the screen's region method at offset 340, and the general block-transfer routine copies from the drawable with the copy operation. For the bitmap formats the header is one bit deep with a row size of the width rounded up to 32 bits divided into bytes, and the plane-extraction routine fills it. The region is released through the method at offset 352 and the header handed back. 580 bytes at offset 0x4ca800; read in full against `cfb/cfbimage.c` (the get-image routine); there is no screen-enabled test as in the public source. Evidence: two scratch-header acquisitions with two releases, two region-method pairs, one bzero and calls to the two blit routines. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb16GetImage` in `crm.so`: reads a rectangle of a drawable into the client's buffer for the 16-bit-per-pixel frame buffer. Its arguments are the drawable, source corner, size, format, plane mask and destination bytes. A zero width or height returns at once; a one-bit-per-pixel drawable goes to the monochrome reader and any format other than the packed-pixel one goes to the generic machine-independent reader, since the plane-copy branch of the public source is absent from this build. For the packed-pixel format it wraps the buffer in a scratch pixmap header whose pitch comes from the depth-indexed padding table, clears the buffer first when the plane mask is not 0xffff, builds a one-box region at the destination, and copies from the drawable's screen position with the 16-bit bit-blit in copy mode. 416 bytes at offset 0x4caa50. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbimage.c, the disassembly lacks the drawable-enabled test, and shows one clear call, one region slot call (340) and the final teardown through slot 352. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb16LineSD` in `crm.so`: draws a dashed one-pixel polyline on two-byte pixels. It is the one-byte dashed routine `cfbLineSD` for halfword pixels, with the context index at the global at gp minus 25908 and the pixmap through the screen private slot at the global at gp minus 25904; the dash list, offset, style, background pair (16-entry table at read-only offset 2020, plane mask ignored for raster op 3) and the dash continuation across vertices are as there, and there are again no word runs: 104 halfword stores and 1 word store. 16028 bytes (4007 slots) at offset 0x4cabf0, with 3 div.d for the dash-distance reduction, a 992-byte frame and no calls. Compared with cfb/cfbline.c. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: the same 150 dashed polylines plus 60 relative-mode ones matched the reference, and the sixteen background operations matched the X table.
- `cfb16LineSS` in `crm.so`: draws a solid one-pixel polyline on two-byte pixels. It is the one-byte routine `cfbLineSS` rebuilt for halfword pixels, with the same arguments, clip walk, tie word, last-pixel rule and end-point rule; the context-private index is the global at gp minus 25908 and the pixmap is found through a screen private slot numbered by the global at gp minus 25904 (pitch at pixmap offset 28, base at offset 32) instead of the screen field at offset 116. Pixels are halfword stores (93 of them); a horizontal run goes out as whole words of two pixels (25 word stores for x=3 to x=50, ends included), a vertical or sloped run as one halfword per pixel with the end point a masked word read-modify-write. 9368 bytes (2342 slots) at offset 0x4cea90, with 77 word stores, no calls, no floating point and a 608-byte frame. Compared with cfb/cfbline.c. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: 120 random polylines (coordinates from -6 to 70, one or four boxes, both coordinate modes, two cap styles, random tie words) matched the same reference pixel for pixel; copy writes the xor word and every other raster op writes (old and the and word) xor the xor word; store counts are from the listing.
- `cfb32LineSS` in `crm.so`: draws a solid one-pixel polyline on four-byte pixels. Arguments, clip walk, tie word, last-pixel and end-point rules are those of the one-byte routine; the context-private index is the global at gp minus 25920 and the pixmap comes from the screen private slot numbered by the global at gp minus 25916. Every pixel, horizontal runs included, is one word store (48 stores for a 48-pixel horizontal line and 38 for a 38-pixel slope), so the body holds 166 word stores and no byte or halfword stores, and the end point needs no masked read-modify-write. 9104 bytes (2276 slots) at offset 0x4d0f30, no calls, 608-byte frame, no floating point. Compared with cfb/cfbline.c. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: 120 random polylines (coordinates from -6 to 70, one or four boxes, both coordinate modes, two cap styles, random tie words) matched the reference pixel for pixel; store counts are from the listing.
- `cfbLineSD` in `crm.so`: draws a dashed one-pixel polyline on one-byte pixels. The context supplies the dash state: an unsigned halfword offset at offset 8, an unsigned halfword list length at offset 10, the list pointer at offset 12, the line style in the top two bits of the word at offset 16 (1 on-off, 2 double dash) and the cap style in the two bits below; the raster-op byte is at offset 5, the plane mask at offset 20 and the background pixel at offset 28, while foreground pixels use the private and-and-xor pair as in the solid routines. Even-numbered dashes paint the foreground pair; in double dash the odd ones paint a background pair that the body derives inline from the raster-op byte through a 16-entry jump table of distinct targets inside the routine (read-only offset 5068): raster op 3 writes the background pixel itself and ignores the plane mask, every other operation gives the operation applied to the old pixel, under the plane mask. The dash distance starts at the offset and runs on across the vertices, advancing by each segment's major-axis length, so the pattern is continuous along the polyline; the list is used as given, so an odd-length list simply repeats (a list of three, two and one gives three on, two off, one on, then three on again). A distance beyond the list total is reduced with a double-precision divide at three sites (0x4d3764 for the starting offset, 0x4d3a38 and 0x4d4b00 for the length clipped off the front), none of which runs for small offsets on unclipped lines. The last-pixel, closed-polygon, tie and clip rules are those of the solid polyline routine, but every pixel is a byte store (104 byte stores and 1 word store), so a horizontal dashed line is one store per pixel. 16100 bytes (4025 slots) at offset 0x4d32c0, no calls, 3 div.d, 992-byte frame, 193 movz and 255 movn. The earlier count of about 107 byte stores was wrong. Compared with cfb/cfbline.c (dashed polyline). It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: 210 random dashed polylines (dash lists of two or four entries, offsets from 0 to 20, on-off and double dash, one or four boxes, both coordinate modes, two cap styles, random tie words) matched a separately written reference pixel for pixel, and all sixteen raster operations of the background pair matched the X operation table under random plane masks.
- `cfb32LineSD` in `crm.so`: draws a dashed one-pixel polyline on four-byte pixels. It is the one-byte dashed routine rebuilt for word pixels, with the context index at the global at gp minus 25920 and the pixmap through the screen private slot at the global at gp minus 25916; every pixel is a word store (105 in the body, 48 for a 48-pixel run), the background table is at read-only offset 3996 and the dash and clip rules are unchanged. 15804 bytes (3951 slots) at offset 0x4d71b0, 960-byte frame, 3 div.d, no calls. Compared with cfb/cfbline.c. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: 150 dashed polylines plus 60 relative-mode ones matched the reference pixel for pixel.
- `cfb16XRotatePixmap` in `crm.so`: rotates a tile pixmap horizontally in place, by a signed pixel count. It takes the pixmap and the rotation, returns for a null pixmap, hands one-bit pixmaps to the monochrome rotator, and prints an error for any depth other than 16. The rotation is reduced to a non-negative remainder by a double-precision divide and a correction. Only the case where the pixmap is exactly two pixels wide is handled, as one 32-bit word per row: each word is shifted right by the rotation times 16 bits, OR-ed with the bits shifted out on the left, masked by a table entry at the end-mask table indexed by the rotation. The word loop is unrolled by four, with prefetch and a remainder prologue. Any other width prints an internal-error message and does nothing. 1036 bytes at offset 0x4daf70. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbpixmap.c, the assembly has two ErrorF paths, one monochrome tail-call, the constant 32 and the table lookup. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb16YRotatePixmap` in `crm.so`: rotates a tile pixmap vertically in place, so that row n moves to row n plus the rotation. It takes the pixmap and a signed row count, returns on a null pixmap, prints a message for any depth other than 1 or 16, and reduces the count modulo the height with a floating divide; the one-bit case is a first inlined body and the 16-bit case a second one (starting at instruction 82), both identical in structure. It stacks a scratch buffer sized as the bytes above the cut (pitch times height, minus pitch times rotation), then makes three block copies: low rows to the scratch, the high rows down to the top, and the scratch back after them. A failed allocation returns silently. 536 bytes at offset 0x4db380. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbpixmap.c, the disassembly has three block-copy calls in each body, the pitch read at offset 28 and the pixel-data pointer at offset 32. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfbCopyRotatePixmap` in `crm.so`: makes a destination pixmap that holds a copy of a source tile or stipple, padded out to a full word and rotated by an x and a y amount, taking the source pixmap, the address of the destination pointer and the two rotation values. When a destination already exists with the same row pitch and height it overwrites its pixel rows with a block move and copies the width, depth, bits per pixel and a fresh serial number, taken from the global counter and wrapped back to 1 once it passes 0x10000000; otherwise it frees the old one through the screen's destroy method at offset 240 and creates a new pixmap through the screen's create method at offset 236, with the same size and depth, stores it through the pointer and block-moves the rows. It then replicates the pixel columns until the row fills 32 bits (the pad step the public source reaches through a helper is inlined: when the row is under 32 bits wide a floating divide of 32 by the row's bit width gives the repeat count, and a shift-and-or loop widens each row), and calls the x-rotate routine when the x amount is nonzero and the y-rotate routine when the y amount is. 872 bytes at offset 0x4db5a0; read in full against `cfb/cfbpixmap.c` (the copy-rotate routine, with its copy and pad helpers), the same steps as the body: the copy and pad helpers are inlined, so the only direct calls are two block moves, the two screen methods and the two rotations. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfbXRotatePixmap` in `crm.so`: rotates the columns of a pixmap by a given number of pixels, for a tile that is one word wide. It returns for a null pixmap, handles one-bit pixmaps by tail-calling the bitmap rotate routine and prints an unsupported-depth message through the error-print routine for any bits per pixel other than 8 and 1. For the eight-bit case it reduces the shift modulo the width (floating divide and truncate, plus the width when negative), and when the width is four pixels it rotates each row word by shifting right by the rotation times eight and merging the bits shifted out from the other side under the end mask taken from a table in the data section, the loop unrolled by four rows with a three-row lead-in; another width prints the internal-error message about odd-sized pixmaps. 1036 bytes at offset 0x4db910; read against `cfb/cfbpixmap.c` (the x-rotate routine), the same switch, modulus step and word rotation, except that the public source calls the bitmap routine as an ordinary call and this build tail-calls it. The unrolled row loop was read at its entry and the first lines, not to the last. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the body of the unrolled word loop was not read in full.
- `cfbYRotatePixmap` in `crm.so`: rotates the rows of a pixmap by a given amount, moving the top rows to the bottom as the pattern origin shifts. A null pixmap returns; a bits-per-pixel byte of 1 (the first body) or 8 (the second body, entered by a branch) is handled, anything else prints a message through the error-print routine and returns. It reduces the shift modulo the height by a floating divide and truncate, adds the height back when the result is negative, converts to byte counts from the row pitch, rounds the saved size up to a multiple of 16 and takes that space from the stack, and then does three block moves: the leading rows to the stack, the rest of the rows down to the start, and the saved rows to follow them. 536 bytes at offset 0x4dbd20; read in full against `cfb/cfbpixmap.c`, the same algorithm; the one-bit case that the public source passes to the bitmap routine is inlined here as a near-identical body, the first one, with the eight-bit body starting at instruction 82, so the three moves appear twice, six block-copy calls in all besides the message call. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb32CopyRotatePixmap` in `crm.so`: makes a rotated copy of a four-byte-pixel pixmap, taking the source pixmap, a destination slot, and the x and y rotation; it copies the source with four `memmove` calls (and 5 byte loads), then rotates it, and the digest shows 26 variable right shifts and an immediate of 0x1000 in the body, in a routine of 856 bytes at offset 0x4dbf40; the public counterpart is in `cfb/cfbpixmap.c`, with 214 instructions; the shift loop was not read in full. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb32YRotatePixmap` in `crm.so`: rotates a pixmap's rows by an amount, so that row zero becomes the row at the rotation; it takes the pixmap and the shift, does nothing for a null pixmap, reads the bits-per-pixel byte (offset 3) and for the 32-bit and the one-bit depth runs the same row rotation (in this build the one-bit case is a second copy of the code instead of a call), but for any other value prints an error naming the depth, reduces the shift to the range of the height by floating-point division and truncation, adds the height to a negative remainder, takes the row pitch from offset 28 and the data pointer from offset 32, and with three block moves through a stack buffer of the wrapped part swaps the two row blocks, 536 bytes at offset 0x4dc2a0; read in full against `cfb/cfbpixmap.c`, whose temporary-buffer scheme it reproduces, the divide-by-zero trap on a zero height being the compiler's check. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb32XRotatePixmap` in `crm.so`: rotates a pixmap sideways by a number of pixels, a word-wise shift of every row with wraparound; it takes the pixmap and the amount, reads the bits-per-pixel byte, reports an unsupported depth through `ErrorF` and returns, reports an internal error for an odd-sized pixmap, and otherwise rotates each row with variable left and right shifts and an or to join the two halves (21 of each shift, 21 stores), 1056 bytes at offset 0x4dc4c0; it matches `cfb/cfbpixmap.c`, whose error messages and row rotation it shares (3 `ErrorF` call sites), with 264 instructions; the unrolled row loop was not read word by word. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb16CopyRotatePixmap` in `crm.so`: duplicates a source tile into a destination slot and rotates the copy. It takes the source pixmap, a pointer to the destination pixmap pointer and the x and y rotations. When the destination exists with equal pitch and height it block-copies the pixel rows, copies width, depth and bits per pixel, and takes a fresh serial number; otherwise it destroys the old destination through the screen's destroy slot (240) and creates a new pixmap through slot 236, then copies the bits. The tile is then padded out to a full 32-bit word by replicating its pixels, a loop with two-pixel steps that is inlined here, and finally the x rotator and the y rotator are called when their counts are nonzero. 872 bytes at offset 0x4dc8e0. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbpixmap.c, whose copy and pad helpers are folded into this body; seen are two memmove calls, the two rotators and the two pixmap slots of the screen. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the inlined padding loop was skimmed, not traced instruction by instruction.
- `cfb32FillPoly1RectCopy` in `crm.so`: fills a convex polygon in four-byte pixels with the colour word stored directly, for a context with a single clip rectangle; it takes the drawable, the context, the shape hint, a coordinate mode, a point count and the point array, converts the points to origin-relative coordinates (the 0x8000 sign bit is folded into a bias, and 24 pairs of upper-half shifts split packed x/y words), finds the top and bottom vertices, and walks the left and right edges with a fixed-point step per row before filling each span with word stores, 3904 bytes at offset 0x4dcc50; this is the copy variant of the single-rectangle polygon filler in `cfb/cfbply1rct.c`, whose general twin below differs by using the and/xor merge; the digest shows two indirect calls, a 320-byte frame and 976 instructions; the edge-walk loops past instruction 38 were not read. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb16FillPoly1RectCopy` in `crm.so`: fills a convex or other single-pass polygon with a solid colour in copy mode when the whole polygon lies inside one clip rectangle. It takes the drawable, context, shape, coordinate mode, vertex count and vertices; the previous-mode case jumps away to the generic polygon filler, and the clip test packs x and y into one word and compares it with the box using the packed constant 0x00010001. It finds the top vertex, steps the left and right edges with integer error terms (dx, dy, step and sign for each side held in registers), and fills each scan line with start mask, whole words and end mask as plain stores (44 word stores in the body). Its 1051 instructions are 464 fewer than the general form. 4204 bytes at offset 0x4ddb90. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbply1rct.c built with the copy raster operation; the assembly has two jumps through a register to other routines, 24 packed-coordinate shifts and the constant 32767. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the entry, clip packing and edge setup were read, the span loops were not.
- `cfb32FillPoly1RectGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills a convex polygon in four-byte pixels with an arbitrary raster operation, under a one-rectangle clip; arguments and the converting prologue match the copy variant (point packing, 0x8000 bias, top and bottom vertex search with 49 signed compares), but each span is merged by an and with the destination followed by an xor with the context's stored words rather than a plain store, 3820 bytes at offset 0x4dec00; it is the general-raster counterpart of the single-rectangle polygon filler in `cfb/cfbply1rct.c`, 21 instructions smaller in total (955 against 976), and it makes the same two indirect calls; the edge and span loops were not read in detail. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfbFillPoly1RectGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills a convex or general polygon with an arbitrary raster operation under a one-rectangle clip on one-byte pixels. It matches the copy-only sibling in structure: previous-mode lists and any polygon not wholly inside the clip rectangle go to the generic polygon filler by tail call (two such exits, one early and one near the end), the packed point words are scanned for the top row and the clip violation, and two edges are stepped down the polygon with error terms. Where it differs is the store: each span word is combined with the destination through the and and xor values taken from the context's private record (loaded in the prologue from two adjacent words of the record the screen index selects), under the start and end masks, so every row needs a read-modify-write of whole words, which shows in 68 word stores, against 44 in the copy variant. 5996 bytes at offset 0x4dfaf0. Compared with `cfb/cfbply1rct.c`, the general-rop build. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: setup, the two exits and the store shape were read; the scanline loops were not read through.
- `cfb16FillPoly1RectGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills a polygon inside a single clip rectangle with any raster operation. The arguments and edge-stepping are those of the copy-mode filler, and but each scan line is combined with the and and xor words from the private record, so every destination word is read, anded, xored and written back. It is 1515 instructions with 64 and-instructions and 72 xor-instructions, 68 word stores and the constant 0x8000. 6060 bytes at offset 0x4e1260. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbply1rct.c built for the general operation, the disassembly has 24 packed-coordinate shifts in the same places as the copy form. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: counts and the head were read.
- `cfb16PolyPoint` in `crm.so`: plots a list of single pixels in a 16-bit drawable, clipped to the composite clip region. The inputs are the drawable, context, coordinate mode, point count and the packed points. It reads the raster-op byte at offset 0 of the context's private record and the xor value at offset 12 and the and value after it, takes the clip region at offset 8, and for previous-mode points first turns the list into absolute coordinates with an unrolled add loop. The clip test packs both bound checks into one word with the constant 0x80008000, adding 0x8000 to the box corners; the pitch is taken in pixels (the long-word pitch halved) and, when it is a power of two, turned into a shift through the library bit-scan call. Pixels are written as 16-bit stores, a plain store for copy and a mask-and-xor read-modify-write for the other operations. A clip region with no rectangle list takes a single-box fast path. 1484 bytes at offset 0x4e2a10. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbpolypnt.c, the disassembly shows one call to the bit-scan routine, 13 half-word stores in the three plot loops and the 0x8000 constants. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the prologue, previous-mode loop and the xor and general loops were read, the copy loop was matched by shape.
- `cfb32PolyPoint` in `crm.so`: plots points in a four-byte-pixel drawable, taking the drawable, the context, the coordinate mode, the count and the points, and returning at once when the raster-operation byte of the private record is the no-op code 5; it picks the first-point-relative path when the mode is 1, packs each point into one word, uses the sign bit 0x8000 and the mask 0xfffe to test containment with one compare, calls `ffs` once, and writes pixels with and/xor merging when the operation is general, 1500 bytes at offset 0x4e2fe0; its counterpart is `cfb/cfbpolypnt.c`, and the digest agrees on a 128-byte frame, 375 instructions and 7 upper-immediate loads (the constant 0x80000000); the writes were not read word by word. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfbPolyPoint` in `crm.so`: plots a list of single pixels on one-byte pixels, taking the drawable, the context, the coordinate mode, the point count and the point list. It looks up the context's private record, returns at once when the raster-operation byte there is 5 (the no-operation value), and when the mode is relative (the value 1) first turns the list into absolute coordinates by accumulating each point onto its predecessor. It then forms the pixel base address from the drawable's pixmap pitch and origin and steps through the clip boxes, packing each point's x and y into one word and testing it against the box corners by two subtractions and an or whose sign bits are checked (the mask 0x8000 in the sign positions); a point inside is written as one byte, with a plain store of the foreground value from offset 8 of the record when the operation is copy, and otherwise a load, and with the and value, xor with the xor value, and store. A row pitch that is a power of two is turned into a shift through the library's find-first-set call so the row offset is a shift instead of a multiply. 1276 bytes at offset 0x4e35c0; read against `cfb/cfbpolypnt.c`, where the same function has the same no-op return, accumulation, packed clip test and copy, xor and general paths, but the copy and general paths are three separate loops here, and the pitch shift loop is selected at run time as in the public source. Evidence: the single callee is the find-first-set call at instruction 261, and there are 5 byte stores. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the entry, the packed test and the first loop of each kind were read; the later loop variants were only compared by instruction pattern.
- `cfbPushPixels8` in `crm.so`: pushes a one-bit mask (a bitmap used as a stipple) onto the drawable in the context's foreground colour on one-byte pixels, taking the context, the source bitmap, the drawable, the width and height of the area and its destination origin. It builds a box from the origin and size on its stack and asks the screen's region-rectangle method at offset 380 how it falls against the clip; when the answer is fully outside it returns, when it is partly inside (the value 2) it goes to the clip-aware slow path through the monochrome push routine, and when fully inside it expands the mask bits into pixels directly: it splits each mask word into nibbles and writes bytes or halfwords for a nibble through jump tables, 17 of them in the body. 3940 bytes at offset 0x4e3ac0. Compared with `cfb/cfbpush8.c` (the eight-bit pixel push routine). Evidence is the call through the offset-380 method at instruction 32, the monochrome fallback call at 981, and 256 byte plus 100 halfword stores that write the isolated pixels of each group. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, the clip answer handling and the fallback were read; the 17 jump-table bodies were not read, so the nibble dispatch is taken from the public source.
- `cfb8FillRectTransparentStippled32` in `crm.so`: fills rectangles with a stipple whose width is 32 pixels, drawing only the pixels whose stipple bit is set, on one-byte pixels. It caches the stipple mode, plane mask, foreground colour and raster operation in module globals and builds the and/xor pairs in an inline 16-way switch, then for each box rotates the stipple row for the left edge phase, splits the width into a left partial word, a run of whole words and a right partial word, and expands each stipple nibble to four pixel writes as the public routine does; the body dispatches through four jump tables whose selector is unknown. 9456 bytes at offset 0x4e4a30. Compared with `cfb/cfbrctstp8.c`, the transparent-stipple rectangle routine. It has no call sites, so the stipple setup and every nibble expander are inlined, while the public source calls its setup helper; the body has 536 byte stores and 218 halfword stores for the partial pixels on the box edges. The sibling span filler for the same stipple (`cfb8Stipple32FS`) shares the store mix and the tables, but this one works box by box and has no clipper call. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: structure, caching and store counts were read; the nibble loops were not traced to the end.
- `cfb8FillRectOpaqueStippled32` in `crm.so`: fills rectangles with a 32-pixel-wide stipple in opaque style on one-byte pixels, painting foreground where the stipple bit is set and background where it is clear. At entry it calls the opaque-stipple setup routine (the only call, at instruction 35) so the shared globals hold the raster operation, foreground, background and plane mask and the sixteen per-nibble and/xor words are rebuilt. For each box it takes the stipple row from the rotated pixmap selected by the box's y, left-rotates it for the x phase, writes a start-masked word, whole words and an end-masked word, each destination word being the stipple-selected and/xor words from the setup tables, and wraps the row counter at the stipple height. 3832 bytes at offset 0x4e6f20. Compared with `cfb/cfbrctstp8.c`, the opaque variant of the stipple rectangle routine. It has 94 word stores and no byte or halfword stores, and no jump table, in contrast with the transparent sibling `cfb8FillRectTransparentStippled32` whose body is full of byte and halfword stores and has four jump tables. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the setup call and the store shape were read; the nibble loops were not.
- `cfb8FillRectStippledUnnatural` in `crm.so`: fills rectangles with a stipple whose width is not a divisor of the machine word, handling both the opaque and the transparent style on one-byte pixels. It begins by calling the opaque-stipple setup (instruction 32) and then, per box, steps through the stipple at the box's x and y phase, wrapping the position when the stipple width is used up and the row when the stipple height is, and stores pixels for the style, with one jump table in the body whose selector is unknown. 3800 bytes at offset 0x4e7e20. Compared with `cfb/cfbrctstp8.c` (the unnatural-width stipple rectangle routine). It has 22 word stores and 49 doubleword register spills, far fewer pixel stores than the 32-wide siblings `cfb8FillRectOpaqueStippled32` (94 word stores), the way it builds each pixel word is unknown. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: setup, the call and the store mix were read; the jump-table purpose and the inner loop were not.
- `cfb16SegmentSD` in `crm.so`: draws a list of independent dashed one-pixel segments on two-byte pixels. It is the one-byte dashed segment routine for halfword pixels: context index at the global at gp minus 25908, pixmap through the screen private slot at the global at gp minus 25904, a 16-target jump table for the background pair at read-only offset 2788, and 104 halfword stores as the only pixel stores. 14024 bytes (3506 slots) at offset 0x4e8d00, 816-byte frame, 835 addu instructions, 2 div.d, no calls. Compared with cfb/cfbline.c built for segments. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: 150 random dashed segment lists matched the reference pixel for pixel; the earlier note of a clip bound of 32767 could not be tied to any behaviour and is dropped.
- `cfb16SegmentSS` in `crm.so`: draws a list of independent solid one-pixel segments on two-byte pixels. Arguments, per-segment rules, clip walk and tie word are those of the one-byte segment routine: each segment keeps its last pixel unless the cap style is zero, and a negative zero-line screen index leaves the tie word at zero rather than returning. The context-private index is the global at gp minus 25908 and the pixmap comes through the screen private slot numbered by the global at gp minus 25904. The raster-op byte picks between only two writes: 3 stores the xor word, any other value stores (old and the and word) xor the xor word, both as halfwords. A horizontal run is 25 whole-word stores for x=3 to x=50 (two pixels per word, partial words at the ends), any other run one halfword store per pixel. 8908 bytes (2227 slots) at offset 0x4ec3d0, no calls, 93 halfword stores and 76 word stores, 321 addu instructions, 576-byte frame. The same segment build of cfb/cfbline.c supplies the contract, with halfword pixel stores. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: 150 random segment lists matched the reference pixel for pixel. The earlier statements that a negative screen index returns early and that the pixel step is chosen per raster operation were wrong: the listing falls through with tie word zero and has the two-way write above.
- `cfb32SegmentSD` in `crm.so`: draws a list of independent dashed one-pixel segments on four-byte pixels. It is the one-byte dashed segment routine for word pixels: context index at the global at gp minus 25920, pixmap through the screen private slot at the global at gp minus 25916, a 16-target jump table for the background pair at read-only offset 4124, and 104 word stores. Clipping uses conditional moves (186 movz and 255 movn); there are no calls, since the one register jump in the body is the table dispatch, with an 800-byte frame and 2 div.d for the dash distance. 13880 bytes (3470 slots) at offset 0x4ee6a0. Compared with cfb/cfbline.c built for segments. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: 150 random dashed segment lists matched the reference pixel for pixel; the earlier claim of a single indirect call was this table jump.
- `cfbSegmentSD` in `crm.so`: draws a list of independent dashed one-pixel segments on one-byte pixels. Arguments are the drawable, the context, the segment count and 8-byte point-pair records. The context supplies the dash state: an unsigned halfword offset at offset 8, an unsigned halfword list length at offset 10, the list pointer at offset 12, the line style in the top two bits of the word at offset 16 (1 on-off, 2 double dash) and the cap style in the two bits below; the raster-op byte is at offset 5, the plane mask at offset 20 and the background pixel at offset 28, while foreground pixels use the private and-and-xor pair as in the solid routines. Every segment restarts the dash at the context's offset (a large offset is reduced by a double-precision divide at 0x4f20ec, and the length clipped off the front by another at 0x4f31a0), paints even dashes in the foreground pair and, in double dash, odd dashes in the derived background pair (16-entry jump table at read-only offset 6632; raster op 3 ignores the plane mask), and keeps its last pixel unless the cap style is zero; there is no end-point pass. Pixels are byte stores only (104), so even a horizontal dashed run is one store per pixel. 14224 bytes (3556 slots) at offset 0x4f1ce0, no calls, 832-byte frame, 2 div.d, 186 movz and 255 movn. Compared with cfb/cfbline.c built for segments. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: 150 random dashed segment lists matched the reference pixel for pixel, an odd-length list repeats as given, and all sixteen background operations matched the X operation table.
- `cfb16SetSpans` in `crm.so`: stores client rows of 16-bit pixels into horizontal spans of a drawable. It takes the drawable, context, source bytes, start points, widths, span count and a sorted flag. The context's composite clip is walked, each span is clipped to it, and the source pixels are merged into the destination words with a start mask, whole words and an end mask, using the raster operation held in the private record; each source word is shifted by the pixel misalignment between the source offset and the destination. The routine has no external calls, so clipping is inline. 3336 bytes at offset 0x4f5470. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbsetsp.c, whose scanline helper is inlined here; the disassembly has 64 shift-left instructions, the constant 0xc and the 0xffff 16-bit mask. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the instruction mix and constants were read, not the loops.
- `cfb32SetSpans` in `crm.so`: stores client scanlines into spans of a four-byte-pixel drawable; it takes the drawable, the context, the source data, the start points, the widths and the count, merges each pixel run with the context's stored and and xor words (42 and, 36 xor instructions), steps the source through whole rows (15 signed compares appear), 1648 bytes at offset 0x4f6180; the public `cfb/cfbsetsp.c` routine merges the same way for the general raster operation, and the digest shows 412 instructions and no calls; only the general-merge loop's shape was checked. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfbSetSpans` in `crm.so`: writes client-supplied pixel rows into a drawable at a list of spans, taking the drawable, the context, the source row data, point and width arrays, a span count and a sorted flag, on one-byte pixels. It reads the composite clip, uses the padding table for the source pixmap stride to move to each row's source data (the table is the one indexed by the depth), and for each span intersects it with every clip box in turn, copying the visible stretch with a word-aligned loop that merges the first and last words under masks and applies the raster operation and plane mask where they are not the default. 3368 bytes at offset 0x4f67f0. Compared with `cfb/cfbsetsp.c` (the set-spans routine of the same name). It has no outgoing calls, 10 word stores and 66 doubleword spills, so most of the copying is word moves done with the loads and stores not counted here; the number of pixel-writing stores was therefore not measured. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, padding-table use and the absence of calls were read; the copy loops were not.
- `cfb16SolidSpansCopy` in `crm.so`: fills horizontal spans with a solid colour by plain stores on a 16-bit drawable. It takes the drawable, context, span count, start points, widths and a sorted flag. A loop over the clip rectangles finds the largest band height, the span and width scratch arrays are stack-allocated at four bytes per entry times that height, and the clip routine is called to clip the spans; allocation failure just returns. For each clipped span the start word is found from x halved and the pitch, a start mask comes from a table indexed by the low bit of x, an end mask from a table indexed by the low bit of x plus the width, and the middle words are written in a loop unrolled eight at a time with the xor word, held at private offset 12. A span within one word combines the masks into one read-modify-write. 912 bytes at offset 0x4f7520. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbsolid.c built for copy, read to its last instruction, with 28 word stores and mask-table loads from the 16960 region of the read-only data. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb32SolidSpansCopy` in `crm.so`: fills spans of a four-byte-pixel drawable by plain stores of the context's colour word, taking the drawable, the context, the span count, the start points, the widths and the sorted flag; it starts by scanning the clip boxes for the tallest band (a loop over the box list, comparing neighbouring bands) to size two stack arrays, calls `miClipSpans`, and stores the colour word at each pixel position of each clipped span (26 word stores), with the colour read from the private record at offset 12, 724 bytes at offset 0x4f78b0; it is the copy case of `cfb/cfbsolid.c`, and the digest shows 181 instructions and one clipping call; the store loop was not read word by word. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb16FillRectSolidCopy` in `crm.so`: fills a list of boxes with a solid colour in copy mode on a 16-bit drawable. The inputs are the drawable, the context and the box list, and the colour is the xor word at private offset 12 of the context. For each box it works out the first word and a start mask when the left edge is on an odd pixel, an end mask for the last odd pixel, and the count of whole words; narrow boxes of one word use a combined mask; whole words are stored with unrolled 32-bit stores. There are 109 word stores and no destination reads outside the masked edge words. 1640 bytes at offset 0x4f7b90. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbsolid.c built with the copy rop; the assembly has no external calls and mask table loads that select between the two 16-bit pixel positions. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the structure and counts were read, the unrolled store loops were not.
- `cfb16FillRectSolidGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills boxes with a solid colour using an arbitrary raster operation on a 16-bit drawable. It takes the same drawable, context and box list, and reads both the and word (private offset 16) and the xor word (offset 12), applying each destination word as (word and mask) xor value; the per-word step costs a load, an and, an xor and a store, so the body is 467 instructions. Edge words use the masks, and the copy filler is only 410 instructions. 1868 bytes at offset 0x4f8200. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbsolid.c built for the general raster operation, the assembly has 43 and-instructions and 37 xor-instructions, 37 word stores, no calls and the constant 0xc. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the counts were read and the loop shape was compared.
- `cfb32SolidSpansGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills spans of a four-byte-pixel drawable with one colour under an arbitrary raster operation, taking the drawable, the context, the span count, the start points, the widths and the sorted flag; it clips the spans through `miClipSpans` (one call), reads the colour as a stored and/xor pair and applies it with and then xor per destination word (28 and, 26 xor), 1220 bytes at offset 0x4f8950; it is the general-merge solid span filler of `cfb/cfbsolid.c`, and the digest shows 305 instructions; the stores were not read word by word. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
