Compared with `cfb/cfbtileodd.c` (the span-fill routine of the same name, general-rop build). It makes no calls and has 25 word stores, against 48 in the copy sibling, and it is the box routine's twin: the span version walks width and start arrays instead of boxes. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue and tile fields were read; the span loop was not.
- `cfbFillBoxTileOddGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills a list of boxes with a tile whose width is not a multiple of the machine word, under an arbitrary raster operation and plane mask, on one-byte pixels. The tile's pixmap supplies its size (its width halfword at offset 12 and height halfword at offset 14), its pitch and its data pointer; the pitch is rounded up and divided by four to count words, a result of one meaning the tile is a single word wide. For each box it starts from the tile row selected by the box's y minus the pattern origin, assembles the tile bits for the box's x phase by splicing the tail of one tile word onto the head of the next, handles a left partial word, a run of whole words and a right partial word, and combines every destination word with the tile word through the merge-rop and plane-mask values. 2924 bytes at offset 0x50fff0. Compared with `cfb/cfbtileodd.c` (the box-fill routine, general-rop build). It has no calls, 12 word stores and 68 doubleword spills, so its stores are few and the loop leans on registers, in contrast with the copy sibling (35 word stores) that writes the tile word as is. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: setup, the tile fields and the store counts were read; the splicing loops were not read to the end.
- `cfbFillSpanTile32sGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills spans with a tile whose width is a multiple of the machine word, under an arbitrary raster operation and plane mask, on one-byte pixels, taking the drawable, a span count, start points, widths, the tile, the pattern origin, the raster operation and the plane mask. It reads the tile's width and height halfwords (offsets 12 and 14) and its data pointer at offset 32, and the destination pitch and base from the screen pixmap, then for each span walks the tile words with wrap at the tile width and combines each with the destination word through the merge constants, using masks at the two ends. 1824 bytes at offset 0x510b60; read against `cfb/cfbtileodd.c` (the multiple-of-word tile span routine, general build). It makes no calls and has 14 word stores and 22 spills (the copy sibling has 58 word stores), since the combine uses loads and the loops are not unrolled. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: setup and the counts were read; the span loop was not read.
- `cfb16FillBoxTile32sGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills boxes with a wide 32-bit-multiple tile under any raster operation and plane mask. It takes the same arguments as the copy form and reduces the rotation in the same way, but it merges with and and xor values prepared from the operation: 50 and-instructions and 44 xor-instructions in 538 instructions, with only 14 word stores since most of the work is in registers. 2152 bytes at offset 0x511280. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbtileodd.c built for the general merge, the assembly has no calls and the constant 0xa. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the box body was compared with its span twin by instruction totals.
- `cfb32FillBoxTile32sGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills boxes of a four-byte-pixel drawable from a tile whose width divides 32 pixels, for any raster operation; it takes the drawable, the box count, the boxes, the tile and the tile rotation, computes the tile row and column of each box by floating-point divide and truncate (4 converts, as in the copy sibling), and merges tile and destination with and then xor (22 and, 19 xor), 1140 bytes at offset 0x511af0; it is the general case of the tile-box fill of `cfb/cfbfillrct.c`, with 285 instructions and no calls (the copy build has one indirect call); the loops were not read in full. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb16FillSpanTileOddGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills horizontal spans with an odd-width tile under any raster operation and plane mask. Its tile-pixel assembly is the same as the copy version's, with the final merge using the and and xor values: 44 and-instructions, 25 xor-instructions and 26 right shifts, in 704 instructions and with 25 word stores. 2816 bytes at offset 0x511f70. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbtileodd.c built for the general merge, the assembly has no calls and the constant 0xa. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the merge step was identified from instruction totals alone.
- `cfb32FillSpanTile32sGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills spans from a tile whose width divides 32 pixels, for any raster operation on four-byte pixels; it takes the drawable, the span count, the start points, the widths, the tile and the rotation, finds the tile row and column of each span by floating-point divide and truncate (6 converts), and merges with and then xor (21 and, 18 xor), 932 bytes at offset 0x512a70; it is the general case of the span-tile fill in `cfb/cfbfillsp.c`, with only 6 word stores against 40 in the copy build and 233 instructions against 273, and no calls; the merge loops were not read through. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb16FillBoxTileOddGeneral` in `crm.so`: fills boxes with an odd-width tile under any raster operation and plane mask. The arguments and the shift-and-or assembly of tile pixels match the copy version, then each assembled word is merged into the destination with the prepared and and xor values: 43 and-instructions, 27 xor-instructions and 24 right shifts in 731 instructions, 29 more than the copy form. 2924 bytes at offset 0x512e20. Compared with the corresponding routine of cfb/cfbtileodd.c built for the general merge, the assembly has no calls and the constant 0xa. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: only the totals, not the merge loops, were read.
- `cfb32ZeroPolyArcSSCopy` in `crm.so`: loops over a list of one-pixel arcs and draws each one clipped to the region's boxes on four-byte pixels, for the copy raster operation, 464 bytes at offset 0x513990; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb16ZeroPolyArcSSCopy` in `crm.so`: loops over a list of one-pixel arcs and draws each one clipped to the region's boxes on two-byte pixels, for the copy raster operation, 464 bytes at offset 0x513b60; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb16ZeroPolyArcSSGeneral` in `crm.so`: loops over a list of one-pixel arcs and draws each one clipped to the region's boxes on two-byte pixels, for an arbitrary raster operation, 468 bytes at offset 0x513d30; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfbZeroPolyArcSS8General` in `crm.so`: draws a list of one-pixel arcs for an arbitrary raster operation on one-byte pixels. For each of the 12-byte arc records it routes the arc by size and clip: unequal sizes with either above 800 go to the generic poly-arc routine, a box (origin plus drawable origin, extent plus one, truncated to 16 bits) with a far edge beyond 32767 goes to the generic zero-width arc routine, a box the screen's rectangle method at offset 380 reports as fully inside the clip region (found at offset 8 of the private record) goes to the one-byte general arc routine, and every other box goes to the generic zero-width arc routine. 468 bytes at offset 0x513f10; read in full against `cfb/cfbzerarc.c`, the general-rop build, and it matches that body step for step. It is the code of its xor and copy siblings with a different per-arc address (the general clone with suffix PK) loaded at instruction 16; the general version is one instruction longer than the other two (117 against 116) because of a register move in the fallback branch. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfb32ZeroPolyArcSSGeneral` in `crm.so`: loops over a list of one-pixel arcs and draws each one clipped to the region's boxes on four-byte pixels, for an arbitrary raster operation, 468 bytes at offset 0x5140f0; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb16ZeroPolyArcSSXor` in `crm.so`: loops over a list of one-pixel arcs and draws each one clipped to the region's boxes on two-byte pixels, for the xor raster operation, 468 bytes at offset 0x5142d0; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfb32ZeroPolyArcSSXor` in `crm.so`: loops over a list of one-pixel arcs and draws each one clipped to the region's boxes on four-byte pixels, for the xor raster operation, 468 bytes at offset 0x5144b0; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfbZeroPolyArcSS8Xor` in `crm.so`: draws a list of one-pixel arcs for the xor raster operation on one-byte pixels, choosing per arc between the fast unclipped routine and two generic fallbacks. Its arguments are the drawable, the context, an arc count and the array of 12-byte arc records; the clip region comes from the context's private record at offset 8. An arc whose two sizes differ and are not both at most 800 is sent to the generic poly-arc routine (one-element list). Otherwise it forms the arc's bounding box (origin plus drawable origin, size plus one, kept as 16-bit values) and, when neither far edge passes 32767, asks the screen's rectangle-in-region method at offset 380 about it; only an answer of fully inside calls the one-byte xor arc routine, and every other case goes to the generic zero-width arc routine, again with a one-element list. 464 bytes at offset 0x514690; read in full against `cfb/cfbzerarc.c` (the poly-arc function built for xor), the same steps as the body. The three call targets are loaded from the table of addresses at entry. The Copy and General siblings have the same code apart from the arc routine address taken at instruction 26 (it is the xor clone with suffix AL here). It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `cfbPolyGlyphRop8Clipped..NK` in `crm.so`: draws one transparent glyph with a general raster operation clipped to a box on one-byte pixels, unprefixed build, 4172 bytes at offset 0x56f750; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `cfbPolyGlyphBlt8Clipped..JL` in `crm.so`: draws one transparent glyph of an eight-pixel-wide font with a clip applied, and is the helper specialised for four-byte pixels: its pixel stores are all 32-bit word stores (157 of them, no byte or halfword stores). It is the clone that the four-byte-pixel text routine `cfb32PolyGlyphBlt8` calls at instruction 554, so the unprefixed name is only the shared source name of the helper; it takes the screen's pixmap through the drawable's screen and a screen-private slot at offset 436, and two private-index globals different from the ones in the one-byte clone. For each glyph row it forms the glyph's bit pattern, combines it with the clip masks computed for the box list, and stores the foreground word wherever a mask nibble says so, switching through three jump tables on the visible pixel group. 3880 bytes at offset 0x585b50. Compared with `cfb/cfbglblt8.c` (the clipped static helper of the glyph routine), as built for 32-bit pixels. It has no outgoing calls. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, the owner relationship and the store width were read; the masking and nibble loops were not.
- `cfbPolyGlyphBlt8Clipped..AM` in `crm.so`: draws one transparent glyph of an eight-pixel-wide font with a clip applied, as the helper compiled for two-byte pixels: 64 halfword stores and 61 word stores carry the pixels, with no byte stores. It is the clone called at instruction 538 of the two-byte-pixel text routine `cfb16PolyGlyphBlt8`, so its unprefixed name is only the shared source name. Like the four-byte clone it reaches the screen pixmap through a screen-private slot at offset 436 with its own pair of private-index globals, which tells it apart from the one-byte clone. It clips each glyph row against the box list with computed masks and then stores the foreground pixel pair wherever the mask nibble selects, dispatching through four jump tables. 3828 bytes at offset 0x586fc0. Compared with `cfb/cfbglblt8.c`, the clipped static helper of the eight-pixel glyph routine, built for 16-bit pixels. No calls leave the body. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the opening instructions, the caller link and the store mix were read; the glyph loops were not.
- `cfbPolyGlyphBlt8Clipped..OK` in `crm.so`: draws one transparent glyph of an eight-pixel-wide font with a clip applied on one-byte pixels, the clone that the unprefixed text routine calls at instruction 532. It reads the screen's own pixmap directly through the screen record at offset 116 and a single private-index global (the other two clones use a slot at 436), and writes with 64 byte stores plus 24 halfword and 33 word stores, so partial groups go out as bytes or halfwords where a mask leaves part of a word alone. Per glyph it walks the visible boxes, computes the horizontal clip masks for the glyph's rows (the glyph box comes from three halfword fields of the character record) and stores the foreground byte under them, dispatching through four jump tables. 3812 bytes at offset 0x5893c0. Compared with `cfb/cfbglblt8.c`, the static clipped helper at 8-bit pixels. No outgoing calls. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the prologue, caller link and store mix were read; the mask arithmetic was not read in full.
- `cfbZeroArcSS8Copy..FL` in `crm.so`: draws one-pixel arcs for the copy raster operation on one-byte pixels: this is the clone the one-byte copy poly-arc routine calls for an arc inside the clip. It takes the pixmap pitch and base from the screen record, calls the shared zero-width arc setup once at instruction 30 with the arc, an info record on the stack and a request for the full-circle form, reads the foreground word from offset 12 of the context's private record, and then walks the midpoint error terms, plotting the symmetric points of each step as plain byte stores of the foreground value (34 byte stores, no load of the old pixel and no and or xor). 1992 bytes at offset 0x58d890; the public one-arc routine in `cfb/cfbzerarc.c` is the same algorithm in its copy configuration. Length is 498 instructions, the shortest of the three byte-pixel arc clones (the xor one has 514, the general one 544), and in comparison with the wider-pixel copy clones it does not use the window-private pixmap slot at offset 436. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, the setup call, store width and the counts were read; the stepping arms were compared by pattern with the other clones, not read through.
- `cfbZeroArcSS8Copy..FM` in `crm.so`: draws one-pixel arcs for the copy raster operation on two-byte pixels. It makes one call to the shared zero-width arc setup at instruction 35, builds the row origins from the drawable origin and a pitch taken through a window-private slot (offset 436), and then plots the symmetric points of each midpoint step as plain halfword stores of the foreground value from offset 12 of the context's private record: 34 halfword stores, with no load of the old pixel and no and or xor instruction. 2200 bytes at offset 0x58e060. Its source counterpart is the one-arc routine of `cfb/cfbzerarc.c` in the copy configuration at 16-bit pixel size, probably called by the poly-arc routine for that depth. Next to the xor clone `cfbZeroArcSS8Xor..EM` it holds more stores (34 against 26) because each pixel is written without a read, and it is 550 instructions against 570. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, store width and the absence of combine instructions were read; the code after the setup call was skimmed, not followed arm by arm.
- `cfbDestroyPixmap` in `crm.so`: drops one reference to the pixmap and frees it when none remain, always returning true, 88 bytes at offset 0x58e950; read in full against the public sample-server source `cfb/cfbpixmap.c`: the body takes 1 off the reference count at +0x18, calls the release routine through the global table when the count reaches zero, and returns 1 on both paths. Established.
- `cfbZeroArcSS8Xor..AL` in `crm.so`: draws one-pixel arcs for the xor raster operation on one-byte pixels: this is the clone the one-byte xor poly-arc routine calls for an arc inside the clip. It takes pixmap pitch and base from the screen record, calls the shared zero-width arc setup once at instruction 30, reads the xor word from offset 12 of the context's private record, and plots the symmetric points of each midpoint step as a byte load, xor with that word and byte store (26 byte stores, 28 byte loads, 26 xor instructions, no and), as the opening of the stepping loop shows with four such read-modify-write sequences per step. 2056 bytes at offset 0x58e9b0; the xor configuration of the one-arc routine in `cfb/cfbzerarc.c` is the counterpart. Length is 514 instructions, sixteen more than the copy clone `cfbZeroArcSS8Copy..FL` and thirty fewer than the general one, the difference being the load-xor step. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, the setup call and the first stepping arms were read; the later arms were compared by pattern only.
- `cfbZeroArcSS8Xor..EM` in `crm.so`: draws one-pixel arcs for the xor raster operation on two-byte pixels. It takes one arc, calls the shared zero-width arc setup once at instruction 35, derives the row origins, and steps the midpoint error terms, where each plotted point is a halfword read-modify-write: load, xor with the value at offset 12 of the context's private record, store (26 halfword stores in the body). The drawable pixmap is reached through a window-private slot at offset 436. 2280 bytes at offset 0x58f990. The matching public code is the xor configuration of the one-arc routine in `cfb/cfbzerarc.c` at 16-bit pixel size, and this clone stands to the two-byte depth as the one-byte xor clone does to its depth. The pixel path has 31 halfword loads and 26 xor instructions with no and, half the and-plus-xor count (52) of the general-rop clone, and the body is 570 instructions to the four-byte clone's 568. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, the setup call and the xor pixel pattern were read; the stepping loop was read as far as its first load-xor-store group.
- `cfbZeroArcSS8Xor..ML` in `crm.so`: draws one-pixel arcs for the xor raster operation on four-byte pixels, plotting each point as a word read-modify-write (load, xor with the value at offset 12 of the private record, store) with 30 word stores in the body. It reaches the pixmap through the window-private screen slot at offset 436, calls the shared zero-width arc setup once at instruction 35, and steps the midpoint terms around the arc. 2272 bytes at offset 0x591190. The one-arc routine in `cfb/cfbzerarc.c` has the xor configuration that this clone follows at 32-bit pixel size. Its two siblings in the same source write halfwords (`cfbZeroArcSS8Xor..EM`) or bytes (`cfbZeroArcSS8Xor..AL`), which is the only structural difference seen; the body has 26 xor instructions and no and, where the general clone has 26 of each. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, the single setup call and the store width were read; the loop that steps the midpoint terms was not followed past its entry.
- `cfbZeroArcSS8Copy..NL` in `crm.so`: draws one-pixel arcs for the copy raster operation on four-byte pixels, writing the foreground value (offset 12 of the context's private record) with 38 plain word stores and no read of the old pixel. It reaches the drawable pixmap through a window-private screen slot at offset 436, calls the shared zero-width arc setup once at instruction 35, and steps the midpoint terms, plotting the symmetric points of each step. 2192 bytes at offset 0x593180. The routine it follows is the copy configuration of the one-arc routine in `cfb/cfbzerarc.c` at 32-bit pixel size, and it is probably the clone the four-byte poly-arc routine calls. It has 548 instructions, two fewer than the two-byte copy clone, and has no and, xor or load instructions on the pixel path. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, store width and the lack of a combine step were read; the symmetric-point plotting arms were not traced one by one.
- `cfbZeroArcSS8General..PK` in `crm.so`: draws one-pixel arcs for the arbitrary raster operation on one-byte pixels: this is the clone the one-byte poly-arc routine for the general operation calls for an arc wholly inside the clip. It takes the pixmap pitch and base directly from the screen record (offset 116), calls the zero-width arc setup once with a request for the full-circle form (instruction 31), and then plots points at the symmetric positions of each midpoint step as a load, and with the context's and value, xor with its xor value, and a byte store (26 of them), the values coming from two adjacent words of the private record. 2176 bytes at offset 0x595d70. The general-operation configuration of the one-arc routine in `cfb/cfbzerarc.c` is the code it implements. At 544 instructions it is shorter than the 16-bit clone with 605 because the byte version needs no word address arithmetic for window privates. It has the one setup call and no others. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, the setup call, store width and the combine pattern were read; only the opening arms of the midpoint stepping loop were read.
- `cfbZeroArcSS8General..IL` in `crm.so`: draws one-pixel arcs for the arbitrary raster operation on four-byte pixels, with 30 word stores for the pixels and a window-private screen slot at offset 436 as the way to the drawable pixmap. For its single arc it calls the shared zero-width arc setup once at instruction 37, forms the row and column origins and pitches, and then steps the midpoint error terms around the arc plotting symmetric points, each loaded, combined with the and and xor words of the context's private record and stored whole. 2408 bytes at offset 0x596820. Compared with `cfb/cfbzerarc.c`, the general-rop build for 32-bit pixels. It is one of three same-source clones (the others write halfwords and bytes), probably the one the four-byte poly-arc routine calls; the 602 instructions are about the same as the two-byte clone's 605, the difference being the store width. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: prologue, store width and the setup call were read; the stepping loops were not.
- `cfbZeroArcSS8General..BM` in `crm.so`: draws one-pixel arcs for the arbitrary raster operation on two-byte pixels, plotting four or eight symmetric points per midpoint step; its pixel stores are halfwords (26 of them) and it reaches the drawable pixmap through a window-private slot at offset 436. It takes the drawable, the context and one arc, calls the shared zero-width arc setup once (instruction 37) with the record address and a request for the full-circle form, derives the row and column origins from the drawable origin and the pitch, and then steps the error terms, loading each target pixel, combining it with the and and xor words of the private record and storing it back. 2420 bytes at offset 0x597820. Compared with `cfb/cfbzerarc.c` (the general-rop, 16-bit build of the one-arc routine). It makes one call, and its 26 and plus 26 xor instructions mark the combine step, where the copy clone has none; which poly-arc routine calls it was not traced. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred: the prologue, the setup call, store width and combine pattern were read; the arc stepping logic was not read in full.

## Monochrome frame buffer (mfb)

- `mfbPixmapToRegion` in `crm.so`: turns a one-bit pixmap into a region by scanning each row for runs of set bits and emitting one box per run; it takes a pixmap, makes an empty one-box region first, grows the box list through the server allocator and reallocator in chunks (the chunk constants 500 and a quarter of that are seen near the growth tests), merges a row's runs into the previous row's boxes when their x edges are identical by increasing the earlier boxes' bottom edges, and fixes up the bounding box at the end, 4308 bytes at offset 0x434110; this is the same algorithm as `mfb/mfbclip.c`, whose shape matches the digest of 13 allocation calls, 6 reallocation calls, one free, 21 indirect calls and 83 half-word loads; the word-scanning inner loop was not read in full. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `mfbCopyPlane` in `crm.so`: extracts one bit plane of a source drawable into a one-bit destination by choosing a raster operation that makes the ordinary area copy do the work; it takes the source and destination drawables, the context, the source rectangle, the destination origin and the plane number, and when the source drawable's depth byte (offset 2) is not one it calls a per-screen copy-plane method found through a registered slot, reporting a fatal error if none is registered, else it returns nothing for a plane other than 1, and otherwise compares the low bits of the context's foreground and background pixels: a set foreground with a clear background copies as it is, equal bits replace the raster operation (offset 5 of the context) by the reduced operation from `mfbReduceRop`, and the remaining case swaps in the inverse operation from a 16-entry table, and in each case it calls the context's area-copy method (offset 12 of the operations table) and restores the saved operation afterwards, 564 bytes at offset 0x4533d0; read in full against `mfb/mfbbitblt.c`, whose three-way test on foreground and background bits it follows branch for branch, with the result of the copy returned unchanged and a 176-byte frame holding the spilled arguments. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `mfbCopyArea` in `crm.so`: copies a rectangle between drawables for one-bit pixels, clipping by the source's visible area and then calling the context's blit function; it takes source and destination drawables, the context, the source rectangle and the destination origin, calls `NotClippedByChildren` six times to find the source region, forms the clipped rectangle with half-word arithmetic (46 stores of halves, 12 pairs of upper-half shifts), and runs the blit through ten indirect calls, 2208 bytes at offset 0x453610; the same routine is the public `mfb/mfbbitblt.c` area copy, whose use of the child-clip test matches the digest (552 instructions, six child-clip calls, ten indirect calls); the exposure handling, which the public routine does through a helper, shows no direct call here and was not traced. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `mfbDoBitbltCopy` in `crm.so`: copies rectangles between one-bit drawables with a plain store, shifting source words to the destination bit alignment; its arguments are the two drawables, the raster operation, the box region and an array of source points, it takes row strides from the drawable at offset 28 (bytes, shifted down by two to words) and the bit data pointer from offset 32, and each box is copied left to right or right to left and top to bottom or bottom to top according to overlap, using 35 left and 35 right variable shifts, 4592 bytes at offset 0x453eb0; the public body is the copy build of `mfb/mfbblt.c` and has the same overlap-direction choice, and the digest here shows 1148 instructions with four indirect calls and 30 half-word loads for the box coordinates; the end-of-row mask handling was not read in detail. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `mfbReduceRop` in `crm.so`: folds a raster operation and foreground colour into a smaller one of the black, white, invert or noop forms, 392 bytes at offset 0x4550a0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mfb/mfbgc.c`: the body tests bit 0 of the pixel argument to pick one of two sixteen-entry jump tables indexed by the raster op, each case loading one of four reduced codes, and returns a stack word for an op of sixteen or more. Established.
- `mfbValidateGC` in `crm.so`: revalidates a context for one-bit drawables after a state change; it takes the context, the change mask and the drawable, recomputes the composite clip via `miComputeCompositeClip` (two call sites) when needed, reduces the raster operation to the three-way code kept in the private record, rotates the tile or stipple to the drawable origin using `mfbCopyRotatePixmap` (three calls) and chooses the span fillers, rectangle filler, line and arc handlers from the fill style, line style and raster operation, 3708 bytes at offset 0x455230; it matches the validation function of `mfb/mfbgc.c` in structure, with the digest agreeing on one release call, the constants 0x7fff/0x8000, and many loads of table addresses; only the call structure and counts were compared, not every table choice. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `mfbPutImage` in `crm.so`: writes client bit data into a one-bit drawable, taking the drawable, the context, the depth, the destination, the size, the left pad, the format and the data; it returns when the plane mask has no low bit or either dimension is zero, wraps the data in a scratch pixmap header of width plus left pad, clears bit 0x80 of the first word of the context's private record (the public graphics-expose flag) for the duration, calls the context's copy-plane method with plane 1 when the format is the bitmap format and its area-copy method otherwise, restores the bit and frees the header, 396 bytes at offset 0x4560b0; read in full against `mfb/mfbimage.c`, with the two method slots (offsets 12 and 16 of the operations table) in the order of the public branches and a 160-byte frame. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `mfbDoBitblt` in `crm.so`: chooses the one-bit rectangle-copy loop for a raster operation; its arguments are the source and destination drawables, the raster operation, the box region and the source offset, and it compares the operation in turn with the codes of copy, xor, inverted copy and or to jump to the matching body, falling back to `mfbDoBitbltGeneral` for any other value, 9112 bytes at offset 0x479890; the public `mfb/mfbbitblt.c` routine is only the switch (copy, xor, inverted copy and or go to separate bodies in `mfb/mfbblt.c`, anything else to the general body), but here the dispatcher is 9112 bytes because the specialised bodies sit inside it, which is why the digest shows a 464-byte frame and about 2278 instructions with word-copy loops using variable shifts (70 shifts left and 70 right) for the unaligned alignment cases; only the first 70 instructions (the dispatch and the setup of bytes-per-row from the drawable at offset 28 and the data pointer at offset 32) were read. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.

- `mfbLineSD` in `crm.so`: draws a dashed one-pixel polyline in a one-bit drawable. The dash state comes from the graphics context, not the private record: an unsigned halfword offset at offset 8, an unsigned halfword list length at offset 10, the list pointer at offset 12, the line style in the top two bits of the word at offset 16 and the cap style in the two bits below, the context's raster-op byte at offset 5 and the background pixel at offset 28; the foreground operation is the private raster-op byte. The body calls `mfbReduceRop` once on entry (at 0x52f040) to turn the context's raster op and the background pixel into one of the four operations; even dashes apply the foreground operation, odd dashes the reduced background operation in double dash and nothing in on-off mode. Plane-mask bit 0 must be set. The dash cursor advances by each segment's major-axis length and carries across the vertices, the list is used as given, and a negative screen index behaves as tie word zero (the earlier statement that it bails out was wrong). Clip, closing-point and cap rules are those of the solid one-bit polyline; pixels are word read-modify-writes (75 of them) and 3 double-precision divides reduce long dash distances. 12188 bytes (3047 slots) at offset 0x52c380, 1136-byte frame, 92 movz and 117 movn. Compared with mfb/mfbline.c. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution except for one fault: 250 random dashed polylines (random lists, offsets, both styles, all sixteen background operations, both background pixels, four foreground classes, plane masks 0 to 3) matched the reference bit for bit, apart from the end point. When the operation that applies to the end point is a no-op it inverts the bit instead of leaving it (found on 3 of the 250 runs and reproduced with a truth table over all operation pairs); no other routine of this set does that.
- `mfbLineSS` in `crm.so`: draws a solid one-pixel polyline in a one-bit drawable. Arguments are the drawable, the context, the coordinate mode (zero absolute, otherwise relative to the previous point), the point count and 16-bit point pairs. A window drawable (type byte 0) draws into the screen record's pixmap at offset 116, any other drawable is its own pixmap (pitch in bytes at pixmap offset 28, shifted right by two to give words, base at offset 32); the clip region is read from the context's private record as in the colour routines, and the screen tie word is the same per-octant word. Nothing is drawn unless bit 0 of the context's plane mask (offset 20) is set. The private raster-op byte at offset 0 gives the pixel operation: 0 clears the bit, 15 sets it, 10 inverts it, and any other value leaves it alone; pixels are packed most-significant bit first in 32-bit words and written with word read-modify-writes (152 word stores in the body). Segments follow the same pixel rules as the colour polyline drawer: each stops one pixel short of its end, the final point is painted unless the cap style is zero or the polyline closes on its first point after more than two points, and exact ties go by the tie word. With invert, a pixel visited by two segments is toggled twice. A segment that is not horizontal or vertical and lies wholly inside one clip box is handed to `mfbBresS` (called at 0x52ff48 with the raster op, the frame base, the pitch in words, the two step signs, the axis, the start point and the error terms in registers); horizontal, vertical and every clipped segment are drawn in line, so the earlier note that it calls the stepper for the edges it cannot finish in line had the condition backwards. 9468 bytes (2367 slots) at offset 0x52f320, one call site, 608-byte frame, 86 movz and 100 movn, no floating point. Compared with mfb/mfbline.c. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established by execution: 150 random polylines (coordinates from -6 to 70, one or four clip boxes, set, clear, invert and no-op, both cap styles, random tie words, random background bits) matched a separately written reference bit for bit; a negative screen index behaves as tie word zero.

It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `mfbDoBitbltCopyInverted` in `crm.so`: does the inverted copy transfer, complementing each source word as it is stored, 4272 bytes at offset 0x579100; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `mfbBresS` in `crm.so`: steps a solid Bresenham line in one-bit pixels, setting, clearing or inverting each bit; the arguments are the raster-operation code, the base address, the row pitch in words, the x and y directions, the major axis flag, the start point, the error terms and the length, it computes the starting word from y times the pitch plus x shifted right by five, takes the bit mask from a 32-entry table indexed by the low five bits of x (the table is at read-only offset 17080), and then branches on the operation (clear, set or invert) to a loop for the major axis, 2672 bytes at offset 0x57a2e0; it is the public helper in `mfb/mfbbres.c`, whose parameter list and three-operation split agree with the code seen (the entry plus the operation tests, 668 instructions, 13 multiplies, no calls); operations other than those three take the early-return path, and the loop bodies were not read in detail. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `mfbZeroArcSS..JM` in `crm.so`: draws thin (zero-width) arcs on a one-bit drawable; it takes the drawable, the context and an arc, tests that the context's raster operation byte is zero (the clear operation sets a mask of minus one), calls `miZeroArcSetup` once to produce the Bresenham-style arc parameters and error terms on the stack, and then plots points in four to eight symmetric positions with word read-modify-writes, using stored bit masks (104 adds, 40 ands, 34 arithmetic right shifts, 22 variable right shifts), 2992 bytes at offset 0x591c50; its public counterpart is the inner drawing function of `mfb/mfbzerarc.c`, which has the same setup call and the eight-way symmetry; the digest shows 748 instructions with a 400-byte frame; the plotting loops were not read word by word. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `mfbSetScanline` in `crm.so`: writes one source scanline into a one-bit drawable under a raster operation; it is a helper of the set-spans routine that takes the destination row, the source row, bit offsets and a width, builds start, whole-word and end masks (36 complements, 28 variable right shifts) and applies the operation through a branch-heavy body (65 plain branches), with four indirect calls whose targets were not resolved, 1664 bytes at offset 0x594930; its source counterpart is the scanline writer of `mfb/mfbsetsp.c`, and the digest gives 416 instructions with 23 loads of table addresses; the masked-word loops were not read through. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.

## Arc, span and region helpers

- `realAllocSpan` in `crm.so`: allocates a chunk of final-span records for the wide-arc code, chains the extra records into the free list and returns the first, 332 bytes at offset 0x543cd0; read in full against the public sample-server source `mi/miarc.c`: the body asks the allocator for 1540 bytes, returns zero when it gets none, links the block into the list of blocks through the word at +0x600, points the global free-record pointer into the block at +0xc, chains the twelve-byte records of the block by storing in each the address of a later one, ends the chain with zero at +0x5f4, zeroes its own first word and returns the block's address. Established.
- `hookEllipseY..GEB` in `crm.so`: returns the ellipse y at which one edge of a wide arc turns back, 152 bytes at offset 0x560720; the public sample-server source `mi/miarc.c` holds the same routine, and the body read in full matches it. The first argument is the scan row's y as a double, the second the record of two bounds (the lower at +0, the upper at +8), the third the arc's accelerator record (doubles at +24 and +40) and the fourth the left-edge flag. When the double at +40 is zero it returns the lower bound for a positive y with the flag clear or a negative y with it set, and the upper bound otherwise. Otherwise it divides the product of the row's y and the double at +24 by the one at +40 and returns the signed cube root of the quotient, through `cbrt` for the magnitude. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `tailSpan..KEB` in `crm.so`: makes the final span or spans of a wide-arc row near the ends of the ellipse, where the inner and outer ellipse edges bend back. It computes the crossing positions through the tail helper, picks which of four cases applies from four mask bits (left or right side, upper or lower), and enters the new spans in the final-span list through two calls of the final-span allocator, then fills in the arc spans. 892 bytes at offset 0x563570. Compared with the same-named function in `mi/miarc.c`; the disassembly shows the tail helper once, the final-span adder four times and the arc-span helper twice. Its calls: `tailX..HCB`, `newFinalSpan..OEB`, `arcSpan..IEB`. It writes span records only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `fillSpans..MEB` in `crm.so`: flushes the accumulated final spans of a wide arc to the drawable. It walks the per-row lists from the lowest to the highest row, skips empty spans, and packs the survivors into a point array and a width array allocated for the count, then calls the context's span method at offset 0 of its operation table with the sorted flag, and finally releases the span storage and resets the row range and the count. 480 bytes at offset 0x563c10. Compared with `fillSpans` in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly shows allocations, four frees and one indirect call. It writes only its temporary arrays. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `drawZeroArc..MDB` in `crm.so`: draws a zero-area arc, one whose width or height is zero, as a line of the given line width, in the arcs that the general code cannot treat as an ellipse. It converts the angles to degrees, runs the angle from start to end in steps that hit each extreme (axis crossings) of the sine and cosine, tracks the extreme x and y values reached, and records the face points at both ends of the arc (centre and the two sides offset by half the line width) into the caller's face records, then fills the swept box of the arc, widened by the line width, as one rectangle through the context's fill-rectangle method at offset 44 of its operation table. 1196 bytes at offset 0x5642f0. Derived from the same-named function in `mi/miarc.c` with the disassembly in view: three ceil calls and three floor calls, one call each of the local sine and cosine helpers, the full-turn constant 23040, and 34 double loads. Its calls: `miDsin..PCB`, `miDcos..OCB`. It writes span records only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `tailEllipseY..NDB` in `crm.so`: computes where the tail of a wide arc's edge begins and stores it in the first double of the accelerator record, 232 bytes at offset 0x565150; the public sample-server source `mi/miarc.c` holds the same routine, and the body read in full matches it. It first stores zero there and returns when the arc's width and height (doubles at +0 and +8 of the arc record) are equal. Otherwise it multiplies the double at +16 by the one at +0, returns when the product lies on the wrong side of the accelerator's bound at +8 for the wider axis, takes a cube root of the product scaled by the height and a data constant through `cbrt`, forms a quotient with the accelerator's double at +40, and when that is positive stores its square root times the height over a second constant. It touches no CRIME hardware. Established.
- `drawArc..AFB` in `crm.so`: draws one wide elliptical arc by combining the quadrant spans of the ellipse. It fetches the cached span table for the ellipse from the wide-ellipse routine, normalises the start and end angles (adding a full turn of 23040 when the end is below the start), and walks the quadrants the arc crosses, working out for each the angle range seen from the quadrant's axis (the constants 5760, 11520 and 17280 are the quarter, half and three-quarter angles). It remembers which quadrant and angle carry the start and end faces, and draws each partial quadrant through the quadrant drawer, with a mirrored variant for quadrants traversed backward. When the cache entry was allocated just for this call it is released afterwards. 3972 bytes at offset 0x567790. Compared against the same-named function in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly has one call to the wide-ellipse routine, one to the quadrant drawer, one free call, and many compare-and-branch pairs for the band bookkeeping (89 branch-not-equal). Its calls: `miComputeWideEllipse..ICB`, `drawQuadrant..BFB`. It writes span records only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `arcSpan..IEB` in `crm.so`: fills one row of a wide-arc ring for the quadrant drawer, splitting the row into a left and a right span from the outer and inner ellipse crossings. Rows inside the inner ellipse's range use the inner extent directly; otherwise it intersects with the left and right face lines (using the hook helper for the elliptical cases) and clips the span to the face, then stores the left and right spans through the span lists. 880 bytes at offset 0x56f230. Checked against the same-named function in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly shows one call of the hook helper, four direct calls of the final-span adder and six flag tests with `andi`. Its calls: `hookX..HEB`. It writes span records only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `newFinalSpan..OEB` in `crm.so`: adds one horizontal span to the per-row span lists of the arc being drawn, merging it with any existing span of that row that it touches. It finds the row's list (growing the row table through the find routine when the row is outside the current range), then walks the list, widening the first overlapping span and unlinking and counting down any further span that the widened one now covers, and when nothing overlaps it allocates a new span and links it in. 428 bytes at offset 0x56f5a0. Compared with `newFinalSpan` in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly shows one direct call of the row-table growth routine and one call of the span allocator. Its calls: `realAllocSpan`. It writes the span lists. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `tailX..HCB` in `crm.so`: computes, for a wide elliptical arc, a position along the ellipse at the point where the inner and outer edges of the ring meet near the end of a thin ellipse. It takes a parameter K, the ellipse definition, the bounds record and the accelerator record, and solves for the crossing with square roots, switching to a cubic solution with a cube root and an arccosine when the simple quadratic does not apply, and returns one double. 1860 bytes at offset 0x573c20. Compared with the tail routine in `mi/miarc.c`; the disassembly shows twelve square-root call sites, two cube-root calls, one arccosine and one cosine, with no stores except to its own frame. It writes nothing but its return value. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `drawQuadrant..BFB` in `crm.so`: produces the spans of one quadrant of a wide elliptical arc, the ring between the inner and outer ellipse limited by the arc's start and end angles. It walks the rows with square roots (thirteen square-root call sites), falls back to the closed form with a cube root (two call sites) and an arccosine where the inner ellipse is thin, takes the angle limits from the bound helper once, and writes finished spans through the span helpers. 6280 bytes at offset 0x574770. Compared against the quadrant drawer in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly shows the quadrant flag tests, a `floor` call, and the constant 5760 (a quarter turn in 64ths of a degree). Its calls: `computeBound..FEB`, `arcSpan0..JEB`, `tailSpan..KEB`. It writes span records only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `realFindSpan..NEB` in `crm.so`: grows the row table of the arc spans so that a given y is covered and returns the slot for that row. It enlarges by at least 100 rows (more when the new row is far outside), allocates the new table, moves the old entries to their place with `memmove`, zeroes the new slots at either end with two `bzero` calls, frees the old table and updates the stored row range and size; it returns null on allocation failure. 412 bytes at offset 0x577540. Compared with `realFindSpan` in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly shows one allocation, one move, one free, two zeroings and the constant 100. It writes the table. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `hookX..HEB` in `crm.so`: computes the x coordinate where a scan line at a given y meets the hook of a wide arc's inner edge, the curl at the end of a thin ellipse. For a circle it uses the Pythagorean form with one `sqrt`; for an ellipse it asks `hookEllipseY..GEB` for the y at the bottom of the tail and compares the scan line against it before choosing the closed form. It returns a double. 592 bytes at offset 0x577ae0. Compared with `hookX` in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly shows one call of the ellipse helper and one `sqrt`, and fused multiply-add instructions (4 of `madd.d`). It writes nothing but its return value. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `computeDashMap..HDB` in `crm.so`: precomputes the table that maps a dash index to a length along an ellipse arc. For each of 91 entries it converts the index to an angle, finds the point on the ellipse (half-width times cosine, half-height times sine), and stores the running sum of the straight distances between consecutive points (`hypot` once). The dash code then turns a length into an angle by looking in this table. 744 bytes at offset 0x584b90. Derived from the same-named function in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly shows one `hypot`, two floor calls, one sine and one cosine, with the constant 91 for the table size and no stores outside its map argument. It writes the map argument. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `QuickSortRects..PM` in `crm.so`: sorts an array of boxes by top edge and then by left edge, in place, for the region validate step. It is a quicksort that special-cases two boxes, swaps the middle box into the pivot slot, partitions the rest, then recurses on one side and loops on the other, swapping whole 8-byte boxes with halfword loads and stores. 640 bytes at offset 0x5850a0. Compared with the quicksort in `mi/miregion.c` and its disassembly, which has one recursive self-call and 40 halfword loads. It writes only the array it sorts. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `QuickSortSpans..BN` in `crm.so`: sorts a list of spans (a point and a width each) by y then by x, in place, with a quicksort that switches to insertion sort for short runs. It takes the point array, the width array and a count. It calls itself on the smaller partition; the partition and exchange of both arrays are inline, with 16-bit loads and stores of the point fields. 1092 bytes at offset 0x585440. Compared with the quicksort in `mi/miregion.c` and the disassembly, which shows one self-call, constants 1 and 4, and a doubly nested partition loop around the half-word loads and stores. It writes only the two arrays it was given. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `QuickSortSpansX..HO` in `crm.so`: sorts a list of spans by x within the same y, a sibling of the y-then-x sorter, for span groups whose rows are already ordered. It takes a point array, a width array and a count, and partitions both arrays together around a pivot, recursing through one self-call. 1068 bytes at offset 0x588120. Compared with the quicksort in `mi/mispans.c`, where the disassembly is 273 against 267 instructions for the y-sort neighbour (the y-field compare is absent) and uses the same constants 1 and 4. It writes only the two arrays it was given. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `computeBound..FEB` in `crm.so`: fills the bounds record the quadrant drawer uses for one arc: the vertical extent of the arc's ellipse from the sine of its two angles times the height, and, for the right and left end faces when present, where each face line crosses the inner and outer ellipse, using square roots and the tail helper and snapping the results to whole rows with floor. 1560 bytes at offset 0x588a30. Compared with the bound routine in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly shows ten square-root calls, two floor calls, two sine and two cosine calls, and 16 double-precision divides. It writes the bounds record passed in. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `arcSpan0..JEB` in `crm.so`: draws the first row (row 0, the centre row) of a wide-arc quadrant, then hands over to the general row routine. If row 0 is within the inner bounds and the left face line is valid and applies, it moves the left and right extents inward by the smaller of the face offset and the inner half width before calling `arcSpan..IEB` with row 0. 352 bytes at offset 0x58a7d0. Compared with `arcSpan0` in `mi/miarc.c`, where the disassembly has one call of the row routine, and six conditional double compares around the face tests. It writes through the span helpers only. It touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.

## Symbol database and conversion (SDB, xdr)

- `SDBsetupMemoryInterface` in `crm.so`: stores its four arguments, the allocation and release routines, into the symbol-database reader's memory-interface record, 36 bytes at offset 0x4283b8; read in full, no public source holds it: the body fetches the address of the interface record from the global table and stores its four arguments into the first four word slots of it. Established.
- `compareSTEName` in `crm.so`: compares the name strings at offset 12 of two symbol-table entries with a string compare, 56 bytes at offset 0x491a48; read in full, no public source holds it: the body adds 12 to both arguments and calls the string-compare routine through the global table, returning its result. Established.
- `mapTypeToXDRPROC` in `crm.so`: maps a symbol's data-type code to the conversion routine for it, asserting on a code it does not know, 524 bytes at offset 0x491a80; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `SDBinitializeSymbolTableEntries` in `crm.so`: clears the first three words and one byte of each of a given number of 44-byte symbol-table entries, 52 bytes at offset 0x491c8c; read in full, no public source holds it: the body walks the entry array at a stride of 44 bytes for as many entries as its second argument says and zeroes the first three words and the byte after them in each. Established.
- `xdr_SDBelementIndex` in `crm.so`: converts an element index by one call to the unsigned-long conversion, 52 bytes at offset 0x491cc0; read in full, no public source holds it: the body calls one routine of the XDR library through the global table with its arguments unchanged and returns that result. Established.
- `xdr_SDBfileOffset` in `crm.so`: converts a file offset by one call to the unsigned-long conversion, 52 bytes at offset 0x491cf4; read in full, no public source holds it: the body is the same single call through the same global-table slot as the element-index converter. Established.
- `xdr_typedData` in `crm.so`: converts a typed data value, choosing the conversion routine by the value's type and handling arrays and strings, 340 bytes at offset 0x491d28; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `xdr_SDBfileHeader` in `crm.so`: converts the header record of a symbol-database file: its version, byte blocks and counts of entries and offsets, 320 bytes at offset 0x491e7c; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `xdr_SDBdataType` in `crm.so`: converts a data-type code by one call to the enumeration conversion, 52 bytes at offset 0x491fbc; read in full, no public source holds it: the body is the same single call with its arguments unchanged, through a different global-table slot, the enumeration converter. Established.
- `xdr_SDBsymbolName` in `crm.so`: converts a symbol name as a string of at most 31 characters and forces the last byte to zero, 112 bytes at offset 0x491ff0; read in full, no public source holds it: the body returns 0 for a null or empty argument, calls the string converter through the global table with a limit of 31 and, on success, ends the string with a zero byte at +0x1f and returns 1. Established.
- `xdr_SDBsymbolTableEntry` in `crm.so`: converts one symbol-table entry as its name, data type, element index and file offset in turn, 200 bytes at offset 0x492060; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `xdr_SDBversionNumber` in `crm.so`: converts the database version number as two unsigned short integers, 104 bytes at offset 0x492128; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `SDBgetSymbolDataAddress` in `crm.so`: looks a symbol up through the shared finder and returns its data address through a method of the memory interface, zero when absent, 104 bytes at offset 0x492348; read in full, no public source holds it: the body calls the symbol search at 0x492248, returns zero when it finds nothing and otherwise calls the method at +0x10 of the table at +0x4 of the database handle. Established.
- `SDBgetSymbolDatatype` in `crm.so`: looks a symbol up through the shared finder and returns the entry's data-type word, zero when absent, 88 bytes at offset 0x4923b0; read in full, no public source holds it: the body calls the symbol search at 0x492248 and returns the word at +0x0 of the record it finds, or zero when none is found. Established.
- `SDBgetSymbolElementCount` in `crm.so`: looks a symbol up through the shared finder and returns the entry's element count, or the caller's default when absent, 92 bytes at offset 0x492408; read in full, no public source holds it: the body calls the symbol search at 0x492248 with a stack slot, and returns the word at +0x4 of the record it finds or the slot's value when none is found. Established.
- `SDBgetSymbolValueArray` in `crm.so`: fetches a symbol's array value through the shared value getter, passing the caller's destination, 56 bytes at offset 0x492464; read in full, no public source holds it: the body calls the shared lookup routine at 0x4922e8 with the caller's own fourth argument and returns its result. Established.
- `SDBgetSymbolValueScalar` in `crm.so`: fetches a symbol's scalar value through the shared value getter with a zero count, 56 bytes at offset 0x49249c; read in full, no public source holds it: the body calls the same shared lookup routine at 0x4922e8 but sets the fourth argument to zero first. Established.
- `SDBcloseReadDatabase` in `crm.so`: frees a read database's tables and closes its file, 184 bytes at offset 0x4924d4; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `SDBreadOpenDatabase` in `crm.so`: opens a symbol-database file, checks its header and reads the symbol table into memory, 424 bytes at offset 0x49258c; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `SDBprocessOneUserMethod` in `crm.so`: calls the user method a database names for one symbol, passing the symbol's value, 420 bytes at offset 0x49278c; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `sortLineTab..OC` in `crm.so`: sorts the display-ID line table with a recursive quicksort so lookups can use a search, 400 bytes at offset 0x56bb90; it belongs to the SGI display code and no public source holds it, and it touches no CRIME hardware. Inferred.
- `itemSearchFunction..IC` in `crm.so`: compares four key words of a line-table item against a query, treating minus one in the query as a wildcard, and returns the item's value word on a match, 108 bytes at offset 0x576d90; read in full, no public source holds it: the body compares up to four key words at +0x0 to +0xc of its first argument with those at +0x4 to +0x10 of the second, a key of minus one matching anything, and returns the word at +0x14 on a match or zero. Established.
